# M4.6 world save: carry-overs from B1's review

Short, and owned. Each entry below is something burst B1 (the counted streams
and the observed fields, branch `save-b1`) found and did NOT build, with the
later burst that owns it. Written 27 Sep 2026 after an independent review of
B1; the build plan itself is the main session's.

## 1. "Load last save" is not a seeded game (owner: B4b)

Only `strigoi_start_game` seeds a game. It sets the server's one-shot seed
(`d2server.SetNextGameSeed`) and reseeds the harness's uuid stream
(`d2app/harness_uuid.go`), then begins the game. The death screen's "load last
save" goes `App.ReloadGame` -> main menu -> `ToCreateGame` (`d2app/reload.go`)
and does neither, so the reloaded game, in a process that was seeded:

- takes a **wall-clock server seed**: `takeNextGameSeed` is one-shot and the
  first game consumed the override. The world RNG, the spawn tables, combat and
  the rising all seed from `gameClient.Seed`, so all four start where no script
  chose;
- **continues the uuid stream** from the dead game's byte count: its player id
  (the connection's uuid) and every entity id are the seed's later ids;
- therefore matches **no fresh launch**. It is deterministic only for the same
  script in the same process.

How to see it: the `uuid` provider reports `seeded_for_this_game` (false on a
reloaded game), `games` (= `seeded_game + 1` there) and `bytes_at_game_start`
(the dead game's count); every stream's `rng.seed_str` shows the wall-clock
seed. Pinned as it is today by `d2app` `TestUUIDStreamOnAFreshLaunchAndOnAReload`
(both halves) and `TestTownWalkDeterministic` (the fresh half).

**B4b** restores these on a load: the server seed from the save, and the uuid
stream with `d2rand.RestoreReader(seed, bytes)` AFTER the entities are rebuilt
(the plan's trap 6). When it does, the reload half of the unit test changes
with it, on purpose.

## 2. Seeds are int64; decode them as int64 (owner: B2, the world file)

A wall-clock seed (UnixNano, ~1.8e18) is past 2^53, where float64 stops
holding every integer. A reader that decodes JSON into float64 -- every
playtest's `mustNum` does -- reads a DIFFERENT seed, and a stream restored from
it is a different stream from its first value. So every stream report carries
`seed_str` (exact decimal) beside `seed`, and **the world file must decode
seeds as int64** (`json.Decoder.UseNumber` then `Int64`, or a typed struct
field), never through `interface{}`/float64. `d2rand`
`TestSeedStrSurvivesAFloat64Decoder` shows the loss and both exact routes.

## 3. A stream is one value (done in B1; for B2's Restore)

Spawns, Combat and Rising each hold one `*d2rand.Stream` (the `*rand.Rand` and
its counted source), and `Stream.Restore(seed, draws)` replaces both at once.
B2's system Restore calls that and nothing else (through
`d2rand.StreamState.RestoreInto` since the B2 review, after
`StreamState.Check` has refused a stream on another stream's seed or past
`MaxDraws`). Do not take `.Rand` out of a
stream and keep it: after a Restore it draws from the old, uncounted source.
The map engine is the exception, because its stream is handed to two factories:
`MapEngine.RestoreRand` goes through `useRand`, which re-hands it, and
`TestRestoreRandHandsTheStreamToTheFactories` holds it there.

## 4. A pack's members include its dead (for B2's snapshot)

`group.members` is never shortened: a death unwatches its member (combat's
`withdraw`) and the group keeps him, and the game reads the dead there --
`spent()` is "no member still watched" over the whole list, a dead man's
profile still names his pack, and a despawn takes every member off the map. `spawns.group_list[].member_ids` reports every
member as placed, dead ones included; the `notice` rows are only the watched.
The snapshot must carry `member_ids`, not the notice rows.

# B2a: the snapshots with no entity ids (branch `save-b2a`, 27 Sep 2026)

Six systems gained `Snapshot()` and `Restore()`, each in
`d2core/d2world/<system>_snapshot.go` with JSON tags, and each closed by its
`_snapshot_test.go` five ways (the shared helpers are in
`d2core/d2world/b2a_sweep_test.go`, all named `b2a*`):

1. **Round trip.** Snapshot, through JSON, into a FRESH system: the provider
   and the behaviour cannot tell the copy from the original.
2. **Run on.** Both advanced by the same steps stay equal (the provider, and
   whatever else the game reads: levels, bars, blows, bodies).
3. **The sweep.** Every leaf of the snapshot zeroed (or, if already zero, set),
   every list element and object key dropped, one at a time, restored and run
   on: each variant must be REFUSED by Restore or DIVERGE from the original.
   One that does neither fails the test ("losing it changed nothing
   observable"). The only exemptions are named with their reason, must stay
   silent, and must be reached. The first read is taken before the first step:
   what a load shows on its first frame is the save's to get right. **The
   sweep restores the untouched snapshot first**, the same way as every
   variant, and fails unless that matches the original: otherwise a sweep
   whose restore path differs from the original's (a binding in another
   order, a step missed) sees every variant diverge and passes having proved
   nothing (added 28 Sep; see "The finish" below).
4. **Classification.** Every field of every runtime struct a snapshot covers
   is listed as S (saved, with the JSON path it is saved at), D (derived on
   load), T (transient: empty whenever a save is allowed) or W (wiring). A
   field added later fails the test until someone classifies it.
5. **The probes** (`b2aMustDiverge`, added 28 Sep). The sweep counts a refused
   variant as its field seen, but for a field Restore CHECKS -- an id, a next
   id, a count tied to another count -- a refusal proves the check, not that
   the field matters: lost consistently, nothing would refuse. So each such
   field gets a valid-but-wrong value (a fresh model's ids and counts, a next
   id one past the last, another stage, another squad selected, a body
   renamed), which Restore must ACCEPT and the run on must DIVERGE from.

Every Restore is checked whole before it changes anything, so a refused
snapshot leaves the system as it was.

| System | The snapshot holds | Not saved, and why | What it leaves to the load order (B4) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Clock | `elapsed` | `frozen`, the moon override: the harness's, not the world's. Dials. | Restore right after `NewClock`, before `bindProgress` (trap 1). |
| Light | every source in creation order (id, kind, burn, lit, carried, x, y) and `next_id` -- which after his torch has burnt out or been put away and been lit again is NOT one past the last source, so it cannot be derived | the carried burn rate (talents, re-applied); the player's x, y (`SetPlayer` every frame); **each source's radius, a dial by kind (D2, review fixes)**. A carried source's own x, y are saved but read by nothing (it shines from the player) -- the sweep's one exemption. | Trap 4: a torch's minutes are in the carried source AND, via `saveKit`, in the embedded kit. **D1 (review fixes): the light model is the truth** -- restore this snapshot whole, carried source included, zero the kit torch's minutes, and never re-light through L. (This row first said the opposite; see "Review fixes".) |
| Squads + Meters | `next_id`, `selected`, every squad in ordinal order: id, owner, ordinal, morale, members (entity, health, max, order), meters (food, water, fatigue, activity, damage) | each meters' conditioning (talents); the body the meters spend | s:1's member is written as `"player"` with health and max 0 (his health is his body's); `BindPlayer` binds s:1 before or after the restore, both work. s:1 is restored IN PLACE, so `game.meters` and his conditioning survive. Restore is refused unless the owner is fresh (s:1 only): a deployed model is a map entity, and must be rebuilt with its saved id (B2b's seam) before the squads are restored. **D3 (review fixes): deployed squads are B4b's**; B4a restores s:1 alone and refuses a snapshot with a deployed squad. |
| Corpses | every body in fall order (id, row, class, state, x, y, was, downed_at) and `risen_as` (every member a body ever walked as), `walker`, `last` | the wiring (row classifier, names, clock, callback) | Restore NEVER calls `changed` (trap 5): the spawn tables' `openBodies` comes from the file. Restore is refused into a registry that holds bodies, so skip `placeTheDead` on a load. |
| Rising | `pressure`, `last_band`, `last_stage` (its name), `rolls`, `risen`, `stood_again`, `wandered`, the stream | the dials (P, hasty weight, edge floor) and the wiring | after the corpses. `wandered` names the next wanderer's body (`wanderer:<n+1>`); lost, the next one is named after a body already lying there. |
| Combat | `next_id`, the stream, every counter, how the last fight ended (reason and the five counts), `last_action_verb`, the last round row and pace row, the last round's blows and the HUD's blow log | the encounter: **`Snapshot` returns `ErrCombatFighting` during a fight**. It also refuses while experience or paced world minutes are waiting for the game screen to take, or the pace window is open -- rather than drop them. Dials and wiring. | with the other systems' counters. Restore is refused during a fight, and checks `next_id == started + 1` and `ended == started`. |

**The streams.** Rising and Combat save their stream as `{seed, draws}` and
restore it with `d2rand.Stream.Restore` and nothing else (section 3). The seed
is written as a JSON **string** (`json:"seed,string"`), so it is exact through
a float64 reader as well as a typed one; d2rand's
`TestStreamStateSeedSurvivesJSON` holds that at a seed past 2^53. The type was
private (`b2aStream`) so B2b's parallel work could not collide with it; **the
review fixes made B2a's and B2b's two copies one type, `d2rand.StreamState`**,
and every Restore now checks the stream's seed against the game seed.

**Never dials** (trap 7): no snapshot carries a dial, a radius derived from
one, or a harness override. (Two did, and the review fixes took them out: the
light's per-source radius and the rising's pressure with the dial's constant
in it -- D2.)

## C6: one seed per stream (done in B2a)

`d2rand.Derive(seed, name)` gives the spawn tables, combat and the rising
each its own seed; the world stream keeps the game seed, so the map and the
digest's world part do not move. It works in math/rand's residue space
(`rngSource.Seed` reduces every seed modulo 2^31-1, and two seeds with one
residue are ONE stream), moving the game's effective seed by a fixed
per-name offset (FNV-1a) around the ring of non-zero residues. So for any game
seed the four streams are four different streams -- guaranteed, not merely
likely -- and every derived seed is below 2^31. At seed 1462: spawns
443254809, combat 179919329, rising 1368529044 (pinned by `TestDeriveIsFixed`).

This changes every seeded fight and night once (the rising already had its own
stream, at seed+4707, and now has a derived one). Three scripts were keyed to
an outcome of the old dice; each was re-measured, and each fix also passes on
the old seeding:

- **TestSpawns act 6c** checked the streams' seeds against the old seeding. It
  now checks each against `Derive`, exactly and as `seed_str`, and that all
  four differ. Seen red with combat put back on the game seed.
- **TestCombatResolver act 4** needs the dog dead ("the corpse has no bar").
  Acts 2 and 3 used to kill it on the old dice; on the new ones it stands with
  12 of 61. The script now finishes it with forced crits and fails if eight do
  not -- a corpse is the act's premise, not its finding.
- **TestTacticalFight act 3's control** clicked while his riposte swing was in
  flight (on the new dice the zombie grazes him and he ripostes), and a click
  during a swing is swallowed by the `IsCasting` gate with no notice. The
  control now waits the swing out, as a person would.

**The unaided man's numbers moved** (history 122's winnability measurements
were taken on the old streams). `TestTheUnaidedChain` at seed 1462: old
seeding, he dies at 21:59 with two aware at the peak; new seeding, at 22:29
with one. One encounter either way.

## The finish (28 Sep 2026): the WIP reviewed, rebased, closed

The first B2a agent was cut off with its work uncommitted; it was recovered
as a WIP commit and finished by a second agent that treated it as unreviewed.

**Rebased** onto master `0997f9ae` (the polish burst, the World Editor,
BUG-27) with no conflicts; both builds and the d2world/d2rand tests green.

**Audited system by system against the plan's list.** Every field of Clock,
Light (and `Source`), Squads (`squad`, `model`), Meters, Corpses (`Corpse`),
Rising and Combat (`action`, `RoundRow`, `PaceRow`, `BlowLine`) is classified
by a test that reflects over the struct, and every S path resolves in the
snapshot. No field of the plan's list was missing. Dials are written only by
`HarnessSet` in all six systems (read at the source), so none is state.

**What the WIP got wrong, and what changed:**

- **The sweep had no baseline.** It never restored the untouched snapshot
  through its own `try`, so a `try` that restored differently from the
  original would have made every variant "diverge" and the sweep pass. The
  squads sweep in particular restores with `BindPlayer` AFTER `Restore`, a
  path whose run on no other test compared. The baseline passes on all six
  (so the WIP's sweeps were valid, but unguarded); it is now required.
- **Two fixtures could not see a derived next id.** The light fixture's
  comment said its removed source made `next_id` "not simply one past the
  last", but it removed source 3 of 5, so `next_id` was 6 = last + 1; the
  squads fixture likewise ended with `next_id` 5 = last + 1. A Restore that
  derived `next_id` from the last id passed every WIP test in both (negative
  controls R2b and R3b below, green). Both fixtures now remove their NEWEST
  (a doused torch, a recalled squad), as a game does, so `next_id` is two
  past the last, and the derived restore goes red (R2, R3).
- **Checked fields were only ever refused.** Combat's `next_id`, `started`
  and `ended`; light's `next_id`, ids and kinds; squads' `next_id`,
  `selected`, owners, a model's max and the activity; rising's `last_stage`;
  a body's id, class and state -- the sweep saw each only as a refusal. The
  probes (item 5) now give each a valid wrong value that must restore and
  diverge; all do. A Restore that parses `last_stage` and ignores it passed
  the sweep and the round trip, and is caught by the probe (R4).
- **Combat's Restore would restore over a dirty model.** Snapshot refused
  while experience, paced minutes or the pace window were outstanding, but
  Restore did not, so a restore into a model holding them would leave a
  resumed game owing what no saved moment owed. Restore now refuses the same
  way (every other B2a Restore already refused a used target: corpses a
  non-empty registry, squads an owner with a deployed squad). R7 below.
- Smaller: combat's stream got its own past-2^53 JSON test (rising's covered
  the shared type only); the two pace-window refusals with no test
  (`decisionSecondsRound`, `paceHealthOpen`) got one each; the combat copy
  closed the clock it registered; the helpers moved out of the clock's test
  file into `b2a_sweep_test.go`; the contract comment in `clock_snapshot.go`
  now names its six systems rather than "every *_snapshot.go" (B2b's follow
  their own).

**Negative controls** (unit: `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b2a\nc-unit-r2.txt`,
script `nc_unit_r2.py`; each broken in place, run, restored, re-run green):

| # | Broken | Expected | Seen |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | the squads sweep restores with health+1 | red | red: "the baseline ... does not match" |
| R1b | the same, baseline check disabled | green (the hole) | green |
| R2 | light Restore derives `next_id` from the last source | red | red (round trip, baseline) |
| R2b | the same on the WIP's fixture, without the probes that need room | green (the hole) | green |
| R3 | squads Restore derives `next_id` from the last squad | red | red (round trip, baseline) |
| R3b | the same on the WIP's fixture, without its probe | green (the hole) | green |
| R4 | rising Restore parses `last_stage` and ignores it | red | red: both probes, and the new-game test |
| R5 | the stream seed written as a JSON number | red | red: combat's seed test |
| R6, R6b | combat Snapshot forgets `decisionSecondsRound` / `paceHealthOpen` | red | red |
| R7 | combat Restore's new refusal of a dirty model removed | red | red |

The WIP's own nineteen unit controls (`nc-unit.txt`, 27 Sep) stand for the
code they break, which the finish did not change; their script's patterns
predate the reshaped test files, so re-running it needs them updated.

**Register controls** (`wt-b2a\nc-reach-r2.txt`, through the gate itself,
`go run ./tools/reachcheck -only ...`): N1, the three `Derive` calls in
`CreateGame` put back on the old seeding -- red, "expected live, measured
dead ... WENT DEAD"; N2, a `worldClock.Snapshot()` call added to
`CreateGame` -- red, "expected dead, measured live ... Now wired". Both green
again restored.

**Playtests on the rebased branch:** the whole suite once at `-parallel 4`,
72 of 72 top-level scripts green in 7 min 11 s
(`wt-b2a\r2-playtest-suite-1.txt`). C6's effects re-read there: act 6c logs
the derived seeds (spawns 443254809, combat 179919329, rising 1368529044);
the unaided man dies at 22:29 with one aware at the peak; act 3's control
waits 5 frames for his riposte to land; `TestCombatResolver` finishes the
dog with forced crits after act 3, and act 4 reads its corpse ("the dead dog
has no overhead bar"). Act 6c's control (combat back on the game
seed) re-run on the rebased branch: red, "combat.rng must be seeded 179919329
... reports 1462" (`wt-b2a\r2-playtest-nc-combat-seed.txt`).
`TestTacticalFight` three times over: green each time, the control waiting 5
frames each time (`wt-b2a\r2-playtest-tactical-x3.txt`).

**The WIP's last full suite had three failures that were not C6.**
`playtest-suite-2.txt` (27 Sep) failed `TestTalk` act 1 (a hover label read
""), `TestASlainManRises` act 5 (a hover read "") and `TestTacticalFight` act 3
(a click 3 tiles off was ACCEPTED -- the refusal half, before any enemy turn,
at the same click point and tick as a passing run). All three are
cursor-position reads; all three passed when re-run alone (`playtest-rerun-3.txt`)
and in the finish's full suite. That is BUG-15's class (a scripted pointer on
the real input; one real mouse movement at the wrong minute), recorded here as
three more occurrences rather than as C6.

**Reachability:** B2a's rows sat in `tools/reachcheck/register_b2a.go`,
appended at init so the two parallel bursts never edited one list (B2b's first
draft did the same, and its audit folded its rows into `register.go`; the B2a
text here said "like B2b's file", which was already untrue when B2b merged).
**The review fixes folded B2a's rows into `register.go` too (C4)**:
`d2rand.Derive` is WIRE (CreateGame must keep seeding three streams through
it), and the Snapshot/Restore/Validate verbs are DEFER, expected dead: Snapshot
to M4.6 B3 (`Game.SaveWorld`), Restore and Validate to M4.6 B4a -- Squads'
to B4b (D3).

**For the B2b merge.** No top-level name collides (checked against B2b's
worktree on 28 Sep). Two things to make one: B2a's `playerEntity` and B2b's
`PlayerRef` are both `"player"`, and the two bursts' private stream types.
(This paragraph first said B2b's spawns wrote `rng_seed` and draws flat; B2b's
own audit had already nested them as `rng {seed, draws}` before it merged.)
**Both made one by the review fixes:** `d2saveref.Player` (d2world's
`PlayerRef` is it) and `d2rand.StreamState`. Both bursts append to this notes
file.

## What B2a did not do

- No file, no save verb, no load: B3 and B4. Nothing in the game calls a
  Snapshot or a Restore yet; their register rows are defer/dead, and flip to
  wire when B3 and B4a call them.
- No entity ids, spawns, notice, pursuit, bodies or motion: B2b.
- No change to the load order itself (trap 4's torch, trap 5's
  `openBodies`, trap 1's clock-first): each Restore's doc comment names its
  part, and B4a owns doing it. (Trap 4 as first written -- re-light through L
  -- was wrong; D1 in "Review fixes" replaces it.)

# B2b: the snapshots that carry entity ids (branch `save-b2b`, 28 Sep 2026)

Three world systems that point at entities -- Spawns, Notice and Pursuit --
gained `Snapshot(r)` and `Restore(snap, r)` through a `Resolver`. The game
spawner gained its own small pair, the entity factory gained the next-id seam,
and creatures and NPCs gained a motion snapshot. Nothing in the game calls
any of it yet: B3 writes the snapshots, B4b restores them.

The work was started on 27 Sep and cut off before review or gate. It was then
inherited, audited field by field against build plan section 5, fixed, and
closed on 28 Sep. "What the audit changed" below lists what the first draft
got wrong.

## The Resolver (`d2core/d2world/resolver.go`)

```go
type Resolver interface {
	Watcher(id string) (Watcher, bool) // the live entity with this id
	Quarry(ref string) (Quarry, bool)  // PlayerRef is the player; else an entity id
}
const PlayerRef = "player"
```

- **No remap table.** Every entity comes back with its saved id (the seam
  below), so a Resolver is a lookup, not a translation. The player is the one
  exception: his id is his connection's uuid and is new every launch. So every
  record that points at him is written as the word `player`.
- **Strict at load.** An id that does not resolve is `ErrUnresolvedRef`, never
  a silent drop. So is an id that resolves to something answering to another
  id. A chase's hunter must also be a `Hunter`, which is the type assertion
  `startChasesForTheAware` makes on the live path.
- **Strict at save.** `Snapshot(r)` takes a Resolver over the LIVE world:
  - it names the player through it. If there is a watch or a chase and no
    player, the snapshot is refused;
  - every ref it writes must resolve now, so a file no load could read is
    refused before it is written.
- **All-or-nothing.** Every Restore checks the whole snapshot before it
  changes anything, so a refused one leaves the system exactly as it was.
- The shared helpers sit beside the interface (`b2bResolveWatcher`,
  `b2bResolveQuarry`, `b2bLivePlayerID`, `b2bSavedRefsResolve`,
  `b2bCheckNumbers`). All are B2b-private. There is no shared `snapshot.go`.
- **The game's Resolver is B4b's to write.** It must be built over
  `MapEngine.Entities()`, and it must return the same adapter the live path
  builds: a `chaser` wrapping the entity, with `Quarry(PlayerRef)` giving
  `prey{localPlayer}`. The save and the load must use the same Resolver type.

## Spawns (`SpawnsSnapshot`, `spawns_snapshot.go`)

| Field | Treatment |
|---|---|
| every group: `id`, `row`, `code`, `members`, `morale`, `born_at`, `band`, `stage` (by name), `weight`, `spawned`, `born_where` | **S** |
| `next_id`, `since_check_minutes`, `open_bodies` | **S** |
| the eight counters: `checks`, `rolls`, `spawned`, `failures`, `dropped`, `despawned`, `cleared`, `released` | **S** |
| `rng {seed, draws}`: the tables' `d2rand.Stream` | **S**. The seed is a JSON string, exact past 2^53. It has the same shape as B2a's Combat and Rising streams |
| `sheltered` | **T**. Snapshot is refused while it is set, because a sleep is running |
| the dials | **D** |
| clock, notice, illumination, spawner, target, chases, the `layDead` hook | wiring |

**Members** are saved as `{id, x, y, gone}`, and the dead are included (B1's
`member_ids`).

- **A member on the map** must resolve at load, to an entity standing EXACTLY
  at the saved x,y. The position is an identity check, not a second copy of
  his motion. The wrong entity is refused, and so is one whose motion was not
  restored first.
- **A gone member** was in his group and not on the map at save: a slain man
  whose body stood up, so `takeOffTheMap` took his remains.
  - Gone is decided at save by the live Resolver, then **checked against the
    notice model**. A death unwatches a man (`Combat.withdraw`) before
    anything takes him off the map. So a member the Resolver cannot find
    while notice still watches him is a living wolf the Resolver lost, not a
    dead man, and the save is refused.
  - At load a gone member must NOT resolve. He comes back as a stand-in
    (`b2bGoneMember`) that answers with his id and his last place, which is
    all the game ever asks of him.
  - His x,y is carried and nothing reads it. The sweep pins that as
    `carried`, so it goes red the day something reads it.
- **Refused at load:**
  - a group id that is not `g:<n>` below `next_id`;
  - a row this build's tables do not have;
  - a band outside -1..2, or an unknown stage name;
  - a non-finite number, or a negative count;
  - an id saved twice;
  - a group with no members.

## Notice (`NoticeSnapshot`, `notice_snapshot.go`)

| Field | Treatment |
|---|---|
| every watch: `watcher` (entity id), `target` (`player` or an entity id), `sees`, `noticed`, `distance`, `light_at_target`, `reach`, `since_check_minutes`, `since_seen_minutes`, `checks`, `notices` | **S** |
| the totals `checks`, `notices` | **S** |
| `hidden` | **T**. Refused while set |
| the dials, the radius above all | **D**. Never saved (trap 7). A test pins that a restore keeps the radius the model was built with |
| sight, illumination | wiring |

`reach` is saved because it is the last sight test's result, not the dial.
Restore evaluates nothing: `Watch()` would run a sight test and move every
counter.

## Pursuit (`PursuitSnapshot`, `pursuit_snapshot.go`)

| Field | Treatment |
|---|---|
| every chase: `hunter` (entity id), `quarry` (`player` or an entity id), `solved_at_x/y`, `solved_distance`, `since_solve_minutes`, `reachable`, `solves`, `arrived` | **S** |
| the total `solves` | **S** |
| the dials; the router | **D**; wiring |

The route is not here: the hunter is walking it, so it lives in the entity's
Motion. Restore solves nothing and hands no hunter a path.

## gameSpawner (`SpawnerSnapshot{arrival}`, `d2game/d2gamescreen/game.go`)

- **S** `arrival`. It sets the next pack's bearing and range (`packSpots`).
  Lost, the next pack comes from the night's first bearing. The scene provider
  reports it as `spawner_arrival`.
- The engine, the asset manager, the bestiary and the adopt/release callbacks
  are wiring.
- A negative count is refused.

## The next-id seam (`d2mapentity/entity_id.go`, `d2mapengine/entity_id.go`)

- **`MapEngine.SetNextEntityID(id)` is the one a load calls.** It refuses an
  id already on the map, because `AddEntity` keys on the id and would
  silently replace the first entity. Then it passes the id to the factory's.
- **`MapEntityFactory.SetNextEntityID(id)`** refuses, with `ErrEntityID` and
  not a panic:
  - `""` and `"player"`;
  - an id while another is still waiting;
  - an id the seam has already used.
- **Every constructor that makes a map entity consumes the waiting id, as its
  first statement** (plan: "every constructor that calls newMapEntity").
  - `NewNPC` and `NewCreature` WEAR it. They are the kinds a save rebuilds.
  - `NewPlayer`, `NewMissile`, `NewItem` and `NewCastOverlay` SPEND it
    without wearing it. The player's id is his connection's, and the other
    three are not saved. Wearing it would be worse than losing it: the engine
    map and the notice model would then hold a monster's saved name on a
    missile.
  - `NewObject` does not call `newMapEntity` and is never saved, so it took
    no part. **The review fixes changed that** (B6): it mints its id with an
    inline `uuid.New`, so an object built while an id waited left it for the
    next monster; it now SPENDS a waiting id like the other unsaved kinds.
  - The id is consumed even when the construction then fails. A consumed id
    counts as used, worn or not.
  - `TestEveryEntityConstructorTakesTheWaitingID` reads the factory's source.
    Every `MapEntityFactory` method that calls `newMapEntity`,
    `newMapEntityWithID`, `NewAnimatedEntity` or `newCreature` must take the
    id in its first statement, so a constructor added later cannot slip past
    the seam. Its positive control is the six names. **Since the review fixes
    it reads TRANSITIVELY** (any chain of the package's own functions that
    reaches `newMapEntity`, `newMapEntityWithID` or an inline `uuid.New`), and
    its positive control is seven names, `NewObject` among them.
- A set id draws nothing from the uuid stream. **But the construction still
  draws from the WORLD stream** (NewCreature a behaviour seed when it stands in
  for a monstat, NewNPC a behaviour seed and one roll per equipment slot:
  `factory.go`), so the load restores the uuid count AND the world RNG after the
  entities (trap 6; corrected at the review, which found the load list here
  restoring neither in the right place). A rebuilt NPC's equipment is
  therefore RE-ROLLED at the load's position of the world stream: a deployed
  model may wear other gear than when saved (the composite's look only).
- `PendingEntityID()` reports what is still waiting.

## Motion (`d2mapentity/motion.go`): `Creature`/`NPC` `.MotionSnapshot()` and `.RestoreMotion(Motion)`

- **Carried:**
  - `pos`, `target`, `velocity`, `path` and `speed`, in raw sub-tiles, so the
    round trip is bit-exact;
  - `dir` (facing), `mode` (the animation mode), `action` (a held mode) and
    `corpse`.
- **Not carried:**
  - the arrival callback: a chase's walk is handed over with none
    (`chaser.Follow` passes nil). **A paced fight's walk is not** (corrected at
    the review): `Game.StepToward` passes `borrowPace`'s return. It needs no
    carrying: saves are refused in a fight, and every frame outside one
    `tacticalAdvance` runs `returnAllPace`, which empties the table the
    callback reads, so the callback is a no-op whenever a save is allowed
    (`TestNoPaceIsBorrowedOutsideAFight`);
  - the animation frame: a held action restarts from its first frame. A
    creature saved while its DEATH is held plays the death again after a load
    and then lies as a corpse; the held action's callback is not carried
    (the game starts every action with a nil one). **Corrected by the B4b
    review fixes (29 Sep 2026; BUG-75, BUG-76): "and then lies as a corpse, as
    it would have" was untrue.** The death ended later than it would have,
    and a monster slain while it walked walked on through its death, so its
    corpse came to rest further along its route. A death now ends the walk
    where it begins, and no held action is saved or loaded: the save refuses
    while one plays, the load refuses a file that holds one. See "B4b review
    fixes" at the end of this file. **Superseded by BUG-87 (29 Sep 2026,
    branch `save-held`): a held action's frame and the time into it ARE
    carried (`action_at`), `RestoreMotion` resumes the action there, and
    neither refusal remains -- see "BUG-87: the held action is saved" at the end of this file;
  - `drawLayer` and `highlight`, which are render state;
  - an NPC's patrol and its per-NPC RNG: only villagers patrol, and the RNG
    has no effect (plan section 1);
  - the directioner, which is wiring.
- **Velocity** is recomputed by the first step. It is seen only at the load,
  by the renderer's debug overlay, and a test pins that.
- **RestoreMotion** sets the fields directly, never through `setTarget`, which
  would re-derive an NPC's mode. A Motion no entity could have had is refused
  before anything changes.
- **Rule 4 ("a walk you were in the middle of does not continue") is the
  player's alone, and it is a B4 load decision.** There is no MotionSnapshot on
  `*Player`, and B2b implements nothing for it. B4 does not restore his walk:
  he stands at his saved x,y. Monsters, the risen and deployed squads keep
  their motion.

## What B4b must do on load

**Superseded by the one authoritative B4 load order** in "Review fixes (28 Sep
2026)" below. The list that stood here restored the villagers' ids AFTER the
entities (the plan puts them before), and left the world RNG out of the order
(rebuilding an entity draws from it, so it goes after them). Keep one list.

## Tests (unit, fake watchers, no MPQs)

**The fake world** (`resolver_test.go`) is `advanceWorld` in miniature: clock,
tables, notice and pursuit, over fake entities that walk the routes they are
handed.

- It is run from dawn into dusk until every saved field has something to say:
  - two kinds of group;
  - a failure and a dropped member;
  - a gone member;
  - a despawn with its chases released, and a daybreak clear;
  - watches that see, remember and are unaware;
  - chases both arrived and still walking.
- A resume is a RELAUNCH: another seed, another player id, the entities
  rebuilt with their saved ids and motion, and the snapshots read back through
  JSON.

**The closing tests the plan names:**

- **Round trip.** `TestEntitySnapshotsRoundTripThroughARelaunch`: the resumed
  world shows exactly what the saved one did and re-saves the same JSON.
  `TestCreatureMotionRoundTripsAndAdvancesInStep`,
  `TestNPCMotionRoundTripsAndStepsInStep` and `TestGameSpawnerSnapshot` do the
  same for motion and the spawner.
- **Advance in step.** `TestEntitySnapshotsAdvanceInStep` runs 400 ticks, and
  the tables, sight tests, solves and stream must all move within them.
- **Zero a field, and it diverges or is refused.**
  - `Test{Spawns,Notice,Pursuit}SnapshotEveryFieldIsSeen` sweep every field,
    including identity swaps (another pack's man, another entity, the old
    player id).
  - `TestCreatureMotionEveryFieldIsSeen` and `TestNPCMotionCarriesItsPose`
    cover motion.
  - `Test{Spawns,Notice,Pursuit}Since*IsBehaviour` show that the hidden
    clocks move WHEN things happen, not only a reported number.
- **The id seam:**
  - `TestNextEntityIDIsUsedOnceThenGenerationResumes`;
  - `TestNextEntityIDRefusesDuplicates`;
  - `TestAFailedConstructionConsumesTheID`;
  - `TestASetIDDrawsNoUUID`;
  - `TestTheKindsASaveDoesNotRebuildSpendTheID`;
  - `TestEveryEntityConstructorTakesTheWaitingID`;
  - `TestEngineRefusesAnIDAlreadyOnTheMap`.
- **Resolver strictness:**
  - `TestResolverIsStrict` and `TestResolverThatLiesIsCaught`;
  - `TestNoticeSnapshotNeedsThePlayerNamed`;
  - `TestPursuitRestoreRefusesAHunterThatCannotWalk`;
  - `TestSpawnsSnapshotRefusesAWatchedMemberTheResolverLost`.

**Negative controls: 46, each seen red.** Each one is a copy of the package
with one break applied, under `_nc/` (`nc.py` / `nc-run.sh` in
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b2b\`). Their summaries are `r2-nc-*-summary.txt`
and the per-control logs are in `nc\r2-*.txt`.

## Register

Fourteen rows sat in `tools/reachcheck/register.go`'s DEFER block, all
expected DEAD (seventeen since the review fixes added the three Validate
twins, below):

- for M4.6 B3: the three Snapshots, the two MotionSnapshots and
  `gameSpawner.Snapshot`;
- for M4.6 B4b: the three Restores, the two RestoreMotions,
  `gameSpawner.Restore`, `MapEngine.SetNextEntityID` and
  `MapEntityFactory.PendingEntityID`.

The day B3 or B4b calls one, its row goes live and the gate is red until the
row moves to `wire`.

## What the audit changed (the first draft's gaps)

1. **The seam covered only NewNPC and NewCreature.** A waiting id could sit
   through any number of other constructions and be taken by whatever monster
   came next. Now every constructor that makes a map entity consumes it, and
   the source test holds that.
2. **"Gone" was the Resolver's word alone.** A Resolver that lost a living
   member would have saved him as dead. At load he would have come back a
   stand-in while his entity walked beside the pack, and a daybreak despawn
   would have left that entity on the map. It is now cross-checked against
   the notice model, and refused.
3. **The spawns stream was written flat** (`rng_seed`, `rng_draws`) while B2a
   writes `rng {seed, draws}`. It now has B2a's shape, so the world file
   carries every stream one way.
4. **The register rows were in a file of their own**, appended at init. The
   register lives in one place (`RegisterMarkdown`'s own comment), so they
   were folded into `register.go`.
5. **An NPC's corpse and held action had no test at all.** The pose half is
   now tested; the composite half needs MPQs (below).

## Found, and left for later

- **The NPC composite pose is not unit-tested.** A composite needs the MPQs.
  Deployed squad models are NPCs (`squadDeployer`), so B4b's playtest must
  cover them.
- **A held action restarts from its first frame.** Saves are refused during a
  fight, so only a swing's tail after a fight can be cut short. **Fixed by
  BUG-87 (29 Sep 2026): the frame is carried.**
- **Two private stream types with one JSON shape** (`b2aStream`,
  `b2bStream`). Folded into `d2rand.StreamState` by the review fixes.
- **"B2a added no register rows for its Snapshot and Restore verbs"** -- this
  line was wrong: B2a added twelve, in `register_b2a.go`, appended at init.
  The review fixes folded them into `register.go` beside B2b's (C4).

# Review fixes (28 Sep 2026, branch `save-b2-fixes`)

Two independent reviews read B2a and B2b after both merged (master
`ddc3ea7c`). They found one design error that would have made a resumed night
diverge -- trap 4's re-light -- and a set of guards and tests that were weaker
than they looked. Every fix below has a test that was seen RED without it: the
unit controls are N1-N31 (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-savefix\nc-unit.txt`,
script `nc_unit.py` beside it), the playtest controls P1-P2
(`wt-savefix\playtest-nc-*.txt`). Five decisions were taken by the main
session and implemented here; Josh can overturn any of them.

## The decisions (D1-D5)

**D1 -- trap 4: THE LIGHT MODEL IS THE TRUTH ON LOAD.** The plan said: "a lit
torch's minutes live in the light model and are zero in the kit, so re-light
via the L path and never restore both". Re-lighting would have made a resumed
night diverge three ways: the torch would get a NEW id and move `next_id`; L
is a toggle, so a DOUSED torch (a douse keeps the source and its minutes)
would come back lit; and `ui.torch_verbs` would count a verb the saved game
never made. And the kit is not zero: `saveKit` writes the carried source's
burn into the kit's torch for the sidecar whether it is lit or doused, so the
file holds the minutes twice. So:
- `Light.Restore` restores the snapshot WHOLE, carried source included: id,
  lit, burn, `next_id` exactly (it always did; its doc comment and this file's
  B2a table prescribed the re-light, and now say this).
- B4 zeroes the off-hand torch's `BurnLeft` in the kit it binds whenever the
  light block holds a carried source -- the state the L key leaves the kit in --
  and never presses L. Documented here and in `Light.Restore`; B4a builds it.
- Tests: `TestLightRestoresTheCarriedTorchExactly` (lit and doused: id,
  `next_id`, lit, burn equal, and advancing both copies keeps them equal) and
  d2player's `TestALoadedTorchIsNotRelit` (the controls over the restored model
  have counted no verb, and the next L toggles the SAME source on both, one
  verb each). Controls N23, N23b (restore without the carried source -- the old
  prescription's half -- red in both).

**D2 -- trap 7 against the plan's Light row: A VALUE DERIVED FROM DIALS IS NOT
SAVED; it is re-derived at restore from the dials of the build that loads.**
- `Source.Radius` is the kind's dial (`TorchRadius`, `HearthRadius`). It is no
  longer in `LightSourceSnapshot`; `Light.radiusOf` sets it at `Add` and at
  `Restore`. `TestLightRadiusIsTheDialsNotTheSave` (control N24).
- Soul pressure was `dials.Pressure` plus what the dawns and rites moved,
  saved as one number (`rising.go`: `NewRising` started it at the dial). The
  rising now keeps `accrued` (the dawns and rites) and `Pressure()` adds the
  dial; the snapshot saves `pressure_accrued` only, and the provider reports
  it beside `pressure`. A script's `pressure` write is a dial (it moves the
  constant so the total reads what was written) and is not saved, like every
  harness dial. `TestRisingSavesOnlyTheAccruedPressure` (control N24b).
- The plan's Light row lists "radius" as saved; it should not (for the plan).

**D3 -- squads with deployed models are B4b's.** A deployed squad's models are
map entities, rebuilt with their saved ids through B2b's next-id seam, which is
the hunted-night load's. `Squads.Restore`'s register row (and its Validate
twin's) moved from B4a to B4b. B4a's scope is a quiet evening with no deployed
squad models: its load restores s:1 through `Squads.Restore` and REFUSES a
snapshot holding a deployed squad until B4b. That makes the row go live in
B4a, early; the burst that moves it to wire must show the refusal. Documented
on `Squads.Restore` and in the register.

**D4 -- all-or-nothing load: every Restore has a Validate twin.** On all nine
systems -- Clock, Light, Squads, Corpses, Rising, Combat, Spawns, Notice,
Pursuit -- `Validate(snap[, ...])` runs every check Restore runs and changes
nothing, and each Restore calls its Validate first, so the two cannot
disagree. The signatures: `Clock/Light/Squads/Corpses.Validate(snap)`,
`Rising.Validate(snap, worldSeed, bodies CorpsesSnapshot)` (it reads the
FILE's corpses, since the registry is still empty when a load validates),
`Combat.Validate(snap, worldSeed)`, `Spawns.Validate(snap, r, worldSeed)`,
`Notice/Pursuit.Validate(snap, r)`. The load validates every block, then
restores every block, and any refusal tears the whole game down and falls back
per rule 7 (the load order below). Control N22 (Corpses.Validate reduced to
its emptiness check: red); every refusal test calls both where it can.

**D5 -- rule 8 against exact positions: decided for B4b, documented, no code.**
If the map's SHA matches the file's, positions must match exactly (today's
check: a member must resolve to an entity standing where he was saved). If the
SHA differs, the world file is SET ASIDE with a message and he begins at dawn
(rule 7's path), rather than half-restoring packs on a changed map. **This
narrows plan rule 8** ("if the village has been rearranged since you saved,
the save still loads") **to the hero only**: on a rearranged village his
hero, kit and progress still load, at dawn; the night he saved in does not.
A decision for Josh to see.

## The B4 load order -- the one authoritative list

It replaces the plan's section 5 list, B2a's per-system notes above and B2b's
"What B4b must do on load" (which put the villagers after the entities and
left the world RNG out). B4a builds the steps that name no entity; B4b the
rest. **Both are built (29 Sep 2026).** B4b moved step 3 (below: it runs in
`CreateGame`, before step 4, not on the first frame) and added the bodies to
step 5; "B4b (a hunted night resumes)" at the end of this file has the whole
account.

1. **Before `Open`.** Read the file; a version other than 1 is refused (rule
   7). Decode every seed as an int64 (B1 notes section 2). Refuse in a network
   game -- rule 9 refuses LOADS as well as saves (the TCP handlers race the
   counted world stream, C13) -- **and set the file aside** (the B4a review
   fixes, B4a-R1; B4a-2 had left it, and the next single-player load wrote
   its older sidecar over the network evening, BUG-61). Compare the map's SHA (D5): different -> set
   the file aside, begin at dawn. **The pairing (B3 review, 29 Sep): the
   file must be this hero's -- `World.CheckHeroFile(his .od2)`, name and
   class -- and of the sidecar file's generation --
   `World.SameMoment(his sidecar file)`, the sidecar's `generation` equal to
   the file's `saved_at`; either refusal sets the file aside and falls back
   per rule 7** (a sidecar of another generation is a save cut off between its
   files). `SetNextGameSeed(file seed)`, `SetNextStartPosition(hero.pos)` --
   `hero.pos` in sub-tiles, bit-exact; `hero.x, y` are the same point in world
   tiles, for reading, and are never the start position (the review's wording
   item: this step said "hero x, y"). **Keep his own sidecar first** -- in
   memory and as `N.od2.strigoi.json.preload`, flushed -- so any refusal from
   here on can put it back byte for byte, and a crash before the load ends is
   undone at the next start (the B4a review fixes, A1: BUG-60). Write the
   embedded sidecar blocks, with
   the off-hand torch's `BurnLeft` ZEROED when the light block holds a carried
   source (D1); its `generation` comes with it, so the resumed game's kit saves
   carry the file's generation from the first (bindKit reads it). **Only a game that
   resumes it does** (the B4a review fixes, B4a-R2): any other game's kit
   saves carry no generation.
2. **In `CreateGame`, the clock: Validate, then Restore, right after
   `NewClock`** (trap 1). It is the one block restored before the others are
   validated, because every system built after it samples it; it checks
   itself first, and a later refusal still tears the whole game down (step 8).
3. **~~In `bindGameControls`, after `bindKit`~~ AT THE END OF `CreateGame`,
   once the seed and the map are the file's and before step 4's validation
   (B4b, decision B4b-1: step 4 validates through a Resolver that must find
   the entities, and B4a had already put step 4 in `CreateGame`): rebuild the
   world's entities** (`Game.rebuildEntities`, inside `Game.checkLoad`).
   Villager re-ids first (`Game.rekeyNatives`): each villager the map built,
   matched to the file's native by (`name_key`, `born`), given his saved id IN
   PLACE by the re-key verb B4b built (`MapEngine.RekeyEntity`) and his saved
   motion; a villager the file lacks taken off the map (B3-6) -- **and, since
   the B4b review fixes, noted in the load report and the log and dropped
   from the screen's natives (BUG-79)**. Then each
   saved entity (`Game.rebuildEntity`): the engine's `SetNextEntityID(id)`,
   build its kind (`NewCreature` from its bestiary entry and that entry's
   stand-in monstat, `SetCreatureID`; `NewNPC` from its monstat), check
   `ID() == id` (a mismatch refuses the file), `RestoreMotion` (refuses a
   Motion no entity could have had) and read it back equal, ~~its name the
   saved `name_key`~~ **its name noted when it is not the saved `name_key`
   -- a label, not the key: the entry is found by `creature_id`, the monstat
   by its key (the B4b review fixes, BUG-80)**, `AddEntity`. ~~**An entity or a
   villager saved while it held an action is refused before any of it
   (BUG-76).**~~ **An entity or a villager saved while it held an action is
   restored with it at its saved frame, read back equal (BUG-87; the refusal
   is gone).** Then assert `PendingEntityID()` is empty.
   Deployed squad models are among these entities (D3). **The re-key must be
   IN PLACE -- the entity the map built, given the saved id -- or the game
   screen's natives must be captured again after it (B3 review, B3):** the
   screen holds each native by its entity and its (`name_key`, `born`), not
   its id, so a re-keyed villager is still native at the next save; a
   villager rebuilt as a new entity is not. B4b re-keys in place.
4. **VALIDATE EVERY BLOCK (D4)**, through ONE Resolver built over the resumed
   map: Light, Squads (~~B4a: refuse a deployed squad~~ B4b: every deployed
   model an NPC on the map, `Game.checkSquadModels`), Corpses, Rising
   (against the file's corpses), Combat, Spawns, Notice, Pursuit, **Seek (the
   raid's R2: its rows and stand-ins resolve nothing, so it is checked alone,
   after Pursuit in `checkLoad`'s list)**, the game
   spawner's arrival, the bodies (B4b: `Game.checkBodies`, every body a
   monster on the map, none known yet), every saved stream
   (`StreamState.Check(fileSeed, name)`, the world's under `StreamWorld`), the
   hero's health, the scene. Nothing has been restored but the clock and the
   entities. **The Resolver names the player before the controls bind** (B4b,
   BUG-65: `Game.thePlayer` is the client's own player until
   `bindGameControls` sets `localPlayer`; B4a's quiet evening had no watch on
   him, so no step-4 Resolver had asked).
5. **RESTORE EVERY BLOCK**, in this order: player health (after
   `applyProgress`); squads (deployed ones too, B4b); light, WHOLE (D1);
   corpses WITHOUT the `changed` callback, `placeTheDead` skipped (trap 5);
   rising (after the corpses); spawns, which puts the tables' stream back,
   then the spawner's arrival; notice; pursuit; **seek (the raid's R2: after
   the watches it reads, before combat)**; combat; the monsters' bodies
   (B4b: `Game.restoreBodies`, health and max per id, refused over bodies
   already known); the scene, `lastStage` and `dawnPaidDay` together from the
   file (trap 1, BUG-17); **fog (fog of war F3, version 4): the explored grid,
   after a step-4 check that it is this map's and this size -- what he sees
   is not restored, the first frame's fog update sees it again from where he
   stands**. He stands at his saved `hero.pos`: his walk
   is not restored (rule 4). **He faces `hero.facing` (`SetDirection`), has
   `hero.stamina`, and runs if `hero.run`** (`SetIsRunning` with the toggle,
   as the HUD's run button does): B3 review, B6.
6. **THE WORLD RNG AND THE uuid STREAM, AFTER the entities** (trap 6):
   `MapEngine.RestoreRand(seed, draws)` -- NewCreature and NewNPC drew from the
   world stream in step 3 -- and, in a harness build, the uuid stream's byte
   count (`RestoreReader`), on BOTH paths: a fresh launch and the death
   screen's in-process "load last save" (B1 notes section 1).
7. **Harness only: re-apply the dials a script set**, which are never saved.
8. **Any refusal, at any step from 1 to 6, tears the whole game down** --
   the client closed, the world released -- **puts his own sidecar back
   first, byte for byte, when step 1 had written the file's over it** (the
   B4a review fixes, A1), sets the file aside with the reason (every refusal;
   ~~but HUNTED's, which stays for B4b: B2~~ -- B4b resumes a hunted night and
   the HUNTED refusal is gone; a file that cannot be moved is not read again
   in that run: B1), and falls back per rule 7: he begins at dawn
   from HIS sidecar, **on the file's seed** (C3). A half-restored world never
   runs a frame. A game that never opened puts his sidecar back too and
   leaves neither seed nor start position armed (C5). **Around the order:**
   only a game that resumed the world file carries its generation in its kit
   saves (B4a-R2), and "load last save" out of a network game refuses the
   world file under rule 9 (C4). The whole account: "B4a review fixes" at the
   end of this file.

**As B4a built it (29 Sep 2026; "B4a (a quiet evening resumes)" below has
the whole account).** The order stands; what B4a settled inside it:

- Step 1 writes the embedded sidecar byte for byte, the torch's minutes IN it,
  and step 5 zeroes them in the kit it binds (decision B4a-1: D1's own "the
  kit the load binds"; a fall back to dawn from that sidecar keeps a torch
  that lights). Rule 9's refusal did not set the file aside (B4a-2) --
  **overturned by the review fixes (B4a-R1): it does.**
- Step 3, for B4a: the villagers the map built are checked against the
  file's natives by (`name_key`, `born`) and standing as saved, not re-keyed
  (B4b re-keys).
- Step 4 is `Game.checkLoad` at the end of `CreateGame` (the seed, the map,
  the natives, every block), plus three checks that need his kit and progress
  bound, made on the first frame BEFORE the first restore (`checkBound`: a
  kit, his health within his maximum, a carried torch only with a torch in
  his off-hand). Nothing but the clock is restored before all of them pass.
- Step 5 restores spawns, the spawner, notice and pursuit in B4a too when they
  name no entity (B4a-3), and ends with the region read at once (the sound
  environment is derived).
- The first frame of a loading game binds its controls BEFORE anything else
  in `Advance`, so no frame of the world runs before step 5.

## For the plan (the main session's to write into the build plan)

- **Trap 4, replace the text with:** "A torch's minutes are in the light
  model's carried source and, via `saveKit`, in the embedded kit, lit or
  doused. The light model is the truth: restore the light snapshot whole,
  carried source included (id, lit, burn, `next_id`), and zero the off-hand
  torch's `BurnLeft` in the kit the load binds. Never re-light through the L
  path: it gives the torch a new id, moves `next_id`, lights a doused torch
  and counts a torch verb the saved game never made (D1, 28 Sep)."
- **Trap 7, add:** "nor anything derived from one: a source's radius, soul
  pressure's constant (D2)". And the section 1 Light row loses "radius".
- **Rule 9:** "a network game refuses to save" becomes "...to save or to
  load".
- **Rule 8:** narrowed to the hero (D5).
- **Section 5, B4a/B4b rows:** `Squads.Restore` is B4b's (D3); every Restore
  has a Validate twin and the load validates all before it restores any (D4);
  the load order is the list above.

## Findings, fixes, tests and controls

| B1 | Restored streams were unchecked: any `{seed, draws}` was taken, so the rising's and combat's blocks swapped restored both, each on the other's dice, and a count near 2^64 would have hung the load in the replay | `d2rand.StreamState.Check(worldSeed, name)`: the seed must be `SeedFor(worldSeed, name)` (the game seed for `StreamWorld`, `Derive` for the rest; an unknown name is an error) and the draws at most `MaxDraws` = 10^9. The number: a night is estimated at thousands of draws per stream (plan section 8: not measured), so 10^9 is ten thousand nights at a hundred thousand each; `BenchmarkRestoreSkip` measured the replay at 1.5 ns a draw, so a stream AT the cap restores in ~1.5 s (10^10, the review's example, would be ~15 s). Rising, Combat and Spawns Restore now take the world seed and check; `MapEngine.RestoreRand` refuses a count past the cap | `TestStreamStateCheck`, `TestStreamStateRefusesASwappedBlock` (every seed of the Derive tests), `TestCombatAndRisingRefuseEachOthersStream` (THE REVIEW'S MUTATION), the refusal tables of rising/combat/spawns, `TestRestoreRandRefusesACountPastTheCap` | N1, N2, N3, N4, N5 |

**The fixtures moved to game seed 2026** (`b2aWorldSeed`): with the seed check,
a fixture's streams must be the ones a game DERIVES, and on the combat stream
1462 derives, the combat fixture's scripted fight does not wait on its second
round. 2026 is the first of a short list on which both the combat and the
rising fixture play as written; B2b's fake world derives its tables' stream
from 1462 and needed no change beyond that.

- **Unit negative controls:** 43 (N1-N31 and their b/c variants), every one

- **Playtest controls:** P1 (a forced disengage before act 3's crits) red,
  "ended_reason "disengaged", want enemies_dead"; P2 (the removal's unwatch

  (`playtest-nc-P1.txt`, `playtest-nc-P2.txt`).
- **Gate** (`gate-1.txt`): gofmt clean, both builds, the three vets, 59 test

  entries, every one where the register says, in 35 min.

  `TestRising`, `TestTownWalkDeterministic`, `TestSquadsOnScreen`) green; then
  the whole suite once at `-parallel 4`, **72 of 72 top-level scripts green in

## What the review fixes did NOT do

- No save verb, no file, no load: B3 and B4. Every Snapshot, Restore and
  Validate is still dead in the shipped game; their rows stay DEFER.
- D5 is documented only; no SHA is read or compared yet.
- The kit-torch zeroing of D1 is a B4 step, documented, not built.
- `Squads.Restore` still does not check that a deployed model's entity is on
  the map (it has no Resolver); B4b's order rebuilds them first.
- `wandered` is refused only when it is LOWER than a wanderer's body; the
  machine writes exactly the count of `wanderer:<n>` bodies, and a higher
  value restores a consistent world (the next one gets a fresh name).
- No runtime test that `NewNPC` wears the id (MPQs); B4b's playtest owns it.
- No test of the seam's mutex.

# B3: the file and the save verb (branch `save-b3`, 28 Sep 2026)

Josh's ruling is that the save "stops at that point then resumes at that
point". B3 writes the file B4 will load, and the one verb every save goes
through. Nothing loads the file yet (B4); nothing in the shipped game calls the
verb yet (B5: the escape menu, the close hook, the dawn autosave). Today the
harness's `strigoi_save_game` is its one caller.

## The file: `N.od2.world.json` (package `d2core/d2save`)

Beside the hero's `N.od2` and his sidecar `N.od2.strigoi.json`
(`d2save.WorldPath`). JSON, indented two spaces, a trailing newline, the
top-level blocks always in this order (`d2save.Blocks`, held to `World`'s field
order by `TestBlocksAreTheWorldsFields`):

| Block | Type | What |
|---|---|---|
| `version` | int | `1`. Anything else is `ErrWorldVersion` (below). |
| `build` | string | The build that wrote it: `"<branch> <commit>"` (`SetSaveBuild`, from `d2app.Create`; `main.go`'s defaults read `"local build"`). Informational: never refused on. |
| `saved_at` | string | Wall clock, RFC 3339 with nanoseconds, UTC. The ONE field two saves of one moment differ in. |
| `map` | `{path, sha, generated}` | `d2mapgen.HostMap()`: the `.tmj` the game built and the SHA-256 of its bytes in hex (D5). Diablo II's generated Act 1 is `{"path": "", "sha": "", "generated": true}`. |
| `seed` | string | The game seed (`gameClient.Seed`), as an exact decimal STRING (`json:"seed,string"`): a float64 reader is exact too. |
| `rng` | `{world, spawns, combat, rising, uuid?}` | Every counted stream as `d2rand.StreamState {seed (string), draws}`. `world` is the map engine's (`RandSeed`, `RandDraws`). `spawns`, `combat`, `rising` are COPIES of the stream each of those blocks already carries, and must equal it (decision B3-1). `uuid` `{seed (string), bytes}` is the harness's seeded uuid reader, present only in a harness game whose stream is seeded. |
| `hero` | `{name, class, x, y, pos, facing, health, stamina, run}` | `name`, `class` the .od2's `heroName` and `heroType` (by name, `"Amazon"`): whose file this is (B3 review, A1). `x`, `y` in WORLD tiles (as everything but a motion); `pos` the same point in SUB-tiles, bit-exact (decision B3-5); `facing` the direction his body faces (`Player.Facing`, 0-63); `health` his body's (`Stats.Health`); `stamina` (`Stats.Stamina`, which the .od2 does not carry); `run` the run toggle (`IsRunToggled`). No motion: rule 4, he stands at `pos` after a load, facing `facing` (B3 review, B6). |
| `sidecar` | object | The kit-and-progress document -- `{version, generation, kit, progress, village, land, journal}` -- as THIS save wrote it into `N.od2.strigoi.json`: `d2items.HeroBytes` is taken once and the same bytes go into both files (decision B3-4). `generation` is this file's `saved_at` (B3 review, B7). |
| `clock` | `d2world.ClockSnapshot` | B2a. |
| `light` | `d2world.LightSnapshot` | B2a (the carried torch whole, D1; no radius, D2). |
| `squads` | `d2world.SquadsSnapshot` | B2a. |
| `spawns` | `d2world.SpawnsSnapshot` | B2b, through the game's Resolver (below). |
| `spawner` | `{arrival}` | `gameSpawner.Snapshot` (B2b). d2save names the same JSON (`d2save.Spawner`): the game screen's `SpawnerSnapshot` lives in a package that imports d2save. |
| `notice` | `d2world.NoticeSnapshot` | B2b. |
| `pursuit` | `d2world.PursuitSnapshot` | B2b. |
| `corpses` | `d2world.CorpsesSnapshot` | B2a. |
| `rising` | `d2world.RisingSnapshot` | B2a. |
| `combat` | `d2world.CombatSnapshot` | B2a. |
| `bodies` | `[{id, health, max_health}]` | Every monster body (`Game.bodies`), sorted by id. A slain monster's body is here at 0. |
| `entities` | `[{id, kind, native, monstat, creature, name_key, born, x, y, motion}]` | Every NPC and creature on the map, sorted by id (never the player, an item, a missile or an object). `kind` is `npc` (an inherited monster: `monstat` names its monstats `Id`) or `creature` (a project creature: `creature` names its BESTIARY entry, `Creature.CreatureID`). `name_key` who stands in for him. `native` marks the entities the map built -- present when the game screen was made (`nativesOf`, in `CreateGame`) -- and `born` is where they stood then, world tiles; decision B3-6. `x`, `y` world tiles, `motion` B2b's `d2mapentity.Motion` (sub-tiles). |
| `scene` | `{watch_stood, field_dead, dawn_paid_day, last_stage, watch_clock, watch_clock_set}` | The game screen's bookkeeping. `field_dead` in the order laid; `last_stage` by name. `watch_clock` is SAVED, not derived (decision B3-2). |

A real one, 16 KB, from `TestSaveResume` act 7c:
`strigoi-harness-runs\pt\TestSaveResume\save-resume-7c.world.json` (rewritten
by every run of the script).

**Why the B2 types directly.** A field a B2 snapshot gains is in the file the
day it is added, and the classification tests B2 wrote over every system are
what keep the file complete. d2save adds only what B2 did not snapshot: the
hero, the entities, the bodies, the scene, the map, the streams' index.

## Reading it: `d2save.Decode` (what B4's step 1 calls)

In order, refusing anything a load could not restore exactly:

1. Not a JSON object: `ErrWorldFile`.
2. `version` not the integer literal `1` -- `2`, `0`, `-1`, `"1"`, `1.0`,
   `null`, or absent -- is a `*VersionError` naming it, which `errors.Is`
   `ErrWorldVersion`, and nothing else is read. `VersionOf(data)` reads only
   the version.
3. A block missing, `null`, or not one of `Blocks`: `ErrWorldFile`. **A missing
   block is never an empty system.**
4. A field this build does not know, at any depth: `ErrWorldFile`
   (`DisallowUnknownFields`).
5. `World.Check` (next section).

Seeds decode through typed `int64` fields, never float64
(`TestSeedsPast2To53Survive` at 2^62+12345: exact typed, and an exact string
to a float64 reader, where the same number as a JSON number reads another
seed).

## `World.Check`: the file's own refusals

Everything the file can refuse with no game to hand (the systems' own checks,
each B2 `Validate`, need the game the load builds: B4, D4):

- `map`: generated XOR (path and a 64-hex sha).
- `rng`: each copy equals its block's stream; each of the four streams passes
  `d2rand.StreamState.Check(seed, name)` (on the seed's stream, at most
  `MaxDraws`) -- so a swapped pair is refused.
- `hero`: finite; `x, y` exactly `d2vector.Position.World()` of `pos`; health
  above 0 (a dead hero is never saved).
- `sidecar`: an object of version 1 with a kit.
- `clock`: finite, not negative.
- `entities`: ids unique, sorted, never `""` or `player`; `npc` names a
  monstat and no creature, `creature` the reverse; `native` iff `born`;
  finite; `x, y` exactly the world point of `motion.pos`.
- `bodies`: unique, sorted, each an entity in the list, `0 <= health <= max`,
  `max >= 1`.
- LIVE REFERENCES: every spawns member not `gone` is an entity standing
  EXACTLY at the member's `x, y`; a `gone` member is NOT an entity; every
  watcher, watch target, hunter, quarry and deployed squad model is an entity
  or the word `player`. (The corpse registry's maps are history, not live
  references, and are not checked.)
- `scene`: finite, `watch_stood >= 0`, `last_stage` a stage.

`SaveWorld` runs `Check` on the world before encoding and `Decode` on the bytes
before writing: **strict at save** -- a file no load could read is never
written over the player's save.

## Writing it: `d2save.WriteWorld`

- A version-1 file already at the path is the previous save: copied to
  `.bak` (`d2items.KeepGeneration`), rule 5.
- A file of any other version, or not a world file at all, is one this build
  cannot read; rule 7 says it is never overwritten: it is MOVED to
  `.v<N>.unread` (a whole-number version) or `.unread` (anything else), under
  a numbered suffix (`.1`, `.2`...) if that name is taken, so a second never
  overwrites the first. `.bak` is left alone. (Decision B3-7: this happens at
  SAVE too, not only at load -- until B4 exists, a newer build's file could
  sit there when an older build saves.)
- Then `d2items.WriteFileAtomic`: a temporary file and a rename, retried while
  Windows holds the target.

The `.od2` now writes the same way (`HeroStateFactory.Save`: `.bak`, then tmp +
rename; BUG-48). The sidecar has NO `.bak`: it is rewritten on every equip,
level and fight's end, so its "previous generation" would be the last equip,
not the last save -- and the world file carries its own copy, so `world.bak`
is the previous save whole (decision B3-11).

## The verb: `Game.SaveWorld(SaveOptions)` (`d2game/d2gamescreen/save.go`)

1. **Arguments**: `omit` without `to` is refused (`errOmitNeedsTo`).
2. **Refusals** (a `*SaveRefusal`, `errors.Is` `ErrSaveRefused`; NO file is
   touched -- nothing has been read or written yet), in this order:
   - `NOT_READY`: no game; `NETWORK` (rule 9: the TCP handlers race the
     counted world stream); `NOT_READY`: no hero save opened, or no hero in
     the world yet;
   - `DEAD`: `died` or his body at 0 (checked BEFORE the fight: a man who died
     in a fight is `DEAD`);
   - `COMBAT` (since the combat status, 30 Sep 2026: "The combat status (30
     Sep ruling)" at the end of this file): he is in combat -- his fight
     live, a hostile chasing him, his own swing or hit or block reaction
     playing, or the grace after the last of these. It took `FIGHTING`'s "a
     fight is running" and its swing clause;
   - `FIGHTING`: a fight running (`Combat.Fighting`), a paced fight holding the
     world (`WorldHeld`), the fight edge not yet taken back (`wasFighting`), a
     strike waiting on a walk (`pendingStrike`), a tactical pace or tile still
     out, or the journal still reading a fight (`journalFight`) -- and, after
     these, `Combat.Snapshot`'s own refusal (experience or paced minutes not
     yet taken, the pace window open) is `FIGHTING` too;
   - `TALKING`: `talk != nil` (a talk that reaches its end is ended by
     `Answer` at once, so this is "a talk is open"). A sleep's shelter is set
     only inside one answer's call and can never be seen by a caller, so it
     is not a refusal of its own (decision B3-10);
   - `JOURNAL`, `LOADOUT`;
   - `NOT_READY`: no kit (he has never chosen), or a world system missing.
3. **Snapshots**, every one a read: every B2 `Snapshot`, through ONE Resolver
   over the live map for spawns, notice and pursuit (`worldResolver`: an
   entity id is that entity wrapped as the live path wraps it -- `chaser` --
   and `player` is `prey{localPlayer}`; **B4b's load must build its Resolver
   the same way**), the sidecar bytes (`heroBytes`, the one document
   `saveKit` also writes now), the bodies, the entities, the scene, the map.
4. `World.Check`, `Encode(omit)`, and (without omit) `Decode` of the bytes.
5. With `to`: write ONLY the world file there, and stop (decision B3-3).
6. Otherwise: **the world file** (`WriteWorld`), then **his `.od2`**
   (`OnPlayerSave` -> the server's save, synchronous for a local client; its
   error now comes back, BUG-49), then **his sidecar** (the SAME bytes), then
   **the death screen's copy re-taken** (`snapshotHero`), so "load last save"
   after a death means this moment in all three files. A failure part-way
   says which files are written ("the world file is written and his .od2 is
   not"); the `.bak`s hold the save before.

**It changes nothing in the world.** No stream is drawn, no minute passes, no
system advances; the one thing a save moves is the death screen's copy.
`TestSaveResume` 7a and 7c: the state digest before and after is one digest;
control P1 (one draw from the world stream) is red on `[systems rng]`.

**It is not in `OnUnload`** and cannot be: `OnUnload` releases every world
system and nils the bodies before its `.od2` save. `OnUnload` is unchanged but
for one thing: a failed `.od2` save is logged and the unload goes on (the kit's
save, the client's close) instead of returning early (BUG-49).

## What B4 reads, step by step (the one authoritative load order above)

| Load step | Reads |
|---|---|
| 1, before `Open` | `version` (Decode); rule 9; `hero.name`, `hero.class` against his .od2 (`CheckHeroFile`); `saved_at` against his sidecar file's `generation` (`SameMoment`); `map` (D5: `HostMap` vs `path`/`sha`/`generated`); `seed` -> `SetNextGameSeed`; `hero.pos` -> `SetNextStartPosition` (sub-tiles; `x, y` are the same point in world tiles); `sidecar` -> write it to `N.od2.strigoi.json` (re-indented, it is the document `SaveHero` writes), the off-hand torch's `BurnLeft` zeroed when `light` holds a carried source (D1). |
| 2 | `clock`. |
| 3 | `entities`: `native` ones re-keyed to `id` by (`name_key`, `born`); every other one rebuilt through `SetNextEntityID(id)` -- `npc` by `NewNPC(monstat)`, `creature` by `NewCreature` from the bestiary entry `creature` (and `SetCreatureID`) -- then `RestoreMotion(motion)`. |
| 4 | Every block's Validate; `rng.*` checked against `seed` (already done once by Decode); `hero.health`; `scene`. |
| 5 | `hero.health`, `hero.facing`, `hero.stamina`; `squads`; `light`; `corpses`; `rising`; `spawns`; `spawner.arrival`; `notice`; `pursuit`; `combat`; `bodies` (each id's health and max); `scene` (`last_stage` and `dawn_paid_day` together; `watch_stood`, `field_dead`, `watch_clock`, `watch_clock_set`); `hero.run`. Also derive `paceRoundKey` from `combat.last_round` (encounter#round), so the first frame writes no ROUND line for a round already logged (the game screen's field classification says so). |
| 6 | `rng.world` -> `RestoreRand`; `rng.uuid` -> `RestoreReader` (harness only). |

## The game screen's fields, every one classified

`TestGameFieldsClassified` (`save_test.go`) labels every field of `Game` as
`S:<block>` (saved there -- checked to be a block), `D` (derived on load, or
re-taken), `T` (only ever set while a save is refused) or `W` (wiring), as B2
did for every system. A field added to `Game` fails the test until someone
decides. The T fields are exactly the refusals above; the D fields are
`kitPath`, `journalQuiet` (a resumed game's first frame is quiet: it writes
what the saved one would, without the notice), `natives`, `heroAtEntry`,
`noticeDelta` (from his talents), `metersBodied`, `lastRegionType`,
`ticksSinceLevelCheck`, `paceRoundKey` (B4 derives it) and `torchWasOut`
(always false at a frame's end).

## New on providers first (B1's rule)

`scene.natives` (`id`, `name_key`, `born`), `scene.map_path`/`map_sha`/
`map_generated`, and a creature's `creature_id`. The hero's `x`, `y`, `health`
and run toggle were already on `get_player`.

## The harness

`strigoi_save_game` IS `Game.SaveWorld` (harness 0.13.0; the tool count is
unchanged at 37): refusal codes as error codes, `to`, `omit` (needs `to`;
`BAD_ARGUMENT` for a name that is not a block), and `world_path`, `written`,
`kept`, `set_aside`, `blocks`, `bytes` in its result. `d2app/harness_uuid.go`
hands the save the uuid reader's position (`SetUUIDStream`).

## Decisions (the recommended option taken each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B3-1, `rng` holds copies.** The plan lists `rng {world, spawns, combat,
  rising, uuid?}` while B2 put each gameplay stream inside its own block.
  Taken: both, and `Check` refuses a file where a copy and its block
  disagree, so the copy can never be a second truth; `rng` is the one place a
  load reads to check every stream. (Alternative: `rng {world, uuid?}` only.)
- **B3-2, `scene.watch_clock` is saved**, against the plan's "D". Measured by
  reading `keepWatch`: a labour verb moves the clock inside a frame and the
  next frame credits the jump (capped at `watchJumpMinutes`); a save in
  between that derived the clock would credit nothing. With the menu or the
  dawn that gap cannot be hit; with a paused harness it can.
- **B3-3, `to` writes only the world file**, and `omit` needs `to`: a file
  with a block missing never replaces his save, and a negative control's file
  is a whole save of the moment (it embeds the sidecar).
- **B3-4, the sidecar is embedded whole** (its `version` too), the same bytes
  the sidecar file gets.
- **B3-5, positions twice where the load must be exact**: world tiles to read
  and compare (`x`, `y`), sub-tiles to restore (`pos`, `motion.pos`), held
  equal by `Check`.
- **B3-6, native = on the map when the screen is made.** B4b re-keys them by
  (`name_key`, `born`). A native that died and was taken off the map is simply
  absent from the list; B4b must remove what the map rebuilds and the file
  does not have. Natives carry their motion, but an NPC's patrol (path index,
  repetitions) is not carried (B2b: "only villagers patrol"), so a villager
  resumes his walk, not his patrol's count -- B4b's call.
- **B3-7, set aside at save as well as at load** (above).
- **B3-8, any change to a snapshot's JSON shape bumps `Version`.** Decode
  refuses unknown fields but NOT a missing nested field (it would read zero):
  a file from before a field existed would load that field as zero. There is
  no migration (plan section 6), so an added field is an incompatible change.
  **Nothing held this rule until the B3 review (B1): Decode now refuses a
  missing field at any depth, and the file's shape is pinned to its version
  by a hash and a golden file (`TestTheFileShapeIsPinnedToItsVersion`).**
- **B3-9, refusal order**: arguments, readiness and network, `DEAD`, then
  `FIGHTING`, `TALKING`, `JOURNAL`, `LOADOUT`.
- **B3-10, `TALKING` is `talk != nil`**; the shelter needs no refusal.
- **B3-11, no sidecar `.bak`** (above).
- **B3-12, a failed `.od2` save is an error to its sender** (BUG-49), and
  `OnUnload` logs it and goes on.
- **B3-13, the register**: `SaveWorld`, every B3 Snapshot row and the d2save
  verbs are HARNESS-ONLY until B5 gives the save its game callers; `Decode`
  and `World.Check` until B4a's load. What the game already calls is wire:
  `KeepGeneration` (every hero save), `HeroBytes` (every kit save),
  `Creature.SetCreatureID` (every creature the spawner places).
- **B3-14, a script's `strigoi_reseed_world` makes every later save refuse**
  (`INTERNAL`: the world stream is no longer on the game's seed, which no load
  could restore). Harness only; the game never reseeds mid-game.

## Tests and controls

**Unit** (all green; `go test ./d2core/d2save ./d2core/d2items ./d2core/d2hero
./d2game/d2gamescreen ./d2networking/d2server ./d2core/d2map/d2mapentity`):

- d2save: `TestTheFixtureIsAReadableFile`, `TestBlocksAreTheWorldsFields`,
  `TestEncodeDecodeEncodeIsByteStable` (save, decode, encode: equal bytes; the
  keys in Blocks' order), `TestSeedsPast2To53Survive`,
  `TestAVersionOtherThanOneIsRefused`, `TestEveryBlockIsRequired`,
  `TestEncodeOmit`, `TestCheckRefusesWhatNoLoadCouldRestore` (44 cases, each
  the fixture with one thing wrong), `TestAGeneratedWorldIsAMap`,
  `TestWorldPath`, `TestWriteWorldKeepsTheLastGeneration`,
  `TestWriteWorldSetsAsideWhatItCannotRead`,
  `TestWriteWorldGoesThroughATemporaryFile`.
- d2hero: `TestSaveKeepsTheLastGeneration`, `TestSaveGoesThroughATemporaryFile`.
- d2items: `TestKeepGeneration`, `TestHeroBytesIsWhatSaveHeroWrites`.
- d2server: `TestAFailedHeroSaveIsReturned`.
- d2gamescreen: `TestEverySaveRefusalTouchesNoFile` (17 cases, each with its
  files hashed, and a refused save to another path writing nothing),
  `TestANetworkGameIsNotSaved` (LAN host and client; the local control),
  `TestOmitNeedsTo`, `TestGameFieldsClassified`, `TestTheEntityList`,
  `TestTheWorldResolver`.
- d2mapentity: `TestMotionFieldsClassified` gains `creatureID`.
- A REAL fight's refusal is two halves: `Combat.Snapshot` refuses mid-fight
  (`ErrCombatFighting`, d2world's `TestCombatSnapshotRefusals`, B2a), and the
  save turns that into `FIGHTING` with every file untouched
  (`TestSaveResume` 7b, a real fight in a real game). The unit tests above set
  the screen's own fight-only state exactly.

"Every block present and non-empty in a full-game fixture": the unit fixture
fills every block (`b3Fixture`), and the FULL-GAME fixture is the playtest's
7c -- a game screen with a player cannot be built without the MPQs.

**Playtest** `TestSaveResume` act 7 (above; `docs/harness.md` has the act
list): green (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b3\playtest-saveresume-2.txt`).

**Negative controls** (each broken in place, run, restored):

- Unit, 35: `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b3\nc-unit.txt` (script `nc_unit.py`) --
  U1-U23 with their b-variants: version check off; a missing, a null block, an
  unknown field taken; blocks written in another order; the seed as a number;
  the rng copies, a stream's seed, the live refs, the hero's pos, a member's
  place unchecked; no `.bak`, a v2 file overwritten, a second set-aside
  overwriting the first, a write in place (world file and `.od2`); the server
  swallowing the error; an empty `.bak`; `SaveHero` writing another document;
  each refusal clause off (DEAD, the fight edge, the strike, the tactical
  walk, the journal's fight, TALKING, JOURNAL, LOADOUT, NETWORK); a write
  before the refusal; omit without to; an unclassified `Game` field; a
  creature with no bestiary entry saved; no native marked; `creatureID`
  unlabelled. **All red.** Four (U1, U6b, U8, U14) were first written so they
  broke the BUILD -- red for the wrong reason -- and were rewritten and re-run
  red on the assertion (the second block of `nc-unit.txt`). U6-U8b were run a
  third time after `World.Check` stopped using method values ("Found on the
  way"; U7 rewritten for the new shape): red (the third block).
- Playtest, 4 (`wt-b3\playtest-nc-P*.txt`, script `pt_nc.py`): P1 the save
  draws one number from the world stream -> 7a "saving moved the world: the
  digest parts [systems rng] changed"; P2 the death copy not re-taken -> 7e;
  P3 the world file written before the refusal -> 7b "a refused save touched
  a file"; P4 another sidecar embedded than written -> 7a. All red. (P1's
  first form did not compile -- `playtest-nc-P1-invalid-build.txt` -- and was
