## The runs (28 Sep 2026, `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b3\`)

- **Gate** (`gate-2.txt`, the final code): gofmt clean, both builds, the
  three vets, 60 test packages (d2save the new one) and the harness tests,
  the ebiten and headless checks, Article V, mod tidy, notices -- all green.
  d2save, d2hero, d2items, d2gamescreen, d2server and d2mapentity link no
  ebiten (`ebiten_check.sh`).
- **Reach gate** (`reach-gate-2.txt`, `-Jobs 4`): 366 entries, every one where
  the register says, in 27 min. (`reach-gate-1.txt` is the red first run --
  "Found on the way".)
- **Playtests:** `TestSaveResume` alone green (`playtest-saveresume-2.txt`);
  the whole suite at `-parallel 4` twice -- the first RED on three cursor
  flakes that passed alone (`playtest-suite-1.txt`, `playtest-rerun-3.txt`,
  BUG-15), the second, on the final code, **74 of 74 top-level scripts green**
  (one opt-in skip) in 7 min 51 s (`playtest-suite-2.txt`).
- **Controls:** `nc-unit.txt` (35), `playtest-nc-P1..P4.txt` (4),
  `nc-reach.txt` (2): every one red; the files restored and the tests green
  after (the gate above ran on the restored tree).

## Found on the way

- **BUG-48**: the `.od2` was written in place with nothing kept (fixed: `.bak`
  and tmp + rename).
- **BUG-49**: a failed hero save reported success to its sender, and
  `OnUnload` returned early on any save error (fixed).
- **A method value reads as wired to the reach gate.** `World.Check` first
  ran its ten checks from a `[]func() error` of method values. The first full
  reach gate went RED: `d2rand.SeedFor` and `StreamState.Check` "expected
  harness-only, measured live" -- `deadcode -whylive` showed the path
  `main -> d2app.Create -> parseArguments -> (dynamic) pkg/profile.Start$10 ->
  (dynamic) d2save.World.checkRNG`: an escaping `func() error` is callable, to
  the analysis, from every dynamic call of that type in the program. The
  checks are now called one by one, and every row measures where it says
  (`wt-b3\reach-gate-1.txt` the red run, `reach-targeted-2.txt` the rows
  after the fix). The pattern is worth knowing for every future verb whose
  register row must stay harness-only: no method values of a common
  signature on its path.
- **Three cursor flakes** in the first full suite (`TestStrigoiIsTheGame`,
  `TestWatch`, `TestSquadsOnScreen`), all scripted pointer reads, all green
  re-run alone: BUG-15's class, filed there.

## What B3 did NOT do

- **No load** (B4): nothing reads the file; `Decode` and `Check` run only on
  what a save is about to write.
- **No game caller** (B5): the escape menu's SAVE GAME, SAVE AND EXIT, the
  close hook, the dawn autosave (rule 10) and the refusal notices.
  `SaveWorld` is callable from inside a frame with no effect on the world
  (7a/7c's digest), which is what rule 10 needs.
- Acts 1-6, 8 and 9 of `TestSaveResume`.
- The sidecar's own `.bak` (B3-11).
- A unit-level full-game fixture (no MPQ-free player; the playtest is it).
- `-race` on the save (no cgo on the laptop, BUG-23's note).

# B3 review fixes (29 Sep 2026, branch `save-b3`)

An independent review read B3 (`19188a47`) and found one defect that would
have handed a deleted hero's night to the next hero made (A1), a set of holes
between what the file promised and what anything held it to (A2, B1, B2, B7),
a saved hero missing two things the digest compares (B6), a native record keyed
by an id B4b changes (B3), two harness defects (B4, B5), and a list of small
ones (C). Every fix below has a test that was seen RED without it: the unit
controls are N1-N25b (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b3fix\nc-unit.txt`, script
`nc_unit.py` beside it), the playtest controls P1-P11
(`wt-b3fix\playtest-nc-P*.txt`, script `pt_nc.py`). The recommended option
was taken wherever one was open (the decisions below); Josh can overturn any.

**The file's shape changed and it is still version 1** (there are no shipped
saves): `hero` gained `name`, `class`, `facing` and `stamina`, and the embedded
sidecar gained `generation`. The golden file and the shape's hash were made
from the new shape (`d2core/d2save/testdata/world-v1.json`,
`b3ShapeHashes[1]`).

## The decisions (R1-R9)

- **R1 (A1) -- a hero's identity is his name and class; a deleted hero's files
  all go.** The `.od2` holds no hero id or creation stamp, so the world file
  names `hero.name` and `hero.class` (the `.od2`'s `heroName`, `heroType` by
  name) and `World.CheckHeroFile` holds it to the `.od2` beside it. Name and
  class do not tell two heroes of one name apart; their NUMBER does, and the
  number is now never handed out while any file named after it remains
  (`firstFreeFileName`). Delete REMOVES (not sets aside) every file named after
  the save -- `N.od2`, `N.od2.bak`, `.tmp`, the sidecar, the world file, its
  `.bak`, every `.unread` -- because the player asked for the hero to be
  deleted; his `.od2` goes last and stays if anything else could not be
  removed, so he is still listed and the delete can be finished.
- **R2 (B3) -- natives by (`name_key`, `born`), held by the entity.** The
  review's recommended option. The pair is checked unique at save
  (`saveEntities` refuses) and in the file (`native-twice`). **B4b must re-key
  a villager IN PLACE** (the entity the map built, given its saved id) or
  capture the natives again after rebuilding them.
- **R3 (B7) -- the generation token is the world file's `saved_at`, carried in
  the sidecar as `generation`.** Built (about forty lines of code, under the
  review's sixty). The save writes it into both; every kit save between world
  saves writes the same generation (`Game.saveGeneration`, read back by
  `bindKit`), so the pair agree until the next world save and disagree only
  after a save cut off between its files. `World.SameMoment(sidecar file)`
  refuses the pair; **B4 falls back per rule 7 on that refusal**. The `.od2`
  carries no token -- it is upstream's format -- and needs none, by the
  invariant below.
- **R4 -- the `.od2` invariant, written down.** Of what the `.od2` carries,
  Strigoi's game changes only `Health` and `MaxHealth`. `Health` is the world
  file's (`hero.health`). `MaxHealth` is re-derived on every load from the
  sidecar's `BaseMaxHealth` and his talents (`applyProgress`); the `.od2`'s is
  read once, on a hero's first load, to seed `BaseMaxHealth`. Everything else
  it holds -- name, class, level, experience, attribute and skill points,
  mana, `MaxStamina` (his body's), gold, act, Diablo II's equipment and skills
  -- is written when he is made and never changed by Strigoi's game. So an
  `.od2` one save behind its world file resumes the same moment.
  `TestPlayerAndControlsFieldsClassified` labels each of those fields `O`
  (the `.od2`'s, never changed); a field Strigoi starts changing must move to
  the world file.
- **R5 -- stamina is saved** in the `hero` block (the review's recommendation:
  cheap and future-proof). The `.od2` never carried it (`json:"-"`).
- **R6 (B5) -- `to` is fenced in two places.** The harness makes it absolute
  and keeps it under `%APPDATA%` or the temporary folder and out of any source
  tree of this game (`harnessSaveTo`, reusing the editor's
  `harnessSourceTree`). `SaveWorld` refuses a `to` that is one of HIS files
  (in his folder, named after his save) **with or without `omit`** -- stricter
  than the review asked: without `omit`, a `to` over his world file would leave
  the world file one moment and his `.od2` and sidecar another. Both are
  `BAD_ARGUMENT` (`ErrBadSaveArgument`).
- **R7 (the order item) -- `WorldHeld` dropped, combat's own refusal moved.**
  `Combat.WorldHeld` is `encounter != nil && ...`, and `Fighting` is
  `encounter != nil`, the clause before it: it could never fire. The combat
  model's own refusal (`Combat.Snapshot`'s: experience or paced minutes not yet
  taken, the pace window open) now sits in `fightUnsettled` with the screen's,
  before `TALKING`, `JOURNAL` and `LOADOUT`. And one refusal is new: **his last
  swing still playing is `FIGHTING`** (`Player.isCasting`, found by classifying
  the Player's fields: the digest compares `casting` and the animation mode,
  and a load stands him still).
- **R8 (B2) -- strict at save runs the load's own checks.** `Validate` for the
  clock, light, rising and combat; for the five systems whose `Validate` also
  refuses a system in use -- squads, corpses, spawns, notice, pursuit -- a new
  `CheckSnapshot`: `Validate` is now that in-use refusal followed by
  `CheckSnapshot`, so the two cannot disagree; and the spawner's `Restore` got
  its check as `validate`. A refusal is an error, not a `SaveRefusal`: the live
  world holds something no load would take, reported `INTERNAL` with the
  block, before any file is touched.
- **R9 (B1) -- B3-8 has teeth in two halves.** `Decode` refuses a field this
  build writes that the file lacks, at any depth (`field-missing`); and the
  shape -- every path, JSON option and kind the `World` type writes -- is
  hashed and pinned to the version, beside a committed golden file of that
  version. Regenerating the golden file alone does not turn a shape change
  green: the version-1 hash is what it was.

| C | Stamina | R5 | as B6 | N13, P7 |

**The savable unit game.** B3's unit tests stopped at `NOT_READY`: the screen
had no world systems. `b3SavableGame` (`save_review_test.go`) builds every
system as `CreateGame` does, on seed 2026, around a bare player, so
`SaveWorld(to)` writes a whole file in a unit test. Most of the tests above
use it, `TestASavableGameSaves` is its control, and `saveProbe` (a package
variable only a test sets) is how B2's probes reach the file between the
snapshots and the checks.

## New on providers first (B1's rule)

`get_player` already reported `direction`, `stamina`, `name` and `class`; the
sidecar's `generation` is read from the files by the playtest.
`strigoi_remove_entity` reports `body_dropped`.

## The register

`Clock/Light/Rising/Combat.Validate` moved from dead to HARNESS-ONLY (the save
calls them; the load is still their game caller, B4a).
`Squads/Corpses.CheckSnapshot` (B4a) and `Spawns/Notice/Pursuit.CheckSnapshot`
(B4b) are new, harness-only. `World.CheckHeroFile` and `World.SameMoment` are
new, dead, for B4a's step 1. `Player.Facing` harness-only (B5). WIRE:
`d2hero.DeleteHero` (the hero screen calls it) and `d2items.RenameRetrying`
(every save). OBSERVE: `Game.ForgetBody`, the harness removal's third half
beside `Game.Unwatch`. Measured where the register says, targeted
(`wt-b3fix\reach-targeted-1.txt`) and in the full reach gate below.

## Found on the way

- **BUG-58: the digest moves on its own for a second after a walk.** The ui
  provider reports the overhead bars in screen coordinates, and the camera
  eases toward him over render frames, which run while the simulation is
  paused. 7a went red on it ("saving moved the world: [systems]") the first
  time the script walked him before a save; a scratch probe read the bar's x
  as 385, 382, 381, 380 over four digests with nothing called between them
  (`wt-b3fix\zzdump`, the probe kept as `zz_b3debug_test.go.keep`). Worked
  around by `digestSettled`; **the fix is B4's to choose**, whose acts compare
  digests across a relaunch.
- **A hero faces one of 64 directions**, not 16: the playtest measured 56. The
  file's bound is 0-63.

## The runs (29 Sep 2026, `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b3fix\`)

- **Unit:** `go test` of d2save, d2items, d2hero, d2gamescreen, d2mapentity,
  d2world, d2server and reachcheck, and d2app under the harness tag: green
  (`unit-1.txt`), and green again with every control's file restored
  (`unit-2-after-nc.txt`).
- **Unit negative controls:** 35 (N1-N25b with their b-variants), every one as expected -- red where
  the break must be seen, green where it shows the hole a fix closes (N6b: the
  review's mutation under the old assertion) or where a constant does its work
  (N25) -- and each red for its own assertion, not a build failure
  (`nc-unit.txt`, `nc_unit.py`).
- **Playtest negative controls:** 11 (P1-P11), all red on the assertion they
  guard (`playtest-nc-P*.txt`, `pt_nc.py`). P8's first form did not compile
  (`playtest-nc-P8-invalid-build.txt`) and was re-run red on its assertion.
  P5 let the game write `b3-relative.world.json` into the worktree -- the
  defect itself -- and the control removed it.
- **Playtests:** `TestSaveResume` and `TestSpawns` first (`playtest-1.txt`):
  TestSpawns green (act 8's `body_dropped`), TestSaveResume RED at 7a on the
  digest's camera drift (BUG-58, diagnosed with a scratch probe,
  `debug-*.txt`, `zzdump\`); with `digestSettled`, green
  (`playtest-2.txt`). Then **the whole suite once at `-parallel 4`: 74 of 74
  top-level scripts green, one opt-in skip, 476 s** (`playtest-suite-1.txt`).
  After the last code change (the combat check asked once, not twice, in
  `fightUnsettled`) `TestSaveResume` again, green (`playtest-saveresume-3.txt`).
- **Reach, targeted** (`reach-targeted-1.txt`): every changed and new row where
  the register says. **Gate** (`gate-2.txt`) and **reach gate**
  (`reach-gate-1.txt`, `-Jobs 4`) on the final tree: gofmt clean, both builds, the three vets, 60 test packages and the harness
  tests, the ebiten and headless checks, Article V, mod tidy, notices -- all
  green; **377 entries, every one where the register says, in 28 min 36 s**.
- **Launches:** 90 (the suite's 72, 12 controls, 6 development and probe
  runs); no startup crash, no panic.

## What the review fixes did NOT do

- **No load.** `CheckHeroFile`, `SameMoment`, the facing, stamina and run
  restores, the natives' in-place re-key: all are B4's steps, documented in
  the load order above, not built.
- **The fallback on a torn save is B4's**: the token detects it; rule 7 says
  what to do.
- **No hero id or creation stamp** was added to the `.od2` (R1).
- **BUG-58 is worked around in one script, not fixed.**
- **The dropped `WorldHeld` clause has no control**: it could not fire.
- **The fsync is proved by reading**, not by a test that could see one.
- Retries and the held-file tests have teeth on Windows only; on Linux the
  rename succeeds at once.

# B4a (a quiet evening resumes) (branch `save-b4a`, 29 Sep 2026)

Josh's ruling is that the save "stops at that point then resumes at that
point". B3 wrote the file; B4a is the first code that reads one back into a
game, for every world that names no hostile entity. The acceptance test the
milestone was designed around -- save at T, step 120 world minutes, relaunch,
load, and the relaunched game equals the original at T and again at T+120 --
is green (`TestSaveResume` acts 1-6), and so is the death screen's "load last
save" in the same process (6b).

## The way in

Every way into a game goes through `App.ToCreateGame` (`d2app/app.go`), so
that is where a load begins: the character select's load, the death screen's
"load last save" (`App.ReloadGame`), the World Editor's playtest and the
harness's `strigoi_start_game`. A new hero has no world file (the hero screen
never hands out a number a file is named after: `firstFreeFileName`), so a
new game is exactly what it was. The menu's SAVE GAME is B5's.

## What loads, where, and in what order

The order is the authoritative one above ("The B4 load order"), and
`LoadReport.Steps` records it as it runs (the harness reads it:
`strigoi_start_game`'s and `strigoi_get_game_info`'s `load.steps`). Every step
is in `d2game/d2gamescreen/load.go` unless named.

| Step | Where | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `PrepareLoad`, called by `App.ToCreateGame` before `d2client.Create` and `Open` | Read `N.od2.world.json`. Refuse (below) on rule 9, `d2save.Decode` (version, shape, `World.Check`), `CheckHeroFile`, `SameMoment`, and B4a's own limit (`huntedNight`). Then write the embedded sidecar over his sidecar file (re-indented: byte for byte the document the save wrote). The App then calls `d2server.SetNextGameSeed(file seed)`, `d2server.SetNextStartPosition(hero.pos)` and, in a harness build, reseeds the uuid stream from byte 0 of `rng.uuid`'s seed (`harnessLoadBegins`). |
| 2 | `CreateGame`, right after `NewClock` (`Game.restoreClock`) | `Clock.Validate`, then `Clock.Restore` (trap 1). Every system built after it samples the restored clock. |
| 3 | -- | B4b's: rebuild the entities. B4a rebuilds none. |
| 4 | the end of `CreateGame` (`Game.checkLoad`) | The game runs on the file's seed; the map is the file's (D5: generated either way, or the same SHA); the villagers the map built are the file's natives one for one by (`name_key`, `born`), each standing as saved (`checkNatives`); then every block's own `Validate` through one Resolver over the resumed map (`worldResolver`, the save's type): light, squads (a fresh owner), corpses (an empty registry), rising (against the FILE's corpses), spawns, the spawner's arrival, notice, pursuit, combat, the world stream (`StreamState.Check`) and the scene's stage. Nothing but the clock is restored. |
| 5 | the first frame, in `bindGameControls` after `bindKit` (`Game.resumeLoad`) | First the checks that need his kit and progress bound (`checkBound`): a kit (so the loadout choice is not holding the world), his saved health within the maximum `applyProgress` gave him, a carried torch only with a torch in his off-hand. Then, in order: his health; squads (s:1 in place); light WHOLE and the kit torch's `BurnLeft` zeroed (D1: step "torch"); corpses WITHOUT the open-count callback, `placeTheDead` skipped (trap 5); rising; spawns (their open bodies, counters, stream and the minutes to the next check from the file); the spawner's arrival; notice; pursuit; combat, and `paceRoundKey` derived from its last round; the scene, `lastStage` and `dawnPaidDay` together (trap 1, BUG-17), `watch_stood`, `field_dead`, `watch_clock`, `watch_clock_set`; then him -- standing at `hero.pos` bit-exact with no walk and facing `hero.facing` (`Player.StandAt`, rule 4), his stamina, his run toggle (`GameControls.RestoreRun`, the way the HUD's button flips it) -- and the region he stands in read at once (`checkRegion`: the sound environment is derived, and the saved game had long read it). |
| 6 | the end of `resumeLoad` | `MapEngine.RestoreRand(rng.world)` last, after everything that draws from the world stream (trap 6). In a harness build, the resume hook (`SetResumeHook`, `d2app/harness_load.go`) puts the uuid stream at `rng.uuid`'s count (`d2rand.RestoreReader`). |
| 7 | the resume hook | Harness only: every dial the script set in this process re-applied, the last value of each (below). |
| 8 | `Game.abandonLoad`, `App.FallBackToDawn` | A refusal anywhere tears the whole game down and falls back per rule 7 (below). |

**A half-restored world never runs a frame.** A loading game binds its
controls at the TOP of its first `Advance` (a fresh game binds at the end of
its first frame, as before), restores there, and returns before anything else
of the frame when a load is still pending or was abandoned. In a harness game
under the stepped clock every frame before `start_game` returns has a zero
delta anyway; in the shipped game the first frames have real deltas, and none
of them reaches `advanceWorld` before the file is back in it. The save refuses
`NOT_READY` while a load is pending or abandoned.

**D1, as built (decision B4a-1).** The brief said to write the embedded
sidecar with the torch's minutes zeroed. B4a writes the sidecar byte for byte
as the save wrote it -- the torch's minutes in it -- and zeroes them in the
kit the load BINDS, which is D1's own wording ("the kit the load binds"). Two
reasons: a file refused after the sidecar is written (a changed map, a block
the load refuses) falls back to dawn from that sidecar, and a zeroed torch
there is a torch that will not light (`BurnLeft <= 0` is "no torch" to L); and
the death screen's copy (`snapshotHero`, taken by `bindKit` from this file) is
then the save's document exactly. The unit test and the ui's new
`kit_torch_left` hold the zeroing; control P18 shows it red.

## The refusals

Each is a `*LoadRefusal` (`errors.Is` `ErrLoadRefused`) with a code, written
to the log (`LOAD refused ...`) and to the harness's load report (`refused`,
`reason`, `set_aside`, `fell_back`). The player-facing notice is B5's.

| Code | Where | When | The file |
|---|---|---|---|
| `NETWORK` | step 1 | a LAN host or client (rule 9) | ~~stays (decision B4a-2)~~ **set aside since the review fixes** (B4a-R1: staying, it lost the network evening to the next load, BUG-61). He begins with his hero, kit and progress. |
| `VERSION` | step 1 | a version other than 1 | set aside `.v<N>.unread` (or `.unread`) |
| `FILE` | step 1 | version 1 and not a file a load could read (`Decode`); or not readable at all | set aside `.v1.unread` (one not readable at all: `.unread`, since the review fixes -- before, it stayed) |
| `HERO` | step 1 | another hero's file (`CheckHeroFile`: name and class) | set aside |
| `TORN` | step 1 | his sidecar missing, or of another generation (`SameMoment`) | set aside |
| `HUNTED` | step 1 | B4a's limit: a pack on the map, a watch, a chase, a monster's body, an entity the map does not build, a squad with a deployed model (D3) -- B4b's | ~~set aside~~ **stays for B4b since the review fixes** (B4a-R3); his sidecar untouched |
| `SIDECAR` | step 1 | his sidecar file could not be kept (`.preload`) or written | ~~stays~~ **set aside since the review fixes** (B4a-R1), his own sidecar put back |
| `SEED` | step 4 | the game did not run on the file's seed | set aside; torn down |
| `MAP` | step 4 | another map (D5) | set aside; torn down |
| `NATIVES` | step 4 | the villagers are not the file's: one missing or added, two of one key, or one saved mid-walk or turned (his motion is B4b's to restore) | set aside; torn down |
| `BLOCK` | steps 2, 4, 5 | a system's own check refused its block (or `checkBound`'s) | set aside; torn down |

**Falling back.** A refusal at step 1 needs nothing torn down: nothing was
opened, and the game begins at dawn from his sidecar. A refusal at step 2 or 4
returns from `CreateGame`; `startGame` closes the client (BUG-25's path), and
`App.ToCreateGame` sets the file aside and calls `App.FallBackToDawn`. A
refusal at step 5 cannot come from the file's blocks (they were validated at
step 4) but from `checkBound` or a Restore that disagreed with its Validate;
`abandonLoad` marks the game abandoned -- its `Advance` returns at once, its
`OnUnload` writes neither the `.od2` nor the sidecar -- sets the file aside and
calls the same fall-back. `FallBackToDawn` re-arms the refused game's seed
(`SetNextGameSeed`; in a harness build the uuid stream too) and goes through
`ReloadGame`, so the old game's server lets go of its port first: the dawn
that replaces a refused load is the dawn a fresh launch of that seed would be.
The report of a refusal after the game opened is kept by the dawn game
(`fell_back`: it finds no file, having set it aside). **The review fixes
changed this paragraph's world** ("B4a review fixes" below): every refusal
of a prepared load puts his own sidecar back first (A1), the dawn runs on the
file's seed (C3), a file that cannot be set aside is not read again in the run
(B1), and the dawn keeps the report for itself only.

**Decision B4a-3: "hold anything" is read as "hold a group, a watch or a
chase".** The spawn tables always hold something -- open bodies, their
counters, their stream, the minutes to the next table check -- and so do the
notice and pursuit totals. B4a restores all of them when they name no entity
(the brief's "everything that names no hostile entity"); without the spawns
block act 4 is red on `spawns` (measured: control P8), and trap 5's "open
bodies from the file" is exactly the spawns block. So `Spawns/Notice/Pursuit
.Restore` and `gameSpawner.Restore` are called by B4a, through the save's
Resolver type, with nothing for it to resolve.

**Decision B4a-4: B4a rebuilds no entity**, so any entity that is not a native
is a refusal, and so is any monster body (bodies live with entities) and any
squad beyond s:1.

**Decision B4a-5: the natives are checked, not re-keyed.** By (`name_key`,
`born`) one for one, and each native's saved motion must be the motion of the
villager the map just built (JSON-equal): the authored village's villagers
stand, so in every quiet evening measured they were; one who walked would
need `NPC.RestoreMotion`, which is B4b's. In a harness game the villagers'
ids are also the file's (the uuid stream from byte 0 of its seed); in the
shipped game (crypto/rand ids) they are new each launch, and nothing B4a
restores names a villager by id -- the re-key is B4b's.

## The harness: both paths, and the dials

- **The uuid stream on both paths** (B1 notes section 1). A load reseeds the
  harness's uuid reader from byte 0 of the file's `rng.uuid` seed before the
  game opens, on a fresh launch and on "load last save" alike, so the
  connection and the villagers draw the ids the saved game drew; the resume
  hook then puts the reader at the file's byte count. Before B4a "load last
  save" continued the dead game's stream. (The seed itself is the file's on
  both paths: `SetNextGameSeed` in `ToCreateGame`.)
- **The dials** (step 7). `strigoi_set_system_field` records every write to a
  field `harnessDialFields` lists as a dial (clock frozen/moon; combat's
  twenty-one tuning fields; pursuit arrive_within/repath_tiles; rising
  edge_floor/hasty_weight/p/pressure; spawns chance/check_minutes/max_groups/
  notice_lit_level/notice_radius/rout_at); a load re-applies the last value of
  each, in first-written order. State (meters, light sources, open bodies, a
  group's morale) and verbs (commit, despawn, release, round) are never
  written again over what the load restored. A fresh process has recorded
  nothing: its script sets its dials after `start_game`, as act 4 does.
- **`start_game`** loads the world file when one is there (it goes through
  `ToCreateGame`), reports what the load did (`load`), and refuses
  `BAD_ARGUMENT`, starting nothing, when `seed` is not the seed the file was
  saved on (decision B4a-9; the plan had this in B4b's row). A load with no
  `seed` resumes the file's.

## BUG-58, fixed: the digest's world state and its process state

`digestSettled` is gone from `TestSaveResume`; 7a passes without it. The
digest (`d2app/harness_time.go`) now has SIX parts: `sim`, `process`, `world`,
`entities`, `rng`, `systems`. A provider may split its state
(`d2harness.Digester`): its world state goes to `systems`, this process's own
history to `process`, and presentation that moves on its own to neither.

| Provider | Left out of the digest | To `process` |
|---|---|---|
| `ui` | every screen coordinate: `bars[].x/y/w/h`, `kit_rows[].x/y`, `talent_cells[].x/y`, `talk_view.answer_y`, `journal_view` tabs' and rows' `x/y/w/h`, `mini_panel_buttons`, `run_button`; `hover_label` (a screen point read through the camera); `clock_strip_hours_to_dusk` (the clock sampled on whichever frame the strip last refreshed: 16.4656 saved, 16.46 resumed, one strip text) | `torch_verbs`, `clock` (the controls' frame clock) |
| `uuid` | -- | `games`, `seeded_game`, `seeded_for_this_game`, `bytes_at_game_start` |
| `journal` | -- | `last_written` (the id this session last wrote) |
| `assets` | -- | everything (the census of what this process loaded) |

`strigoi_get_state_digest` also returns `resume_digest` -- the hash over
`world`, `entities`, `rng` and `systems`, what a resumed game must reproduce --
and `systems`, each provider's world-state hash by name. The determinism proof
still compares all six parts between two launches of one script (leak #2 was
in the assets census, now in `process`). `strigoi_get_system_state` is
unchanged: a script still reads every field. Measured before the split, the
first smoke load (`wt-b4a\smoke-1.txt`) differed from the saved moment in
exactly these fields and nothing else; after it, in none
(`wt-b4a\smoke-2.txt`).

## Tests

**Unit** (`d2game/d2gamescreen/load_test.go`, on `b3SavableGame` with the
corpses' callback wired as `CreateGame` wires it and a real torch-and-blade
kit):

- `TestTheLoadResumesTheSavedMoment`: an evening saved, loaded into a fresh
  game through the load's own steps in order; the steps are the authoritative
  order; D1 (the carried torch equal, the kit's minutes zero); trap 5 (the open
  count the file's); rule 4 (his position bit-exact, not moving, run toggled);
  the loaded game saves the saved moment (every block but the stamp); both run
  four world hours on and save one world -- and the run on moved it.
- `TestARefusedLoadRestoresNothing`: fourteen step-2/4 refusals (seed, map,
  natives, each block, the world stream past the cap, a stage that is none)
  and three `checkBound` ones, each with its code, each leaving every block as
  the fresh game had it.
- `TestPrepareLoadRefusals`: the control (a good file taken, his sidecar the
  save's document byte for byte, the torch's minutes in it), no file, and
  twelve step-1 refusals, each set aside where rule 7 says with the sidecar
  untouched; rule 9's not set aside.
- `TestTheDawnAfterATornDownLoadKeepsItsReport`, `TestNoSaveWhileALoadIsPendingOrAbandoned`.
- Elsewhere: `TestNextStartPositionIsOneShotAndLocal` (d2server),
  `TestSetAsideKeepsWhatTheLoadRefused` (d2save), `TestStandAtStandsHimStill`
  (d2mapentity), `TestDigestParts` (d2harness),
  `TestTheLoadReappliesTheScriptsDialsAndNothingElse` and
  `TestTheUUIDReportsGameCountsAsProcessState` (d2app, harness tag). The game
  screen's field classification gained `pendingLoad`, `loadAbandoned` (T,
  refused `NOT_READY`), `loadFailed` (T, reset by `failPendingLoad`) and
  `loadSteps` (D).

**Playtest** `TestSaveResume` (`docs/harness.md` has the act list): act 7 (B3)
in a process of its own, then the evening. Act 1 runs from 02:45 of day 0 to
01:30 of day 2: a dog slain by day and taken off the map with his body, two
forages and three stakes, `dead:1` staked and `dead:2` in a hasty grave, the
other two risen at true dark and staked by day where first light laid them
down, the watch promised and stood from 21:20 (255 minutes by T), the torch
lit at 01:15. At T: night, `dawn_paid_day` 1 on day 2 (so trap 1's control
has teeth), the torch at 45 minutes. Act 3's two hours cross dawn (the night's
50 experience, the watch kept for +8, the rite and the soul pressure's count,
the risen men's groups long gone) and outlast the torch (it burns out at
T+45: `torch_out`, the kit's torch spent). The kept evening
(`pt\TestSaveResume\evening`) feeds the controls.

| U1b | the callback fired after the spawns | red | red: `TestTheLoadResumesTheSavedMoment`, open bodies |
| U2 | the kit torch's minutes not zeroed (D1) | red | red: "Should be zero, but was 60". **First run green**: the unit fixture's torch burnt out before the save, so the kit's zero and the model's agreed for nothing; the torch is now lit last (`nc-unit-1-first.txt`) |
| U3 | the natives unchecked | red | red: `TestARefusedLoadRestoresNothing` |
| U4 | the light block not validated at step 4 | red | red |
| U5 | `checkBound` after the first restore | red | red: "his health was restored" |
| U6 | D3: a deployed squad not refused | red | red: refused by the entity clause, not its own -- **first run green**: the test asserted only the code, and a deployed model is also a non-native entity; each HUNTED case now asserts its own clause |
| U7 | a refused file not set aside (rule 7) | red | red |
| U8 | the brief's D1 wording: the sidecar written with the torch zeroed | red | red: "his sidecar is the save's document, byte for byte" (B4a-1's instrument) |
| U9 | rule 9's file set aside | red | red (B4a-2's instrument) |
| U10 | the dawn forgets the refusal it replaced | red | red (first form did not build -- `prev` unused -- and was rewritten) |
| U11 | a save while a load is pending | red | red |
| U12 | a remote player put at the loaded hero's place | red | red |
| U13 | a second refusal overwrites the first set aside | red | red |
| U14 | rule 4: his walk continues after `StandAt` | red | red: he walked on to 136.25, 106.73 |
| U15 | the digest ignores a provider's split | red | red |
| U16 | state re-applied as if it were a dial | red | red: five writes re-applied, not two |
| U17 | the uuid game counts left in the world part | red | red |

## Register

B4a's load makes 31 rows live that were deferred: every Restore and Validate
it calls (`Clock`, `Light`, `Squads` -- D3's early move, with the deployed
squad's refusal shown by `TestPrepareLoadRefusals` -- `Corpses`, `Rising`,
`Combat`, `Spawns`, `Notice`, `Pursuit`, `gameSpawner.Restore`),
`Squads/Corpses.CheckSnapshot` (their `Validate` calls them),
`Corpses.Snapshot` (`Rising.Restore` reads the restored registry through
it), the stream verbs (`StreamState.Check`, `RestoreInto`, `SeedFor`),
`d2save.Decode`, `World.Check`, `World.CheckHeroFile`, `World.SameMoment`,
and the two `MotionSnapshot`s (`checkNatives` reads the villagers'). Each
moved to WIRE with what its row said before recorded in its reason. New rows:
`PrepareLoad`, `Game.restoreClock`, `checkLoad`, `resumeLoad`,
`abandonLoad`, `SetLoadAside`, `App.FallBackToDawn`, `d2save.SetAside`,
`d2server.SetNextStartPosition`, `Player.StandAt`, `GameControls.RestoreRun`,
`LastLoad` (wire: `PrepareLoad` reads it); `SetResumeHook` (observe). The
Snapshot rows but the corpses' stay deferred to B5 (the save still has only
the harness for a caller).

**The first full reach gate disagreed on five rows** (`reach-gate-1.txt`),
all mine: `Corpses.Snapshot` and `LastLoad` measured live (registered defer
and observe); `Spawns/Notice/Pursuit.CheckSnapshot` measured harness-only
(moved to wire). Those three `Validate`s do not call `CheckSnapshot` -- both
call the same `b2bBuild`, unlike the squads' and the corpses' `Validate`,
which do -- so no load will ever be their caller, B4b's included: the rows go
back to defer with their game caller named as B5's save (B3's note above,
"`Validate` is now that in-use refusal followed by `CheckSnapshot`", holds for
the squads and corpses only). Fixed in the register, measured alone
(`reach-targeted-1.txt`) and in a second full run (`reach-gate-2.txt`).

## Decisions (the recommended option taken each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B4a-1, D1 as worded ("the kit the load binds").** His sidecar is written
  from the world file byte for byte, the torch's minutes in it, and the load
  zeroes them in the kit it binds. (The brief: write the sidecar zeroed.) A
  fall back to dawn from that sidecar keeps a torch that lights; the death
  screen's copy is the save's document exactly. Instruments U2, U8, P18.
- **B4a-2, rule 9 does not set the file aside.** A network game begins at
  dawn and leaves the world file for the next single-player load. (The brief:
  rule 7's fallback, the file set aside.) Instrument U9. **OVERTURNED by the
  review fixes (B4a-R1): the network game's kit saves carried the file's
  generation, and the next single-player load wrote the file's older sidecar
  over the network evening (BUG-61). Set aside now.**
- **B4a-3, "hold anything" read as "hold a group, a watch or a chase".** The
  spawns block, the spawner's arrival, and the notice and pursuit totals are
  restored by B4a when they name no entity. Instruments P8-P8d.
- **B4a-4, no entity is rebuilt**: any non-native entity, monster body or
  squad beyond s:1 is `HUNTED` (set aside, like every rule-7 refusal; there are
  no shipped saves to lose).
- **B4a-5, the villagers are checked, not re-keyed**: by (`name_key`, `born`)
  and standing as saved; a villager who walked is `NATIVES` (B4b's).
- **B4a-6, step 4 in two places, both before any restore but the clock's**:
  `checkLoad` at the end of `CreateGame`, and `checkBound` on the first frame
  for what needs his kit bound. Instrument U5.
- **B4a-7, the first frame of a loading game binds first.** A fresh game is
  unchanged.
- **B4a-8, a refusal after the game opened reopens the same hero at dawn**
  through `ReloadGame`, on the refused game's seed (and, in a harness build,
  its uuid stream from byte 0): the dawn a fresh launch of that seed would be.
- **B4a-9, `start_game` refuses a `seed` the world file was not saved on**
  (`BAD_ARGUMENT`; the plan had this in B4b's row).
- **B4a-10, the digest: a `process` part, `resume_digest`, each system's
  hash, and no screen coordinates** (BUG-58, BUG-59).
- **B4a-11, the sound environment is derived on load** (`checkRegion` at
  once), not a second into play.
- **B4a-12, the uuid stream is reseeded from byte 0 on every load of a file
  that carries one** (harness), so "load last save" equals a fresh launch.
- **B4a-13, the dials a load re-applies are a fixed list** in
  `harness_load.go` (`harnessDialFields`); a new dial must be added there.
- **B4a-14, `ui.kit_torch_left`**, new on the provider, so D1's kit half is
  observable with the panel closed (B1's rule).
- **B4a-15, act 3 leaves through the menu, wounded**, so the `.od2` and the
  sidecar beside the world file are later than it and must lose: the first
  control round found the health restore unexercised (P19b green) because
  both files held T's moment.
- **B4a-16, `Spawns/Notice/Pursuit.CheckSnapshot` back to defer, now B5's.**
  Their `Validate` does not call them (both call `b2bBuild`), so no load is
  their caller, B4b's included; the first full reach gate caught my move to
  wire. The alternative -- make those three `Validate`s call `CheckSnapshot`
  as the squads' and the corpses' do -- changes `d2world` for a register row
  and was not taken.

## For the plan (the main session's to write into the build plan)

- **Rule 9:** "a network game refuses to save or to load" -- ~~and a load it
  refuses leaves the world file for the next single-player game (B4a-2)~~
  **superseded by the review fixes' line below ("B4a review fixes", "For the
  plan"): a load it refuses sets the world file aside.**
- **Trap 4 / D1:** the load zeroes the kit torch's minutes in the kit it binds;
  the sidecar file keeps them (B4a-1).
- **Section 5, B4a's row:** B4a also restores the spawns (no groups), the
  spawner's arrival, notice's and pursuit's totals (B4a-3); the uuid count on
  both paths and the dials are B4a's (as the brief asked), and `start_game`'s
  seed refusal too (B4a-9). **B4b's row** keeps: every entity, the villagers'
  re-key and motion, bodies, groups, watches, chases, act 8.
  `Spawns/Notice/Pursuit.CheckSnapshot` will not go live with B4b's load
  (B4a-16); their game caller is B5's save.
- **Section 4, the acceptance test:** S is the digest's `resume_digest` --
  every part but `sim` and `process` -- not "the full digest", which carries
  the harness's clock and this process's history (BUG-59).

## The runs (29 Sep 2026, `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b4a\`)

- **The gate** (`gate-1.txt`): gofmt clean, both builds, vet x3, 60 packages
  ok / 0 fail, the harness tests, no ebiten in the leaf packages, Article V,
  mod tidy, notices -- `DONE`. Again on the tree as committed, after the
  register's five rows were fixed (`gate-2.txt`): the same, `DONE`.
- **The reach gate** (`-Jobs 4`): the first run (`reach-gate-1.txt`, 390
  entries, 32 min) EXIT=1 on five rows of mine (see "Register" above); the
  five alone after the fix (`reach-targeted-1.txt`, `-only=Snapshot` 17
  entries and `-only=LastLoad`, both OK); the second full run
  (`reach-gate-2.txt`): 390 entries, every one where the register says,
  EXIT=0, 1761 s. The fix touched only `tools/reachcheck`, which no game
  build links, so the playtest runs below stand for the tree as committed.
- **Targeted** (`playtest-targeted-1.txt`, `-parallel 4`): `TestSaveResume`
  (171.8 s), `TestDeath`, `TestTownWalkDeterministic`, `TestKit`,
  `TestProgress` -- 5 of 5 green, 8 launches.
- **The full suite** (`playtest-suite-1.txt`, `-parallel 4`): 74 of 74
  scripts green and the one opt-in skip (`TestMinimizedTick`), 802 s, 74
  launches.
- **Development:** the scratch probe `zz_b4a_smoke_test.go` (`smoke-1.txt`,
  red: BUG-59 found; `smoke-2.txt`, green; deleted, never tracked);
  `TestSaveResume` whole (`saveresume-1.txt`, red at 6c on its own instrument:
  `fell_back` is omitempty and `flag` refused its absence, now read as a bool;
  `saveresume-3.txt`, `saveresume-4.txt` green) and against a kept evening
  (`saveresume-2-from.txt`). The resume digest repeats across four whole runs
  (`saveresume-3`, `-4`, targeted, suite):
  `665cd969d08c` at T and `42ace236a5f2` at U. `evening-3` (from
  `saveresume-4.txt`) is the evening the controls ran against.
- **The controls:** playtest `nc-1\` (the first round, 28 launches) and `nc\`
  (the final round, 30 launches with P17's whole run), summary
  `nc-summary.txt`; unit `nc-unit.txt` (first round `nc-unit-1-first.txt`,
  reruns `nc-unit-2-reruns.txt`).
- **153 launches, no startup crash, no panic** in any of their game logs
  (`panic_check.sh`). The two red development runs were an instrument (6c) and
  the finding (BUG-59), not crashes.

## What B4a did NOT do (B4b's, B5's)

- **Any entity but the villagers** (B4b): packs, the risen, deployed squad
  models, monster bodies; the spawn groups, watches and chases that point at
  them; the villagers' re-keyed ids and their motion; a villager the file
  lacks removed. A file holding any is refused `HUNTED` or `NATIVES`.
- **Act 8** (rule 4 against a save mid-walk) and the hunted night's acts: B4b.
- **The notices a player sees** when a load is refused, the menu's SAVE GAME,
  the close hook, the dawn autosave: B5. B4a logs every refusal
  (`LOAD refused ...` in `strigoi.log`) and reports it to the harness.
- **The `.bak`** is not offered on a refusal (rule 5: kept, not offered).
- **No migration**: a file of another shape is refused and set aside.
- The shipped game's natives keep crypto/rand ids that differ from the file's
  every launch; nothing B4a restores names one, and B4b re-keys them.

# B4a review fixes (29 Sep 2026, branch `save-b4a`)

An independent review read B4a (`744fdfce`) and found a way for Josh to lose
progress -- the one thing a save must never do -- and two more ways the
load could lose or hang, plus a restore no test could fail on, a dial list
with holes, and five smaller items. This section is the whole account: each
finding, its fix, the test that holds it, and the control that showed the
test red without the fix. The recommended option was taken on everything
open (the brief's instruction); the decisions are listed below, and Josh can
overturn any. The unit controls are N1-N15
(`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b4afix\nc-unit.txt`, script `nc_unit.py`), the
playtest controls Q1-Q7 (`wt-b4afix\nc\`, summary `nc-summary.txt`, scripts
`nc_pt.py`, `nc_run.sh`, `nc_q6.sh`).

## Findings, fixes, tests and controls

| # | Finding | Fix | Test | Control (red without the fix) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1** (BUG-60) | A load refused after step 1's write -- at step 2 (the clock), step 4 (SEED, MAP, NATIVES, BLOCK) or step 5 (`checkBound`) -- left the world file's copy of his sidecar in place: the dawn he fell back to had the saved moment's experience and standing, and everything since was gone, while his health came from the later `.od2` (two moments mixed). The review's probe: act 3's files, the map SHA zeroed -- `MAP`, `fell_back`, sidecar xp 55 / rep 25 where it had been 105 / 33. | **Step 1 keeps his own sidecar first** -- in memory and as `N.od2.strigoi.json.preload`, flushed -- and **every refusal of a prepared load puts it back, byte for byte, before anything else** (`SetLoadAside` calls `restorePreload`; `d2gamescreen/preload.go`). So does a game that never opened (`RestorePreload`, from `App.ToCreateGame`'s `noGameOpened`) and a step-1 write that failed half done (SIDECAR). A load that resumes lets the copy go (`loadResumed` -> `forgetPreload`). A crash between the write and the load's end is undone at the next start of that hero (`recoverPreload`, first thing in `PrepareLoad`; decision B4a-R4 has the rules). The dawn he gets is his own last-entered dawn -- the same dawn a refusal at step 1 gives. | `TestARefusalAfterStepOnePutsHisSidecarBack` (the seed, the map, the villagers, the clock, a block -- the App's path -- and the first frame's `checkBound` through `abandonLoad`, and a game that never opened), `TestAFailedStepOneWritePutsHisSidecarBack`, `TestACrashedLoadIsUndoneAtTheNextStart` (recovered, discarded, kept), `TestAResumedLoadLetsTheCopyGo`, `TestPrepareLoadRefusals` (the control keeps the copy; no step-1 refusal keeps one). **Playtest:** `TestSaveResume` act 6e is the review's probe -- his act-3 files beside T's file with the map changed: `MAP`, fell back, his sidecar act 3's byte for byte, his experience act 3's, `load.preload` "restored"; and `refusedToDawn` reads his sidecar in every refusal act (6c-6f). | N1 (no restore: red, `load_review_test.go:130`), N2 (no recovery: red), N3 (the copy kept after a resume: red); **Q1** (no restore: red at 6e, "his sidecar is his own, byte for byte, after the refusal") |
| **A2** (BUG-61) | The network path wiped a LAN evening's progress. Rule 9 refused the load and LEFT the file (decision B4a-2); the LAN game's `bindKit` read the file's generation from his sidecar (`kit.go:52`) and every kit save wrote it back (`kit.go:236`), so the next single-player load paired (`SameMoment`) and wrote the file's older sidecar over the evening. SIDECAR and an unreadable FILE left the file the same way. The review rebuilt the state by hiding the file for a dawn session: 105 -> 125 in the session, 55 after the next load. **C4:** `ReloadGame` always opens a local game, so a LAN player's "load last save" resumed his single-player world file. | **Decision B4a-2 is overturned (B4a-R1): a network game's refusal sets the world file aside** with its reason -- his hero, kit and progress carry on; the saved night does not -- **and so do SIDECAR and an unreadable file** (`d2save.SetAside` now moves a file it cannot read, as `.unread`; one held open is B1's). **C4:** `LoadLastSave` out of a network game refuses and sets aside the world file before the App reopens him (`refuseNetworkReload`). **And the mechanism itself is closed (B4a-R2): only a game that resumed a world file carries its generation** (`bindKit`: `saveGeneration` is the sidecar's only when `pendingLoad` is set); any other game's kit saves carry none, so a file left beside a sidecar that has moved on -- HUNTED's (B2), one that could not be moved (B1), one hidden -- reads TORN and is set aside, never resumed over what he has. `SameMoment` says so in its reason. | `TestPrepareLoadRefusals` (rule 9: set aside whole, his sidecar untouched), `TestOnlyAResumedGameCarriesTheWorldFilesGeneration`, `TestLoadLastSaveOutOfANetworkGameSetsTheWorldFileAside` (LAN host and client; a local game's reload leaves it), `TestAnUnreadableWorldFileIsSetAside`, `TestSetAsideMovesAFileItCannotRead`. **Playtest:** act 6f is the review's probe -- T's file hidden for one session that earns 20; the next load finds it TORN, sets it aside, and the 20 stay. | N4 (rule 9 leaves the file: red), N5 (every game carries the generation: red), N6 (an unreadable file left: red), N6b (SetAside refuses what it cannot read: red), N7 (C4 unguarded: red); **Q2** (the generation carried: red at 6f -- the load resumed T over the session's sidecar) |
| **A3** | No test could fail on rule 4's run toggle as the shipped game restores it: `GameControls.RestoreRun` had no unit test, and at T the run toggle was off -- a fresh game's -- as was his wind (full). The review's controls X1 and X2 stayed green. | **Act 1 now makes every field the load restores on him differ from a fresh game's**, and its precondition says so (`heroNotFresh`, against `heroOf` read the moment the fresh game began): the run toggle on (the HUD's button), his wind below its maximum, a talent taken (Long Marches, from the panel: it changes no health), a fraction of a health point owed to neglect (seven minutes parched: 0.235), his facing not a fresh game's (55 against 0). The village's tiles are a town's, where running never spends his wind, so **the meters gained a `stamina` settable field** -- test setup as `health` is, for a body that has a wind (`d2world.Winded`, the hero's `playerBody`); decision B4a-R11. | `TestSaveResume` act 1's precondition and act 4 (S_R0 = S_T, the player's entity line); `TestTheMetersStandAWindedBodyAtAWind` (the new field, through the squads too). | **Q4** (the `RestoreRun` call removed: red at act 4, `run_toggled` true -> false, speed 13 -> 9), **Q5** (the stamina restore removed: red at act 4, stamina 31.38 -> 84); N14 (the field removed: red) |
| **B1** (BUG-62) | A refused-after-open file that could not be set aside looped for ever: `SetLoadAside` recorded the error, `FallBackToDawn` reloaded, the reload found the same file and refused it again. The review held the file open: 51 teardowns and `TIMEOUT_LOADING`. | **A file that failed to be set aside is ignored for the rest of the process**: remembered by path, size and modification time (`ignoreFromNowOn`), and no load reads it until it changes (`ignoredRefusal`, in `PrepareLoad` and `PeekLoad`); the dawn's report keeps the refusal and says `ignored`. The move itself was already retried for half a second (`d2save.setAside` -> `d2items.RenameRetrying`, the B3 review's C item); nothing to add there. | `TestAFileThatCannotBeSetAsideIsNotReadAgain` (a unit seam, `setAsideWorld`, holds the file portably; a file changed since is read again). **Playtest:** act 6g holds the file open on Windows: `MAP`, fell back, `ignored`, not set aside, **one** teardown, his own sidecar, the file whole where it was. | N8 (not ignored: red); **Q3** (not ignored: red at 6g -- 90 teardowns in the game log and `start_game` never returned) |
| **B2** | Most real saves are HUNTED (measured by the review: every day save quiet; every dusk, night and dawn save hunted; one slain dog makes the rest of that day hunted), and HUNTED set the file aside. | **HUNTED no longer sets the file aside** (decision B4a-R3): the file stays for B4b, and he begins at dawn with his sidecar untouched (the refusal is at step 1, before anything is written). With B4a-R2 the file stays harmless: once a dawn game writes his sidecar it carries no generation, and the file reads TORN at the next load. **B5 (the menu save and the dawn autosave) must not land before B4b**: the dawn autosave is itself HUNTED, so every save it made would be refused, and each refusal's dawn session would then make it TORN. | `TestPrepareLoadRefusals` (each HUNTED case: refused by its own clause, the file whole where it was, his sidecar untouched, no copy kept). | N9 (HUNTED set aside: red) |
| **B3** | The dials a load re-applies missed the village's `rite_radius`, `seen_radius` and `watch_radius` (`talk.go:377-398`): "load last save" put them back at the data's. | **Added** (`harnessDialFields`), and **every settable field of every provider is now classified**: a dial, or named in the new `harnessNotDials` as state or a verb. | `TestEverySettableFieldIsADialOrNamedState` reads every type with a `HarnessSet` from the SOURCE (so a provider added tomorrow is read too), takes its `HarnessName` and `HarnessSettableFields` (both must be literals), and fails on a field in neither list, in both, or listed and on no provider. | N10 (`watch_radius` dropped: red), N10b (the new `meters.stamina` unclassified: red) |
| **C1** | `start_seed` (the seed the script asked `start_game` for) was in the resume digest's world part: a load with no seed resumed the file's world exactly and differed in part `world` alone (the review's `probe-noseed`). | `start_seed` moved to the `process` part. | `TestSaveResume` act 4 now starts with **no seed**, as the shipped game has none to give, and requires the game on the file's. | **Q6** (`start_seed` back in the world part, a whole run: red at act 4 on part world) |
| **C2** | `start_game`'s seed refusal fired for a file the load would refuse anyway. | **Ordered after step 1's checks** (decision B4a-R6): `harnessWorldSeed` asks `d2gamescreen.PeekLoad` -- step 1's checks and nothing else, nothing written or set aside -- and holds the script to the file's seed only when step 1 would take the file. A file that passes step 1 and is refused after the game opens (a changed map, a block) still holds it: that game, and its dawn, run on the file's seed (C3). | `TestPeekLoadChecksAndWritesNothing`; **playtest** act 6c starts another hero's file on seed 7 and requires his dawn on seed 7. | N13 (PeekLoad only decodes -- the old rule: red); **Q7** (the old `harnessWorldSeed`: red at 6c, `BAD_ARGUMENT`) |
| **C3** | The fallback's seed was the refused game's (`gameClient.Seed`) -- the file's for every refusal but SEED, where it was the one the game should not have run on. | **Always the file's seed** (decision B4a-R7): `abandonLoad` and `App.ToCreateGame` pass it to `FallBackToDawn`. | `TestTheDawnAfterATornDownLoadRunsOnTheFilesSeed` (the game's seed moved after step 4 to tell them apart). | N11 (the game's seed: red) |
| **C5** | If `d2client.Create` failed after `PrepareLoad`, the armed seed stayed armed (only the start position was cleared), and the next game of any hero began on the refused file's seed. | `d2server.ClearNextGame` clears both; the App's two "no game opened" branches call it through `noGameOpened`, which also puts his own sidecar back (A1). `d2server.ArmedForNextGame` reports what is armed (observability). | `TestClearNextGameClearsTheSeedAndThePosition` (d2server), `TestAGameThatNeverOpenedLeavesNothingArmed` (d2app, harness tag). | N12 (the App clearing only the position: red), N12b (`ClearNextGame` keeping the seed: red) |

**From the raid's S0 measurements.** The raid's R0.5 burst will make the
world-file version follow `d2save.Version`, not the literal 1. Where this
burst touched `load_test.go`'s version-bound lines they are derived now:
the newer build's file is `{"version": Version+1}` set aside as
`.v<Version+1>.unread`, every other step-1 refusal expects `.v<Version>.unread`
(the `unread` name in `TestPrepareLoadRefusals`), and
`TestTheDawnAfterATornDownLoadKeepsItsReport` builds its name the same way;
the new tests build theirs from `d2save.Version` from the start. Not touched:
`playtest/save_resume_test.go`'s `worldFile` still requires `version` 1, and
`d2save`'s own tests are R0.5's.

## Found on the way (fixed)

- **BUG-63 -- the ui provider's digest carried this process's presentation.**
  The first run with act 1's talent and neglect went red at act 4 on
  `ui.talent_cells` (the talent panel's cells, which exist only once the panel
  has been opened in the process) and `ui.journal_notice` (the journal's timed
  HUD line, "Written in my journal: Thirst and the heat, and 1 more") and
  nothing else (`wt-b4afix\saveresume-2-from.txt`). Both moved to the
  `process` part (`GameControls.HarnessDigest`): the determinism proof still
  compares them; a resume does not. Whether each talent is taken is
  `progress.talents`, which the world part keeps. BUG-58 and BUG-59's class.
- **BUG-64 -- `p:1` stayed the wrong hero.** Act 6c now plays another hero on
  seed 7 (another player id); 6d came back to Evening and `strigoi_get_player`
  answered `UNKNOWN_HANDLE` (`saveresume-3.txt`): Evening's id already had its
  handle, so the reverse map was never put back. `p:1` is always the current
  local player now (`harnessHandleFor`); `TestP1IsAlwaysTheCurrentLocalPlayer`,
  control N15.
- **A torn-down load's report outlived its dawn.** The dawn that replaces a
  refused game keeps the refusal as its report (`fell_back`); the NEXT load of
  the same hero kept it too, so act 6f's hidden-file session read 6e's
  refusal as its own (`saveresume-4-from.txt`). Kept for that dawn only now
  (`LoadReport.carried`); `TestTheDawnAfterATornDownLoadKeepsItsReport`
  checks the next load reports itself.
- **`SameMoment`'s reason** for a sidecar of no generation said "a save was cut
  off between its files"; it says what happened now: a game that did not
  resume this file has written his sidecar since, and the file is older than
  what he has.

## The decisions (the recommended option each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B4a-R1 (A2) -- a network game's refusal sets the world file aside;
  OVERTURNS B4a-2.** So do SIDECAR and an unreadable file (moved as
  `.unread`), and "load last save" out of a network game (C4). The brief's
  alternative -- a network game's kit saves start a new generation so the old
  file reads TORN -- was not taken for the network path: set-aside is simpler
  and visible. Instruments N4, N6, N6b, N7.
- **B4a-R2 (A2) -- only a game that resumed a world file carries its
  generation.** Not in the brief as a separate item; taken because the loss
  mechanism does not need a network game, only a file left in place beside a
  session that did not resume it -- and B2 now leaves HUNTED files in place
  on purpose, and B1 leaves a file that cannot be moved. Without it, the
  first B4b build to resume a hunted night would write a months-old sidecar
  over everything he earned since that night. The review's own probe (a
  hidden file) is exactly this case, and act 6f holds it. Cost: a file left
  in place stops pairing the moment his sidecar is next written -- which is
  right, because it is then older than what he has. Instruments N5, Q2.
- **B4a-R3 (B2) -- HUNTED leaves the file** for B4b and falls back to his dawn
  with his sidecar untouched. **B5 must not land before B4b.** Instrument N9.
- **B4a-R4 (A1) -- the copy and its rules.** Kept in memory and as
  `N.od2.strigoi.json.preload` (`WriteFileAtomic`: flushed) before step 1's
  write; a copy that cannot be kept refuses the load (SIDECAR) before
  anything is written. Every refusal of a prepared load, the SIDECAR write
  included, and a game that never opened, put it back byte for byte and
  remove the file; a resumed load removes it. **A copy a crash left behind is
  judged at the next start of that hero** (`recoverPreload`): put back
  ("recovered") when his sidecar is still exactly the document step 1 wrote
  from the world file beside it and the copy is of that file's generation --
  or when his sidecar is not a document at all; removed ("discarded") when the
  world file beside it is of a later save; otherwise ("kept") moved to
  `.preload.kept`, never put over a sidecar that may be newer, never offered,
  never deleted (a person judges it). The copy is named after his save
  (`N.od2...`), so `d2hero.DeleteHero` removes it with his other files.
- **B4a-R5 (B1) -- ignored for the process, by path, size and modification
  time.** A file written since (a new save, a copy put back) is another file
  and is read. The report says `ignored`.
- **B4a-R6 (C2) -- start_game's seed refusal after step 1's checks**
  (`PeekLoad`), and not after the after-open ones: those games run on the
  file's seed whatever the script asked (C3).
- **B4a-R7 (C3) -- the dawn after a torn-down load runs on the file's seed.**
- **B4a-R8 (C1) -- `start_seed` is process state.**
- **B4a-R9 (C4) -- the guard is on the death screen's side** (`LoadLastSave`
  knows its game is a network one), not an App flag carried through
  `ReloadGame`: the file is refused and set aside before the reload is asked
  for, so the reload finds none, and a test can drive it without an App.
- **B4a-R10 (C5) -- `ClearNextGame` and `ArmedForNextGame`** on d2server; the
  second is observability only (tests read it).
- **B4a-R11 (A3) -- the meters' `stamina` settable field** (test setup; a body
  with a wind, `d2world.Winded`), classified as state (not a dial).
- **B4a-R12 (BUG-63) -- `talent_cells` and `journal_notice` are process
  state** in the digest.
- **B4a-R13 -- a torn-down load's report is kept by its dawn only.**

## The B4 load order, as the review fixes changed it

The authoritative list above ("The B4 load order") now reads, where it
changed (its text is updated in place):

- **Step 1** keeps his own sidecar -- in memory and as `.preload` -- before it
  writes the world file's over it (A1), and first of all undoes a load a
  crash cut off (`recoverPreload`). A file this process refused and could not
  set aside is not read (B1). **Every step-1 refusal sets the file aside but
  HUNTED's**, which stays for B4b (B2): rule 9's too (A2), SIDECAR's, and an
  unreadable file's.
- **Step 8**: a refusal after step 1 puts his own sidecar back FIRST, byte for
  byte, then sets the file aside (or, failing that, ignores it for the
  process), then falls back to dawn **on the file's seed** (C3). A game that
  never opened puts his sidecar back and leaves nothing armed (C5).
- **Around it**: only a game that resumed the world file carries its
  generation in its kit saves (B4a-R2); "load last save" out of a network game
  refuses the world file under rule 9 (C4).

## For the plan (the main session's to write into the build plan)

- **Rule 9, replace B4a's line with:** "a network game refuses to save or to
  load; a load it refuses SETS THE WORLD FILE ASIDE (rule 7's path) -- his
  hero, kit and progress carry on, the saved night does not -- and so does
  the death screen's 'load last save' out of a network game (B4a review
  fixes, B4a-R1, overturning B4a-2)."
- **Rule 7, add:** "every refusal sets the file aside but a hunted night's
  (HUNTED, until B4b resumes it); a refusal after the load wrote his sidecar
  puts his own back first, byte for byte; a file that cannot be set aside is
  not read again in that run."
- **Generation (the B3 review's R3), add:** "only a game that resumed a world
  file carries its generation; any other game's kit saves carry none, so a
  file left in place stops pairing once his sidecar moves on (B4a-R2)."
- **Sequencing: B5 (the menu save and the dawn autosave) must not land before
  B4b** -- the dawn autosave is itself HUNTED (every dusk, night and dawn save
  is), so before B4b every save it made would be refused, and each refused
  file would read TORN after the dawn session it caused.
- **Section 5, B4b's row, add:** a HUNTED file B4a left in place is B4b's to
  resume -- if it still pairs with his sidecar.

## What the review fixes did NOT do

- **No network game in the harness.** `start_game` opens local games only, so
  rule 9's set-aside (A2) and C4 are held by unit tests on real LAN clients
  (`d2client.Create`), and the loss mechanism by act 6f's rebuilt state (the
  review's own method: the file hidden for one session).
- **C3's App half is read, not tested.** A SEED refusal comes back through
  `App.ToCreateGame`, which now passes `load.Seed` to `FallBackToDawn`; the
  unit test drives the Game's half (`abandonLoad`), which shares the rule.
  Driving `ToCreateGame` needs the MPQs and a screen.
- **No notice a player sees** for a refusal, a restored sidecar or a kept
  `.preload.kept` (B5's). They are in `strigoi.log` and the harness's report.
- **No migration of a file a crash left as `.preload.kept`**: a person judges
  it; nothing offers it.
- **Nothing of B4b's or B5's**: a HUNTED file is left for B4b, not resumed;
  B5 must not land before B4b.
- **`playtest/save_resume_test.go`'s `worldFile` still requires version 1**
  and `d2save`'s own tests are untouched: R0.5's.

# B4b (a hunted night resumes) (branch `save-b4b`, 29 Sep 2026)

Josh's ruling is that the save "stops at that point then resumes at that
point". B4a made a quiet evening resume exactly; measured by its review,
though, almost every real save is HUNTED -- every one at dusk, night or dawn,
and every one after a single kill -- and B4a refused each (`HUNTED`) and left
it for this burst. B4b is the burst that makes the save work in practice. The
acceptance test the milestone was designed around now runs on a hunted night:
noticed packs walking in on their chases (three arrived while the table was
forced -- dogs, wolves and opportunists: eight hunters, eight watches, nine
chases in the file), a slain dog lying where he fell, a
wounded survivor of an ended fight, a deployed squad whose model is an NPC on
the map -- saved at T, stepped 120 minutes (in which the pack arrives and its
fight is played), relaunched and loaded; **the loaded game equals the original
at T and again at T+120** (`TestSaveResume` acts 4 and 6), the re-save is T's
file byte for byte but its stamp (act 5), and the death screen's "load last
save" resumes it in the same process (6b).

## What is rebuilt, and how

The order is the authoritative one ("The B4 load order", updated in place);
`LoadReport.Steps` records it as it runs, and a hunted night's reads `clock
natives entities validated health squads light torch corpses rising spawns
spawner notice pursuit combat bodies scene hero world_rng` (and, in a harness
build, `uuid dials`). Everything is in `d2game/d2gamescreen/load.go` unless
named.

| Step | Where | What B4b added |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `PrepareLoad` | **HUNTED is gone.** Every file B4a refused as a hunted night -- a pack, a watch, a chase, a monster's body, an entity the map does not build, a deployed squad -- passes step 1 (`TestAHuntedNightPassesStepOne`). Nothing of the old refusal was still impossible: each sub-case is resumed, and what cannot be rebuilt exactly is refused later with its own reason (`ENTITY`, `NATIVES`, below). With HUNTED gone, every refusal sets the file aside (rule 7); the exception `setsAside` carried for it is removed. |
| 3 | `Game.checkLoad` -> `Game.rebuildEntities`, at the END OF `CreateGame`, once the seed and the map are the file's and BEFORE step 4 (decision B4b-1) | **The villagers first** (`rekeyNatives`): each villager the map built -- the screen captured them by the entity before anything else could be placed (`natives`) -- is matched to the file's native by (`name_key`, `born`); refused `NATIVES` if the file has one the map does not build, two the map built share a key, or one is another kind or monstat than saved; a villager the file LACKS was taken off the map before the save (B3-6) and is taken off again. Each is then given his saved id IN PLACE (`MapEngine.RekeyEntity`, the re-key verb, below) and his saved motion (`RestoreMotion`, read back equal); and each must then answer to his saved id, on the map and to himself (a refusal otherwise). **Then every other entity**, in the file's order (`rebuildEntity`): its kind's record found first (`NewNPC`'s monstat; `NewCreature`'s bestiary entry and that entry's stand-in monstat, as `gameSpawner.Spawn` passes it) so a refusal leaves nothing waiting; the engine's `SetNextEntityID(id)`; built by its kind (`SetCreatureID` for a creature); `ID() == id` checked -- a constructor that SPENT the id instead of wearing it refuses the file (`ENTITY`); `RestoreMotion`, read back through `MotionSnapshot` and required JSON-equal (`restoreMotionExactly`); its `name_key` required the saved one; `AddEntity`. **Then** `PendingEntityID()` must be empty. Monsters, the risen and deployed squad models keep their walk, their pose, a corpse's Dead pose (rule 4 is his alone). |
| 4 | `Game.checkLoad` | Two new checks, and one fix. `checkSquadModels`: every deployed squad's model is an NPC on the resumed map (D3; `Squads.Validate` has no map to look at). `checkBodies`: the game knows no body yet, and every body is a monster on the map at 0..max. **BUG-65 (found by this burst's first hunted load, fixed):** the Resolver named no player before the controls bind -- `worldResolver.Quarry("player")` read `localPlayer`, set on the first frame, after step 4 -- so every file with a watch or a chase on him was refused `BLOCK`; `Game.thePlayer` is the client's own player until then. |
| 5 | `Game.resumeLoad` | Squads with their deployed models (`Squads.Restore` whole; D3); the spawn tables with their groups, notice with its watches, pursuit with its chases -- every member, watcher, target, hunter and quarry resolved through the save's Resolver type over the rebuilt map; **the bodies** (`restoreBodies`, after combat, before the scene): each id's health and maximum from the file -- the wounded survivor at his wounds, the slain at 0 -- and none adopted fresh in its place; refused over bodies already known, so nothing is restored twice. |
| 6 | the end of `resumeLoad` | Unchanged, and now with teeth: `RestoreRand` runs AFTER step 3's `NewNPC`/`NewCreature` drew from the world stream (trap 6), and in a harness build the uuid count after them, on both paths -- a fresh launch (act 4) and the death screen's in-process "load last save" (6b). Control H4 moves it before the entities: red on part `rng`. |

**Rule 4.** His walk does not continue: he stands at `hero.pos`, facing
`hero.facing` (`Player.StandAt`, B4a). Act 8 is the proof on a hunted night:
walked, saved mid-stride, loaded -- he stands where he was saved, and every
system, entity and digest part is the saved moment's but his walk: his
`target`, `waypoints`, `path_len`, `animation_mode` and the PNG hero's
`body_sheet` ("run" saved, "idle" resumed; measured -- the only field beyond
the plan's list that differed). `sameWorldExcept` fails on any other
difference, and on an exemption that is never needed.

## The re-key verb (`MapEngine.RekeyEntity`, `MapEntityFactory.Rekey`)

B2b noted that the verb the load order's "villager re-ids" needed did not
exist. It does now, in two halves on the seam's model:

- `MapEntityFactory.Rekey(e, id)` (`d2mapentity/entity_id.go`) sets the id on
  an NPC or a creature IN PLACE and counts it as given, so the seam never hands
  it out. Refused with `ErrEntityID`, changing nothing: `""` or `player`; the id
  waiting in the seam; an id already handed out (to another entity); any other
  kind. Re-keying an entity to the id it already has is allowed.
- `MapEngine.RekeyEntity(e, id)` (`d2mapengine/entity_id.go`) adds the map's
  checks -- the entity must be on this map under the id it answers to, and no
  other entity may hold the new id -- and moves the engine's own key. The
  entity stays the same pointer, so everything that holds it (the screen's
  natives, a talk, the headman's post) still does.
- **Why it matters, and where it is proved.** In a harness build the map
  draws the villagers' ids from the uuid stream reseeded from the file's seed
  (B4a), so they ARE the file's and the re-key is the identity: acts 4-6g could
  never see it. The shipped game's ids come from crypto/rand, new every
  launch. So act 6h gives T's file its villagers' ids as another launch would
  have made them (each native's id, everywhere in the file, made another; a
  suffix keeps the entity list's order) and loads it: each villager answers to
  the file's id, the map's own is gone, and the resumed game saves that file
  byte for byte but its stamp. Control H5 (the re-key taken out) is green at
  acts 4-6g and red at 6h -- the identity case cannot see it, which is why 6h
  exists.

## The refusals, as B4b leaves them

Each is a `*LoadRefusal`, logged and reported as before (B4a's table stands
for everything not named here). Every refusal sets the file aside and falls
back per rule 7 -- the one exception B4a had, HUNTED, is gone.

| Code | Where | When (B4b's cases) |
|---|---|---|
| ~~`HUNTED`~~ | ~~step 1~~ | **Removed.** B4a's limit: a pack, a watch, a chase, a monster's body, an entity the map does not build, a deployed squad. All are resumed now. |
| `NATIVES` | step 3 (`rekeyNatives`) | the file has a villager the map does not build; two villagers the map built share a (`name_key`, `born`); a villager is another kind or monstat than saved; ~~**he was saved while he held an action (the B4b review fixes, BUG-76)**~~ (no longer, BUG-87: resumed at its frame; a held action with no frame, or at a frame his art lacks, is refused); his saved id cannot be given (another entity on the map holds it, or the seam has handed it out); he does not answer to his saved id after the re-key; his motion is refused or comes back other than saved. **No longer refused:** a villager saved mid-walk or turned (B4a's; his motion is restored now), and a villager the map builds and the file lacks (taken off the map, B3-6). |
| `ENTITY` (new) | step 3 (`rebuildEntity`) | an `npc` whose monstat this build lacks; a `creature` whose bestiary entry this build lacks, or no bestiary; a kind no load rebuilds; `SetNextEntityID` refused (an id already on the map -- e.g. a villager's -- or one the seam has used); the construction failed; the entity came back under another id (the constructor spent the saved id); its motion refused or not read back equal; ~~its name not the saved `name_key` (an entry renamed since the save)~~ (**no longer refused**: a rename is noted, the B4b review fixes, BUG-80); ~~**saved while it held an action (the B4b review fixes, BUG-76)**~~ (no longer, BUG-87: resumed at its frame; a held action with no frame, or at a frame its art lacks, is refused); an id left waiting in the seam after the rebuild. |
| `BLOCK` | step 4 (and 5) | as B4a's, plus: a deployed squad's model not an NPC on the map (`checkSquadModels`); a body of no monster on the map, past its maximum, or restored over bodies already known (`checkBodies`, `restoreBodies`). |

**Playtest** `TestSaveResume` (`docs/harness.md` has the act list): act 1 is a
hunted night now -- the quiet evening's content kept, plus the slain dog left
lying, a second dog grazed and let go (the wounded survivor, by day: at night,
at half a tile's notice radius, a dog one tile away never sees him -- measured,

after T play the pack's walk in and its fight (quick-resolved: the dial
`quick_resolve_advantage` 0 at T, so he lives -- decision B4b-10). New acts:

and 6b): the deployed squad's model is on the resumed map under its saved id,
an npc of monstat `fallen1`, named by its squad -- the runtime proof B2b's
review asked for (`NewNPC` needs the MPQs; no unit test can build one). And
`awaitGame` (6b's wait) now re-reads the load report once the game is up and
waits until it is settled: the report read at the top of its loop could be a
frame old, and a hunted night's longer `CreateGame` made that window wide
enough to hit under a parallel run (`wt-b4b\saveresume-4-from.txt`: "resumed
false, steps [clock natives entities validated]").

**`TestSaveResumeNegatives`** (**the B4b review, its C6: the OMITTED half is
green by design -- Decode refuses every missing block before the load runs,
so no change to the load could turn one of those rows red; the EMPTIED half
is the test**; `playtest/save_resume_negatives_test.go`, B6's
omit sweep as far as B4b takes it; OPT-IN, it needs the kept evening,
`STRIGOI_SAVE_RESUME_FROM`): the untouched file first (the control of the
controls: it must resume S_T exactly), then every block B4b loads dropped two
ways -- OMITTED (the key gone, as `strigoi_save_game{omit}` writes it: Decode
refuses a missing block, `FILE`, and he begins at dawn -- divergent by the
file's own refusal) and EMPTIED (the block present, its content taken out; a
file `World.Check` still takes must RESUME, and must diverge -- the half with
teeth: a load that ignored the block would resume the saved moment) -- and two
entities' ids swapped. Each must refuse or diverge.

**The file controls** (`TestSaveResumeNegatives`, against the kept evening:

resumes S_T; then
