Every B4b block dropped either refuses the file or diverges -- and the
emptied half shows each is RESTORED, not just required.

## Decisions (the recommended option taken each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B4b-1, step 3 runs at the end of `CreateGame`, before step 4**, not on
  the first frame in `bindGameControls` as the load order's text said. Step 4
  (B4a put it at the end of `CreateGame`) validates spawns, notice and pursuit
  through a Resolver that must find every member, watcher and hunter on the
  map, so the entities must be there first; and a step-3 refusal then closes
  the game before its first frame like any step-4 refusal (nothing of a
  half-rebuilt map ever runs). The alternative -- validation moved to the
  first frame -- would have split step 4 again. The load order is updated in
  place.
- **B4b-2, a villager's motion is restored, as saved** (B4a refused a
  villager saved mid-walk, `NATIVES`). What is not carried is a patrol's place
  (path index, repetitions, the arrival that starts his next leg, his own
  rng): only the generated Act 1's villagers patrol. ~~A patrolling villager
  resumed there finishes his leg and stands.~~ **Corrected by the B4b review
  fixes (the review's C1; BUG-78): he does not.** The map's `SetPaths` leaves
  him `isDone`, and the restore gives his walk no arrival, so on his first
  animation loop `NPC.Advance` (`HasPaths && wait()`) calls `NextPath` and
  he abandons the restored leg for `path[1]`. **A `-classic` resume of a
  patrolling villager is not exact.** The authored village's villagers
  stand, so the shipped game is not touched. Making it exact means carrying
  the patrol's place and his rng -- a world-file shape change -- which is not
  the trivial fix the brief allowed, so the code is unchanged and the gap is
  recorded (BUG-78, KNOWN).
- **B4b-3, a villager the map builds and the file lacks is taken off the
  map** (B3-6's reading), not refused.
- **B4b-4, every restored motion is read back** (`restoreMotionExactly`) and
  required equal to the file's: a sheet or composite that cannot hold the
  saved mode is a monster that would not take the saved next step. (It keeps
  the two `MotionSnapshot` rows live: `checkNatives` -- their load-side caller
  -- is gone.)
- ~~**B4b-5, a rebuilt entity must bear its saved `name_key`**: a bestiary entry
  renamed since the save refuses the file (`ENTITY`) rather than resume a
  pack under other names.~~ **Overturned by the B4b review fixes (the
  review's C3; BUG-80):** the entity is matched on its kind and its record
  -- the bestiary entry by `creature_id`, the monstat by its key -- and its
  name is a label. A renamed entry resumes under the new name, with a line in
  the load report's `notes` and the log. B4b-5 would have thrown away every
  save with a wolf in it for a label's rename.
- **B4b-6, a creature whose stand-in monstat this build lacks is built with
  none**: the stand-in only decides what the construction draws from the world
  stream, which step 6 restores after. An NPC whose monstat is missing is
  refused (`NewNPC` needs it). The live spawner refuses to place a creature
  without its stand-in, so a real save never holds one.
- **B4b-7, the bodies are restored after combat, before the scene**, and
  refused over bodies already known.
- **B4b-8, a re-key collision is refused**, not resolved by moving every
  villager aside first: a harness load re-keys each villager to his own id,
  and a shipped load's crypto/rand ids never collide with a file's -- a
  collision is a file two villagers claim one id in.
- **B4b-9, the acceptance test's evening IS the hunted night now**: one
  evening, not two (a second would cost about two minutes a run). The quiet
  evening's own assertions (no group, no watch...) went with it; B4a's unit
  test still resumes a quiet evening, and every B4a act (4-6g) runs on the
  hunted file.
- ~~**B4b-10, the fight after T is quick-resolved** (`quick_resolve_advantage`
  0, a dial set at T and re-set on the relaunch): the pack walks in, the fight
  opens and is finished before its first blow. He lives through the two
  hours -- a death there would end the evening's later acts -- and the dice
  the fight would have spent are not what the act is about. The chase, the
  notice, the walk in and the fight's opening and end all happen, on both
  sides of the resume, the same.~~ **Overturned by the B4b review fixes (the
  review's B2; BUG-77):** with it no roll, no blow on a rebuilt monster, no
  restored speed paced and no restored pack profile read was ever compared
  after T. The fight after T is now fought blow by blow -- the quick resolve
  off, every blow a graze both ways, dials set at T -- so he still lives:
  two fights, 13 rounds, 45 blows, ending `enemies_routed`, him at 214 of
  237 on the evening that was measured.
- **B4b-11, `TestSaveResumeNegatives` is OPT-IN** (it needs a kept evening);
  whether it joins the suite with acts 1-3 of its own is B6's.
- **B4b-12, the day's slain dog stays on the map** (a corpse entity, his body
  at 0), and a second, wounded, is let go: B4a took the first off the map
  because a monster on the map was B4b's.
- **B4b-13, no change to the world file's shape**, and so no version bump.
  Every field B4b reads was written by B3.

## Found on the way

- **BUG-65 -- the step-4 Resolver named no player** (above; `docs/bugs.md`).
  Latent in B4a's code (a quiet evening has no watch on him), found by the
  first hunted load, fixed, with a unit and a playtest control.
- **`awaitGame` read a load report a frame old** (above). The test's own
  instrument, not the game.
- **Measured for act 1 (`wt-b4b\measure-1.txt`, `measure-2.txt`, a throwaway
  scaffold, deleted):** `strigoi_watch` makes a watch, not an awareness -- a
  watcher 13 tiles off at half a tile's notice radius never notices; a dog one
  tile away at night does not either (he is unlit), so the survivor's fight is
  by day; the downed dead of the first night, left unstaked, stand again the
  next night (and fought him on his way to the post); `max_groups` counts the
  risen's groups among the live ones; and `pursuit.arrive_within` does not stop
  a walk -- the route ends beside the quarry whatever it says -- so a pack
  that notices him WILL reach him, and the fight after T is the dial's to end
  (B4b-10), not the chase's to prevent.

## What B4b did NOT do (B5's, B6's)

- **No player-facing notice** of a refusal (B5's). The log and the harness's
  load report carry every one.
- **The menu save, the close hook, the dawn autosave: B5's -- and B5 may land
  now.** B4a-R3's sequencing rule ("B5 must not land before B4b": the dawn
  autosave is itself hunted) is met.
- **The whole omit sweep is B6's**: `TestSaveResumeNegatives` sweeps the
  blocks B4b loads (entities, bodies, spawns, spawner, notice, pursuit,
  squads) and is opt-in; B4a's blocks are swept by B4a's source controls, not
  by it.
- **A risen man walking at T is not in the acceptance test.** The first
  night's risen are staked by day as in B4a; one left to stand again the
  second night noticed and fought him at unpredictable moments (measured). A
  risen man is a bestiary creature like the pack's (the `strigoi` entry),
  rebuilt by the same path, so `ENTITY`'s checks and the pack's acts cover the
  code; the corpses and rising blocks carry the risen's history as in B4a.
  **The B4b review fixes added a unit test of one walking at T
  (`TestARisenManWalkingAtTResumes`, the review's C7).**
- **An NPC's patrol place** is not carried (B4b-2; and see its correction);
  nor a held action's frame (~~it restarts, B2b~~ ~~**the B4b review fixes:
  no held action is saved or loaded**, BUG-76~~ **carried since BUG-87**).
- **No change to the world file** (B4b-13).

## Register

B4b's load makes the rows it calls go live, each moved to WIRE with what its
row said before recorded in its reason: `MapEngine.SetNextEntityID`,
`MapEntityFactory.PendingEntityID`, `Creature.RestoreMotion`,
`NPC.RestoreMotion` (all "defer, M4.6 B4b, dead" until now). Rewritten, still
wire: `Squads.Restore` (deployed squads restored, not refused),
`Spawns/Notice/Pursuit.Restore` (groups, watches, chases), the two
`MotionSnapshot`s (their load-side caller is the read-back now, `checkNatives`
being gone), `PrepareLoad` (no HUNTED), `Game.checkLoad` (steps 3 and 4). New,
wire: `MapEngine.RekeyEntity`, `MapEntityFactory.Rekey`,
`Game.rebuildEntities`, `Game.rekeyNatives`, `Game.rebuildEntity`,
`Game.restoreBodies`, `Game.checkBodies`, `Game.checkSquadModels`. Measured
alone first (`reach-targeted-1.txt`: fourteen `-only` runs, every entry where
the register says) and in the full reach gate (below).
`Spawns/Notice/Pursuit.CheckSnapshot` stay deferred to B5, as B4a-16 said:
no load calls them.

## For the plan (the main session's to write into the build plan)

- **Section 5, B4b's row: DONE** -- every entity rebuilt with its saved id
  (the villagers re-keyed in place), the bodies, groups, watches, chases and
  deployed squads restored, HUNTED gone, act 8, and the uuid count and world
  RNG after the entities on both paths. `TestSaveResume` acts 1-8 on a hunted
  night; the brief's controls and more.
- **The B4 load order, step 3:** it runs at the end of `CreateGame`, before
  step 4, not in `bindGameControls` (B4b-1). Step 5 gains the bodies after
  combat.
- **Rule 7:** every refusal sets the world file aside -- the HUNTED exception
  (B4a-R3) is gone with the refusal.
- **Sequencing:** B4a-R3's "B5 must not land before B4b" is met. **B5 may
  land.**
- **Section 4, act 8:** the comparison leaves out his walk alone -- target,
  waypoints, path_len, and the walk's animation (`animation_mode`, and the PNG
  hero's `body_sheet`); everything else is compared and matched.
- **Section 4, act 9 / B6:** `TestSaveResumeNegatives` sweeps B4b's blocks
  both ways (omitted: refused; emptied: resumed and divergent) and is opt-in;
  B6 decides whether it joins the suite and extends it to every block.
- **Section 1's Villagers row** ("D: the map rebuilds them, and the ids are
  re-applied"): as built -- re-keyed IN PLACE by `MapEngine.RekeyEntity`, and
  their motion restored.

# B4b review fixes (29 Sep 2026, branch `save-b4b`)

The independent review of B4b (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-rev-b4b\review.md`,
on `5c705ce2`) found no A, two B and eight C findings; the merge scout
(`merge-scout`, `5dceaf32`) found one false mismatch that came in with B4a,
BUG-86. Every one is fixed below, or corrected where it was a claim, each
with a negative control seen red where the fix is code. The recommended
option was taken each time; the choices are listed under "The decisions".
Logs: `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b4b-fix\`. Bug rows: BUG-75..BUG-84 and
BUG-86 (`docs/bugs.md`).

## Findings, fixes, tests and controls

### B1 -- a death still playing at the save played again after the load (BUG-75, BUG-76)

**What the review found.** A monster slain while it walked in -- the quick
resolve's last quarter, still on its chase -- walked on through its whole
death: `Creature.Advance` (and `NPC.Advance`) kept calling `Step` while the
death was held, and only the end of the death stopped the walk. Its corpse
came to rest up to about a tile further along its route than where
`Combat.fallCorpse` recorded the fall. And a save made while a death played
resumed it from its first frame (`RestoreMotion` restarts a held action),
so the resumed death ended later than the saved one: the review's real-game
probe had two opportunists `dead` sixty frames after T' in the saved game and
still `death` in the resumed one. B2b's Motion note ("then lies as a corpse,
as it would have") was untrue; it is corrected in place.

**The fix, in two parts, both as recommended.**

1. **BUG-75: a death ends the walk where it begins.** `mapEntity.halt`
   (`map_entity.go`) clears the route, puts the target where the entity
   stands, zeroes its velocity and drops the arrival -- WITHOUT turning it
   (`StopMoving` would: it points the directioner at the entity's own
   position). `Creature.StartAction` and `NPC.StartAction` call it when the
   action is a death, and both `Advance`s no longer step a body playing its
   death (`dying()`), so even a route handed to it then is not walked. The
   corpse lies where the resolver recorded the fall, and turns when the death
   ends, as it always has. No die, dial or roll was changed for it, and no
   existing assertion (the unit and playtest suites below are green without
   one being edited for it). What does change in play is where a corpse lies
   -- and so, for a packmate's tactical walk, which tile it occupies -- now
   where the fall was recorded rather than up to a tile on.
2. **BUG-76: no held action is saved or loaded.** Of the two options the brief
   allowed, **the refusal was chosen: it is exact and simpler.** Restoring a
   held action at its saved frame would carry the frame AND the time into it
   (`Animation` has no accessor for the latter, and an NPC's composite keeps
   its own per mode) -- a new world-file field and new engine accessors, for a
   moment that lasts a second. So `Game.heldAction` (`save.go`) refuses the
   save, `FIGHTING`, while any monster or villager holds an action -- a swing,
   a blow taken, a death -- naming it ("... is still playing its death: a save
   carries no animation's frame"), as his own swing is refused; and
   `heldInFile` (`load.go`) refuses a file that holds one anyway -- `ENTITY`
   for a monster, `NATIVES` for a villager -- before anything is rebuilt: no
   save of this build writes one, and resumed it would restart.
   `RestoreMotion` still restores a held action (its unit tests stand); the
   game no longer asks it to.

**Tests.** The review's unit probe is `TestADeathIsNeitherWalkedNorSavedHalfPlayed`
(`load_b4b_review_test.go`): the walking wolf slain 0, 3 and 8 frames before
the save; his walk ends where his death begins (no target ahead, no route, no
velocity) and he does not move; the save is refused `FIGHTING`, naming him and
his death, touching no file; it is made the frame his corpse lies (17-25
frames later); and the resumed game is the saved one at once and 100 frames
on, the corpse where the death began in both. `TestAFileHoldingAnActionIsRefused`:
a monster's death, a monster's bite and a villager's blow in the file, each
refused with its code. `d2mapentity`: `TestADeathEndsTheWalkWhereItBegins`
(a dog mid-walk: no route, target or velocity from the death's first frame,
facing the way it was going, not moved by a route handed to it while it dies,
the corpse where it fell) and `TestOnlyADeathEndsTheWalk` (a bite and a blow
keep the route). The review's real-game probe is **act 6i** of
`TestSaveResume`: T resumed with B4b's quick-resolving dials; the quick resolve
fired 1200 frames in and slew two; each lies exactly where his fall was
recorded, at the first look and at the save; the save was refused 93 times
(69 of them naming a death still playing) and made 93 frames later, no slain
member holding an action; and that moment resumed exactly and ran on sixty
frames the same (S_R0 = S_T', S_R = S_U'). And, because the playtest's packs
are creatures, **`TestASlainWalkerLiesWhereHeFell`** (`corpse_test.go`) does
it for an NPC -- the kind only a playtest can build: a `fallen1` nine tiles
off notices him and walks in, the fight opens at six tiles (the adjacency
dial, also a blow's reach) and his first blow, a forced crit, kills it 5.65
tiles off; seen dying 24 times, it never moves, and the corpse is where the
fall was recorded. (A quick resolve would be the review's own case, but it
takes only a pack's members, and a spawned dog is in no pack.)

**Controls.** Unit: R1 (the creature's halt taken out) red in both unit
tests; R2 (`Advance`'s guard taken out) red: the route handed to a dying dog
is walked; R3 (the save's refusal taken out) red: saved half-played; R4 (the
load's refusal taken out) red: resumed. Playtest: H15 (the creature's halt
and guard taken out) red at act 6, before act 6i was reached: a feral dog slain in the graze fight after T came to rest at 30.4,24.4 where the saved game has him at 29.85,24.45 (`nc\H15-creature-walks-dying.txt`); H16 (the save's refusal taken out)
red at act 6i: the save was made on its first try, refused not once (`nc\H16-save-mid-death.txt`; its first run went red for a reason of the test's own, fixed and recorded below under "Found on the way": `nc\H16-save-mid-death.first-run.txt`); H17 (the NPC's halt and guard taken out) red: the dog was off the recorded fall at the first look (`nc\H17-npc-walks-dying.txt`).

**No dice, no assertion.** Stopping the walk changed no corpse position an
existing test asserts: none asserted one for a monster slain mid-walk. The
unit suites, `TestSaveResume` and the full playtest suite (below) are green
with no assertion edited for BUG-75 (the one assertion changed in this burst is
C3's, below, and it is that finding's own).

### B2 -- after T, no fight in the acceptance test landed a blow (BUG-77)

**Found.** `huntedDials` set `quick_resolve_advantage` 0 at T (decision
B4b-10), so every fight after T against a mundane pack ended before its
first round: no combat roll, no swing or hit on a rebuilt monster, no
`borrowPace` on a restored speed, no restored pack profile read, no rebuilt
member surviving a blow was ever compared across the resume.

**Measured first** (`measure-1.txt`, a throwaway scaffold on `evening-2`,
kept as `zz_b4bfix_measure_test.go.keep`): T resumed with the quick resolve
off (2.0: above 1 it can never fire) and `forced_band` `graze`, and the two
hours stepped in ten-minute chunks, twice in one process. After T: two
fights, 13 rounds, 45 blows, ending in routs (`enemies_routed`); he goes from
237 to 214 and lives; **the two hours take 9 s of wall time**; and the two
resumes agree to the digest (`fe5257188a4d`). Stable, and far under the
brief's 60 s -- so, as the brief said, **a normal act, not opt-in**: the
dials at T are now the graze fight (`huntedDials`; B4b's are kept as
`quickResolvedDials`, for act 6i and for evenings kept before today), act 3
requires blows and rounds after T and no quick resolve, and act 6's S_R = S_U
compares the fight. The kept evening now records the dials it was made with
(`dials.json`); `keptEvening` falls back to `quickResolvedDials` for one that
names none (`evening-2`). The first whole run with it (`saveresume-2-whole.txt`):
"after T, 13 rounds and 45 blows, none quick-resolved", acts 4-6 green.

**Control.** H19 (the dials at T put back to quick-resolve, a whole run)
red at act 3: "0 blows, rounds 6 -> 14, quick_resolved 0 -> 4" (`nc\H19-quick-resolved-after-T.txt`).

### C1 -- B4b-2's account of a patrolling villager was wrong (BUG-78)

Corrected in place (decision B4b-2, `rekeyNatives`' comment): a `-classic`
villager restored mid-leg does not "finish his leg and stand" -- `SetPaths`
left him `isDone` and the restore gives the walk no arrival, so on his first
animation loop `Advance` calls `NextPath` and he walks to `path[1]`. **A
`-classic` resume of a patrolling villager is not exact.** Not fixed in code:
exactness needs the patrol's place (path index, repetitions, `isDone`) and his
own rng in the file -- a shape change, not the trivial fix the brief allowed.
The authored village's villagers stand, so the shipped game is not touched.
Read only, as the review did; no `-classic` patrol was run.

### C2 -- a villager the file lacks was removed with no trace (BUG-79)

`rekeyNatives` now, as before, takes him off the map (decision B4b-3 kept),
and also: logs it (`LOAD the villager ... is not in the file ... taken off it
again`, a warning), adds it to the load report's new `notes`, and deletes him
from the screen's natives. The scene provider reports only the natives that
are on the map (`scene.natives`), so the game that took him off and the game
resumed from its save agree that he is gone (without it, the resumed game's
`scene` would have differed from the saved one's once he left the natives).
Test: `TestAVillagerTheFileLacksIsNotedAndForgotten`. Controls: R5 (no note),
R6 (kept in the natives), R7 (the scene reporting a native off the map) --
each red.

### C3 -- a bestiary label rename refused every affected save (BUG-80)

**Recommended option taken: match on `creature_id` (and the kind).** The file
has it: a creature is rebuilt from the entry `creature_id` names and an NPC
from its monstat key (`entityBuilder`), so the name was never the key.
`rebuildEntity` no longer refuses a name other than the saved `name_key`; it
notes it (report and log: "... was saved named "Wolf" and this build names it
"Grey wolf": resumed under the new name"). Decision B4b-5 is overturned in
place. **The one existing assertion changed in this burst:** the case "a name
the entry does not give" left `TestARebuildThatCannotBeExactIsRefused` (it
resumes now), and `TestARenamedEntryResumesUnderItsNewName` holds the new
rule. Controls: R8 (the refusal put back) and R9 (no note), red.

**The second hazard -- an art pass adds a death sheet a held death had fallen
back from -- cannot arise any more:** no held action is saved (BUG-76).
**BUG-87 brings it back, as a refusal:** a held action is saved again, so a
creature saved holding a death its art had no sheet for (posed idle) is
restored, after an art pass adds the sheet, in the death pose -- its mode
reads back other than saved, or its frame past the new sheet's count, and the
file is refused `ENTITY` (set aside, he begins at dawn), never resumed
inexactly. **A
known hazard remains**, recorded here: an art pass that REMOVES a sheet a
saved mode used (a creature saved walking, the walk sheet gone) makes the
restored mode read back as idle, and the file is refused `ENTITY`. Art
integration adds sheets; none has been removed.

### C4 -- `sameWorldExcept` passed a hash with nothing to explain it (BUG-81)

Act 8's comparison now fails when a system's hash differs and no field of
its report does (naming the system), and when the systems or entities part
differs with no system or entity line to show it. Its control is a test of
the comparison itself, with no game (`TestSameWorldExceptFailsAnUnexplainedHash`,
`save_resume_test.go`): an exempt difference alone passes; a system hash
differing with an equal report fails naming it; an entities part differing
with equal lines fails. Control H20 (the new checks reverted) red:
`nc-H20-sameworldexcept.txt`. `sameWorldExcept` takes a `testing.TB` for it.

### C5 -- the resume digest's blind spots (BUG-82)

Added to every NPC's and creature's harness state, so to the digest's
`entities` part that a resume compares: `velocity` (world tiles per step; on
the player too), `held` (the action held until it plays through, "" when
none; an NPC's `action` is its patrol's) and `corpse`. All three are cheap
and exact across a relaunch (the file keeps them bit for bit). **A zero
velocity is reported as zero whatever its sign:** the first whole run with the
field was red at act 4 on his velocity alone, `[0,-0]` at T (a walk's end
scales it to length 0, leaving -0) against `[0,0]` resumed standing
(`saveresume-1-whole.txt`); the two are one standstill. Act 8 exempts his
velocity with the rest of his walk (rule 4). Test:
`TestHarnessStateReportsThePoseAndTheVelocity`. Unit controls R10 (a
creature's `held` dropped), R11 (`velocity` dropped), R11b (the sign kept),
R12 (an NPC's `corpse` dropped): red. Playtest controls, each with the load's
read-back made blind to the field (the read-back sees every Motion field at
R0, so a field the digest adds is what would see a loss the read-back did
not): H14 (an NPC's corpse flag not restored) red at act 4 on the slain dog's `corpse`, true saved and false resumed (`nc\H14-corpse.txt`) -- and H14b, the same
break with every field BUG-82 added unreported and BUG-86's split undone,
against `evening-2` (kept before either, so both sides lack them): act 4 PASS -- the blind spot, as it was -- and red at act 5, where the re-saved file carries the flag the digest did not (`nc\H14b-corpse-before.txt`).

**Measured on the way: in the harness every velocity reads zero.** The
harness observes a paused game, and paused, "frames advance with zero
deltas" (`d2app/harness_time.go`): `Step` then scales every velocity to
length 0. At T all fifteen
entities of `evening-3` -- eight hunters walking in -- have velocity `[0,0]`
in the file and in the digest. So the velocity field has teeth in unit tests
and in a live game, not in a scripted one; the playtest control built on it
(H13, velocity not restored) could not go red at act 4 and was dropped from
the table (it went red at act 5 on the SIGN of a zero in the re-saved file,
the file being bit-exact where the digest is not -- `nc\H13-velocity.txt`).
The corpse and held flags carry the review's real cases (a corpse that is no
corpse; a death still held).

**Not added:**

- **An NPC's gear: recorded as a known cosmetic gap, not carried.** The
  deployed model's equipment is re-rolled from the world stream on the
  rebuild (`factory.go` `NewNPC`, `selectEquip`). Carrying it would be a new
  field on the strict world file (`DisallowUnknownFields`, the missing-field
  rule) and new accessors on the composite and the NPC -- a shape change for
  a placeholder model (`fallen1`). Villagers are built by the map from the
  file's seed in the same order, so theirs are the saved ones; packs and the
  risen are creatures, one sheet, no gear. So the one NPC whose gear can
  change on a load is a deployed squad's model. It is left out of the digest
  on purpose: in it, act 4 would go red on every hunted night with a squad.
- **An NPC's per-entity rng**: not carried and not reported. It is drawn only
  by a patrol (`NPC.next`), so it matters exactly where C1 does, and goes with
  it.

### C6 -- the omit half of `TestSaveResumeNegatives` is green by design (BUG-83)

Said plainly now in the test's comment and in B4b's notes above: `Decode`
refuses every missing block before the load runs, so no change to the load
could turn an OMITTED row red; the EMPTIED half is the test. (The test stays
opt-in; B6 decides.) **H12's hole for an NPC villager: no unit control is
feasible.** `rekeyNatives` refuses an NPC villager with no monstat (it
checks the monstat key), only `NewNPC` sets one, and `NewNPC` needs the MPQs'
composite; a bare `&d2mapentity.NPC{}` cannot even `Advance` (it calls the nil
composite). What covers it: the load's villager path is the same
`restoreMotionExactly` for both kinds (U2), and `NPC.RestoreMotion` itself is
unit-tested in `d2mapentity` (`TestNPCMotionRoundTripsAndStepsInStep`,
`TestNPCMotionCarriesItsPose`).

### C7 -- no risen man walked at T

Now one does, in a unit test (`TestARisenManWalkingAtTResumes`): a creature
of the bestiary's `strigoi` entry in a group of the risen row, watched and
chasing, walking in at T; resumed under his saved id, mid-walk as saved,
S_R0 = S_T and 60 frames on S_R = S_U, and he walked on. Deterministic in the
unit game. (The unit spawner cannot stand one up itself -- it has no stand-in
monstat -- so he is built as `b4bHunt` builds the pack, his group put on the
tables through `Restore`.) Not in the playtest: the act's evening would need
a risen man raised at a chosen moment, and the rising rolls on its own stream
at band ticks; the same rebuild path is under the pack's acts. Control R14
(the risen man rebuilt without his route) red.

### C8 -- `nc-summary.txt` quoted the wrong line for H5 and H5b (BUG-84)

Corrected in `wt-b4b\nc-summary.txt` (the two lines now quote act 6h's
failure, with the detail file's line, and a note at the end says what
changed; the original is kept as `wt-b4b-fix\nc-summary-b4b-before-correction.txt`).
The verdicts were right; `nc_run.sh` took the first act line with a colon,
which for H5/H5b was act 4's PASS line. This burst's `nc_run.sh` takes the
last test line before `--- FAIL` (the `t.Fatalf`).

### BUG-86 (the merge scout's) -- a save in a new game's first second

A new game first reads its region a second into play (`ticksSinceLevelCheck`);
the load reads it at once. The village provider put `sound_env` and `music` in
the digest's world part, so a save in the first second resumed with both set
where the saved game had neither. **Recommended fix taken:** the village
provider implements `d2harness.Digester` and reports both in the `process`
part, as BUG-63 did for the ui's presentation (`villageProvider.HarnessDigest`,
`talk.go`); everything else stays in the world part. Tests: unit
`TestTheVillagesSoundIsThisProcesssPresentation`; playtest
**`TestSaveResumeInTheFirstSecond`** (`save_resume_test.go`): a new game
stepped half a second, the premise asserted (no song, no sound environment
yet), saved, resumed in process, S_R0 = S_T, and the resumed game has the song
(the premise's other half). Controls: R13 (unit) red; H18 (the provider's
split taken out) red: S_R0 != S_T (`nc\H18-village-sound-in-world.txt`). Fixed here, on `save-b4b`; the scout's row on
`merge-scout` is the same number.

## Found on the way

  form of `keptEvening` unmarshalled `dials.json` into a slice that shared
  `quickResolvedDials`' array, so on the kept-evening path act 6i ran with the
  graze dials and the pack was never quick-resolved (control H16's first run,
  red for that reason: `nc\H16-save-mid-death.first-run.txt`, and
  `saveresume-3-from.txt`, whose act 6i logged the dials). Fixed: the dials are
  read into a slice of their own (`saveresume-4-from.txt`: green, and act 6i
  logs the refusals -- 69 on a death still playing, 24 "a fight is running").
- **A walk's end leaves a -0 in its velocity** (BUG-82 above): the first
  whole run was red at act 4 on the player's `[0,-0]` against `[0,0]`.
- **In a scripted run every velocity reads 0** (BUG-82 above).
- **`TestASlainWalkerLiesWhereHeFell` took three forms.** A dog due east
  never noticed him -- unlit (the game begins before dawn), and, once his
  torch was lit, still not, the line to him not clear: it now lights his
  torch and places the dog where `strigoi_find_path` says the line is
  clear. Then a dog noticed, fought and died -- never quick-resolved, because
  a quick resolve takes only a pack's members and a spawned dog is in none:
  it now opens the fight at six tiles and kills with his first blow
  (`walker-1.txt`, `saveresume-1/2-whole.txt`; green in `walker-2.txt`).

## The decisions (the recommended option each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B4b-R1, a held action is refused, not carried** (B1's second part) --
  **overturned by BUG-87 (option (a), 29 Sep 2026): it is carried at its
  frame, and both refusals are gone**: the
  save refuses `FIGHTING` while any monster or villager holds one, and the
  load refuses a file with one. Exact and simpler than the frame, and no
  shape change. **What it costs:** a save in the second after a fight's last
  blow is refused and must be retried -- B5's menu save and dawn autosave must
  retry (the dawn autosave the next frame; the menu can say "a moment"). **On
  `raid-r1`** the clock fights he is not in animate their members through the
  same `Animate`, so a save during one is refused for the second after each of
  their blows; the scout's `TestSaveResumeWithAFightHeIsNotIn` saves at T
  mid-clock-fight and may need to retry as the reviewer's probe did.
- **B4b-R2, a death ends the walk and does not turn the monster** (B1's first
  part): `halt`, not `StopMoving`; the corpse turns when the death ends, as
  before.
- **B4b-R3, the fight after T is fought with grazes, in the normal run**
  (B2): measured stable and free; B4b-10 is overturned.
- **B4b-R4, a name is a label** (C3): B4b-5 is overturned.
- **B4b-R5, C1 is recorded, not fixed**: exactness needs a shape change.
- **B4b-R6, gear is a known cosmetic gap** (C5): not carried, not in the
  digest.
- **B4b-R7, the scene reports only the natives on the map** (C2).
- **B4b-R8, a standstill's velocity is reported unsigned** (C5).

# The raid's R0.5: the version follows `Version` (branch `raid-r1`, 29 Sep 2026)

The raid milestone ("the village at night") carries the world file's one
version bump in its R1 (the combat block's clock). Its S0 found that a bump
was not one line: `Decode` compared the literal `"1"`, the refusal's message
named 1, and tests hard-coded it. R0.5 makes every one of them follow
`d2save.Version`, so the bump is the constant, the golden file and its hash.
**R0.5 changes no shape and bumps nothing: `Version` is still 1 after it.**

**The code.**
- `d2save.Decode` compares against `strconv.Itoa(Version)`, not `"1"`.
- `ErrWorldVersion`'s message names `Version` (`fmt.Errorf(... %d ...)`).
- `WriteWorld` already used `strconv.Itoa(Version)` (B3); unchanged.
- Comments that said "version 1" where they meant this build's version now
  say so (`d2save/world.go`, `write.go`; `d2gamescreen/load.go`'s refusal
  codes and step 1). The history lines ("a bad version-1 file used to be
  copied over the good .bak") are left as history.

**The tests: every literal that stood for a version, derived** (each edit
replaces a literal with the constant it stands for; no expectation changed):
- `world_test.go`, `TestAVersionOtherThanOneIsRefused`: this build's version
  is `cur = strconv.Itoa(Version)`, the newer build's `Version+1`; the quoted
  and float forms are built from `cur`. The name is kept (the notes and the
  raid brief find it by it).
- `world_test.go`, `TestCheckRefusesWhatNoLoadCouldRestore`: the case
  "version 2 in a struct" is now "the next version in a struct",
  `w.Version = Version + 1`.
- `write_test.go` (B3's and B4a's; **not in S0's list**, found by the scratch
  bump below): `b3Cur`/`b3Next` replace every `"version": 1`/`2` and
  `.v1.unread`/`.v2.unread` that stood for this build's or a newer build's
  file, in `TestWriteWorldSetsAsideWhatItCannotRead`,
  `TestWriteWorldNeverKeepsABadFileAsTheBak`,
  `TestWriteWorldSetsAsideAFileHeldOpen` and
  `TestSetAsideKeepsWhatTheLoadRefused`. At version 2 unconverted, the last
  goes red and the other three stay green **for the wrong reason** (a
  "newer" file of version 2 would be this build's own, torn; a "bad
  current" file of version 1 would be an older one). `TestWorldPath`'s
  `UnreadPath("w", "2")` is a pure naming case and stays.
- `playtest/save_resume_test.go`'s `worldFile`: `version` must be
  `d2save.Version` (the package now imports `d2save`).
- `load_test.go` and `load_review_test.go` were derived by B4a's review
  already. The `{"version": 1, ...}` literals left in d2save and
  d2gamescreen tests are the **sidecar's** version (`d2items.SidecarVersion`),
  not the world file's.

**The golden file and the pin are "the current version".** They already
were in code (`world-v<Version>.json`, `b3ShapeHashes[Version]`); the header
comment said "the hash for version 1" and now says the current `Version`. A
bump leaves each older golden file committed, as what an older build wrote.
**New test, `TestAnOlderVersionsFileIsRefusedAndSetAside`** (rule 7, no
migration): every committed `world-v<N>.json` with N below `Version`, and the
current golden file with its version set one lower (so the test always has a
file to refuse), must be refused by `Decode` on its version (a
`*VersionError` naming N, `ReasonVersion`, `VersionOf` = N), set aside by the
load's `SetAside` as `.vN.unread` byte for byte, and set aside again by the
save's `WriteWorld` as `.vN.unread.1`, never kept as the `.bak`. It also
holds that no golden file is of a version above `Version` and that every
committed version keeps its recorded hash.

**Controls, seen red** (logs in `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-raid-r1\`):
- **The scratch bump** (`r05-control-bump2.txt`): `Version = 2`, the golden
  file regenerated with `-update-golden`, its hash recorded, never
  committed. d2save 22/0, d2gamescreen 87/0, d2hero 15/0: every converted
  test green at version 2, and the new test refused `world-v1.json` as
  version 1 and set it aside as `.v1.unread`. (The first run of the same
  bump, before `write_test.go` was converted, had
  `TestSetAsideKeepsWhatTheLoadRefused` red: how that file was found.)
- **`Decode`'s literal `"1"` put back, at the scratch bump**
  (`r05-control-decode-literal.txt`): d2save 13 red, d2gamescreen 17 red.
- **One test literal put back each, at the scratch bump**
  (`r05-control-test-literals.txt`): `TestAVersionOtherThanOneIsRefused`
  (`"version": 1`) and `TestSetAsideKeepsWhatTheLoadRefused` (`.v1.unread`)
  red.
- The playtest's literal is held by R1's real bump: `TestSaveResume` green
  at version 2 (the spike, without R0.5, had it red at "7a: version 2").

**At version 1, after R0.5** (`r05-units.txt`): d2save 22/0 (21 + the new
test), d2gamescreen 87/0, d2hero 15/0.

# The raid's R1: the combat block's clock, and version 2 (branch `raid-r1`, 29 Sep 2026)

The raid milestone's R1 built the fights he is not in (one resolver, two
drivers: `d2core/d2world/combat_clock.go`) and, with them, **the world file's
one bump for the milestone: `d2save.Version` is 2.** The shape changes, in
full (for the merge with B4b, which recomputes the golden file and the hash
once if B4b changed a shape too):

| Where | Change |
|---|---|
| `combat.clock` (new object, `d2world.CombatClockSnapshot`) | `next_id` (the clock's own `c:<n>` sequence), `rng` (`{seed, draws}`, seed a string, on the `combat-clock` stream), `started`, `ended`, `rounds`, `declines`, `actions`, `joined`, `quick_resolved`, `last_quick_advantage`, `ended_reason`, `ended_quarry_dead`, `ended_enemies_dead`, `ended_player_dead`, `ended_disengaged`, `ended_routed`, `ended_dawn`, `xp_suppressed`, `released`, `reentrant_reads`, `actions_round`, `last_actions` (omitempty; `CombatActionSnapshot` rows as his), `live` (omitempty) |
| `combat.clock.live[]` (new, `d2world.CombatClockFightSnapshot`) | `id`, `quarry` (an entity id, never `"player"`), `enemies`, `enemy_order`, `dead` / `routed` / `broke` (each omitempty, sorted), `round`, `since_turn`, `initiator`, `surprised`, `surprise_why` |
| `rng.combat_clock` (new, `d2save.RNG.CombatClock`) | the copy of `combat.clock.rng`; `World.Check` holds it equal to the block (`rng-copy`) and on the game's `combat-clock` seed (`rng-stream`) |
| `version` | 2; the golden file is `testdata/world-v2.json` (regenerated with `-update-golden`), the hash `fa82243b...` recorded as `b3ShapeHashes[2]`; `world-v1.json` stays committed, and `TestAnOlderVersionsFileIsRefusedAndSetAside` holds it refused as version 1 and set aside as `.v1.unread` |

Nothing else in the file changed shape. `d2rand.Derived` gained
`combat-clock` (its fourth stream; `SeedFor` names it, so `StreamState.Check`
can check it), derived in the combat model from the one seed `NewCombat` is
handed (`d2rand.Rederive`), so no construction site changed.

**The raid's Q5, default (a): a fight he is not in never stops a save; it is
saved with the world.** Decided in `Combat.Snapshot` alone, as the raid's S0
found it could be:
- `Snapshot` refuses on HIS fight only (`Fighting()`), as before, and writes
  every live clock fight into `combat.clock.live`. `fightUnsettled`
  (`save.go`) needed no clause and has none: `Fighting()`, `WorldHeld()` and
  `wasFighting` all stay his, and `combatUnsettled` reaches `Snapshot`.
- `Snapshot` refuses a clock book or a clock fight holding what no save may
  drop: a step in progress, experience or owed minutes, a pace window, a
  player's verb, a paced round's or a turn's state, a reaction or block cap
  keyed by the running round, a clock fight whose quarry is he, one not
  clock-driven. Each is a T or D classification with its refusal tested
  (`TestCombatClockSnapshotRefusesEveryTransient`, `...Refusals`).
- `Validate` checks the block whole -- counts, `next_id == started + 1`,
  `ended + len(live) == started`, each fight's shape (a `c:<n>` id within the
  sequence, one fight per quarry, a quarry that is an entity and never he,
  enemies unique and not the quarry, `round >= 1`, `since_turn` finite and
  not below `-roundEpsilon` (BUG-85, the raid-r1 follow-up, below),
  `initiator` "enemy", no surprise reason without a surprise),
  the `combat-clock` stream -- and resolves every live fight's quarry and
  enemies. **It does not refuse a model with live clock fights**: B3's save
  validates the live model's own snapshot with it (`validateSnapshots`), and
  at a save the village may be fighting. His `ended == started` rule is
  untouched: the clock has its own sequence and counters (S0-3 (a)).
- `Restore` is `Validate` and then the build, from one function
  (`clockFightsOf`): what Validate accepts is exactly what Restore makes. The
  clock fights replace whatever the model held (the load restores into
  CreateGame's fresh model, which holds none).
- **The refs resolve through a Resolver the game attaches once
  (`Combat.SetResolver(worldResolver{game})`, in CreateGame), not through a
  new argument.** `Snapshot()`, `Validate(s, seed)` and `Restore(s, seed)`
  keep B2a's signatures, so no call site in `save.go`, `load.go` or any test
  changed. A snapshot with live clock fights and no Resolver is refused
  (`ErrUnresolvedRef`), never half restored. The ids in `enemy_order`,
  `dead`, `routed` and `broke` need not resolve: the dead that broke off at
  first light leave the rows (`pruneOrEnd`), and a Downed man who stood again
  leaves his old id behind (`Rejoin`).

**For B4b.** A file holding a live clock fight also holds the watches that
made it (a hostile watching a villager), so B4a refuses it `HUNTED` and it is
B4b's to resume. B4b's load already calls `combat.Validate` and
`combat.Restore` in its order; what it needs is only that the entities are
rebuilt first (the Resolver finds the quarry and enemies on the map), which
is the order it already has. A clock fight's quarry is never `"player"`.

**The five ways, for the clock block** (`d2core/d2world/combat_clock_snapshot_test.go`),
over a world where the village is fighting: a clock fight live at the save
with a dead enemy, a routed one, one broken off at first light and one that
joined late, a second ended with its quarry dead. Classification of every
field of the clock book (`fightBook`) and of a clock fight (`encounter`),
with each T field's refusal checked; the round trip through JSON into a fresh
model on another game's streams, and the run on (the provider, what the game
takes, bodies, packs, chases, watches, the dead) for twelve minutes and a new
quarry; **the sweep, 137 variants, 31 refused and 106 diverged, 0 exempt**;
three probes (the clock's ids and counts one fight on, the live fight under
the ended one's id, the live fight back at round one), each accepted and
seen. His half is the existing combat tests, unedited but for the five
classification rows `TestCombatSnapshotFieldsClassified` asks for the new
`Combat` fields (`player`, `clockFights`, `clockBook`, `resolver`,
`protected`).

**d2save's own tests.** The fixture (`b3Fixture`) gained the clock stream in
`rng.combat_clock` and a clock block in `combat.clock` (a clock fight
started and ended `quarry_dead`): construction data the new fields require,
without which every fixture-based test is refused on its streams. No
assertion changed. At version 2 d2save is 22/0.

**The playtest** (`TestAFightHeIsNotIn`): saves with a clock fight live, the
save not refused and the digest unmoved, and the file decodes with the fight
in `combat.clock.live` and `rng.combat_clock` equal to `combat.clock.rng`.
`TestSaveResume` is green at version 2 with its version check reading
`d2save.Version` (R0.5); the spike, without R0.5, had it red at "7a: version 2".

# The raid's R1 review fixes (branch `raid-r1`, 29 Sep 2026)

An independent review of R0.5 and R1 found one A, four B and four C. What
touches the world file and the save:

**B1 (BUG-68): a dead enemy whose remains left the map made every save
refuse.** A clock fight keeps the rows of the enemies that left it -- dead,
routed, broken off -- for its life (`pruneOrEnd`), and `clockFightsOf`
required every `enemies` id to resolve. The save validates the live model's
own snapshot with it (`save.go` `validateSnapshots` -> `Combat.Validate`), so
from the moment a dead enemy's remains left the map (his body rises and
`takeOffTheMap` removes what lay there; his pack is sent home at daybreak)
until that clock fight ended, every save was refused `ErrUnresolvedRef`: a
fight he is not in stopping a save, which Q5 (a) says never happens. The
review's probe P4 measured it (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-rev-r1\probe-p4.txt`).
**Fixed: only the living must resolve.** A gone enemy is saved as its id
alone -- as `enemy_order`, `dead`, `routed` and `broke` always were -- and at
a restore it is rebuilt from the map when its entity is still there, and as an
id-only row (`goneRow`, standing nowhere) when it is not. A LIVING enemy that
does not resolve is still refused. **The file's shape did not change**:
`combat.clock.live[].enemies` was already a list of ids beside the three
sets; what changed is which of them a load requires. So `world-v2.json` and
its hash (`fa82243b...`, `b3ShapeHashes[2]`) are unchanged, and `Version`
stays 2. Tests: `TestADeadEnemyOffTheMapStopsNoSave` (P4: the save's
`Validate` passes with the dead `d:1` and the routed `d:2` off the map, two
minutes on too; the control beside it, a living `m:3` off the map, is still
refused) and `TestCombatClockSnapshotWithADeadEnemyOffTheMap` (the sweep
case: the world with `d:1` off the map saved, restored into a fresh model
whose map has no `d:1` either, `d:1` back as its id alone, the same model
re-snapshotted, fighting on exactly as the live model does, and B2a's sweep
over the clock block run again on it -- every field refused or diverging).
Control: `b1-resolve-every-enemy` (every enemy must resolve again), both red.

**B3: the bump sets aside every version-1 file. Decision (a), accepted.** The
main session measured on 29 Sep that Josh's `%APPDATA%\OpenDiablo2\Saves`
holds no world file at all, and the save verb is not reachable by a player
until B5, so nothing of his is set aside. `d2save/world.go`'s `Version` comment
says so. The review's other half is a rule Josh must know, because he plays
between bursts, and it is now stated in `world.go` and here: **until the raid
milestone ships, a later burst may amend version 2's shape without a second
bump. A world file written by a build between two such bursts is then refused
by the later build (`FILE`, the shape it does not know) and set aside beside
his save, never lost and never overwritten** (rule 7). The milestone takes one
bump.

**A1 (BUG-67), C1 (BUG-70) and C2 (BUG-71) change no saved field's shape.**
A1's one-fight rule changes which fights exist, not what a fight is. C1
changes what `declines` counts: his saved `declines` (the combat block's
`declines`) now counts, with him bound, only the out-of-reach pairs on him
(it had counted the village's watchers too), and `combat.clock.declines` only
the pairs on the quarry each clock fight was being opened for. Unbound -- the
legacy rule every unit fixture runs on -- nothing changed, and no existing
test's `declines` moved. C2 is readers only.

# The raid-r1 follow-up: BUG-85 and BUG-73 (branch `raid-r1`, 29 Sep 2026)

The merge scout (`merge-scout`, B4b + R1; `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-merge-scout\`)
found two things that belong to this branch. Both touch what a save takes and
what a load refuses; **neither changes the file's shape**: `world-v2.json`,
its hash (`fa82243b...`, `b3ShapeHashes[2]`) and `Version` 2 are as R1 left
them.

**BUG-85: a save on the frame a clock round resolves was refused
`INTERNAL`.** The round loop resolves a round once the accumulated minutes
reach `RoundMinutes-roundEpsilon` and takes a whole `RoundMinutes` off, so a
round resolved on an accumulated 0.9999999999999999 leaves `since_turn` at
about -2e-16 until the next slice, and `checkClockFight` refused anything
below 0: the save's own check (`validateSnapshots` -> `Combat.Validate`)
refused the model's own state, and a load would have refused the file
`BLOCK`. **Fixed: `checkClockFight` refuses `since_turn < -roundEpsilon`, the
loop's own tolerance** (-0.5, "minutes before the round began", is still
refused). This is the scout's fix (`bd1a908b`, filed there as BUG-67 and
renumbered BUG-85 by `5dceaf32`), re-applied by hand because `git cherry-pick`
stops on this branch's own BUG-67 row in `docs/bugs.md`; the two code hunks
went in as they stand, renumbered. Test `TestAClockFightAtARoundsEndSaves`
(fifteen slices of a minute leave c:1 at -1.1e-16: snapshot taken, `Validate`
passes, JSON keeps it exactly, a fresh model restores it and fights on the
same). Control `bug85-lt-zero` (the check back at `< 0`): red,
`since_turn -1.1102230246251565e-16`
(`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-raid-r1-fix2\ctl-bug85-lt-zero.txt`).

**BUG-73: a file whose two clock fights had their quarries swapped was
loaded.** The scout's N3 (`TestSaveResumeAFightHeIsNotIn`'s file-edit
controls): `combat.clock.live[0].quarry` and `[1].quarry` exchanged -- not
refused, resumed, and diverged from S_T. Nothing tied a clock fight to its
enemies: `Combat.Validate` asks only that the quarry and each living enemy
resolve, and the notice and pursuit blocks are each checked alone. At unit
size the swapped world, one step after the load, has let the swapped `c:1`'s
enemies go (each is aware of the other quarry) and ended it, and they fight
on in the other fight: `c:3@v:1`, `c:4@v:9` where the saved world fought on
as `c:1@v:1`, `c:3@v:9`.

**Fixed: `d2world.CheckClockWatches(combat, notice, pursuit)`, a cross-check
of the file's blocks.** For each live clock fight, each LIVING enemy (not in
its `dead`, `routed` or `broke`) is refused when its watch in the notice block
is `noticed` and names someone other than the fight's quarry AND its chase in
the pursuit block does not name the quarry. That is `pruneOrEnd`'s own rule:
at the end of every clock step it lets go of a living enemy whose noticed
watch names another (`awareOfAnother`), and a frame makes every watch and
awareness change before combat steps (`Game.advanceWorld`: the tables step
the notice model, the rising stands the Downed, then combat) -- so no file a
frame leaves holds one.

- **Where it runs.** Not in `Combat.Validate`: at the load's step 4 the
  combat model's notice is the new game's, which watches no one until step 5
  restores the file's, so only the FILE holds both halves. It runs in
  `Game.checkLoad` (step 4), after the blocks' own checks, refused `BLOCK`
  ("combat: ..."), and in the save's `validateSnapshots` on the file the save
  assembled (B3: the save runs the load's checks), refused `INTERNAL` with
  nothing written. `Combat.Validate`, `Restore` and every signature are
  unchanged.
- **What it takes, because the game makes it.** A gone enemy's row, with or
  without a watch or an entity (B1, BUG-68). A living enemy whose watch names
  another and is NOT noticed: `awareOfAnother` keeps it while it is in reach
  -- a Downed man standing again into the village's fight is a spawn table's
  member watching HIM (`Spawns.Raise`), unnoticed while he is far off, and
  he fights on there. A living enemy with no watch (a harness
  `strigoi_unwatch`). A noticed retarget whose chase is still on the quarry: a
  harness `strigoi_watch` moves the watch and never the chase, so a save
  between that verb and the next frame is taken. Any chase, or none. The A1
  rule (one combatant, one live fight, BUG-67) is `Combat`'s and is not read
  here.
- **Limits.** A swap in which every living enemy's watch had lapsed (not
  noticed: out of sight past `MemoryMinutes`, still in reach) is not seen.
  The one save it refuses that a script can make is a harness verb that
  moves a watch to a NOTICED target, on an enemy whose chase is not on its
  quarry, followed by a save before the next world minute -- the game makes
  no such state.
- **Tests.** d2world: `TestSwappedClockQuarriesAreRefused` (the swap refused,
  naming `m:3` aware of `v:1`; `Combat.Validate` alone takes it; swapped
  back, taken; the divergence above), `TestTheWatchCheckTakesWhatTheGameMakes`
  (16 watch and chase states of one enemy, refused or taken), and
  `TestEveryFrameOfTwoClockFightsPassesTheWatchCheck` -- **the save's side**:
  a live model with two clock fights, 40 frames in the game's order through a
  late joiner, a watch moved to him and never noticed, a Downed man standing
  again into `c:1` (`Rejoin`) watching him with no chase, a living enemy
  unwatched, a noticed retarget let go at the next step, and the fights' own
  deaths and a quarry's end: 46 files (every frame, and every harness verb's
  moment between frames), none refused. Game screen:
  `TestAClockFightsSwappedQuarryIsRefused` (a unit game with two clock fights
  on its map: the save writes it, step 4 takes it, and refuses it `BLOCK`
  swapped) and `TestASaveThatTookASwappedClockBlockWritesNothing`.
- **Controls** (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-raid-r1-fix2\ctl-bug73-*.txt`, each
  applied, run and restored by script, the tree's diff hashed):
  `bug73-no-check` (the check a no-op: 4 red), `bug73-no-load-call` (step 4's
  test red), `bug73-no-save-call` (the save's test red), `bug73-strict` (every
  living enemy's watch or chase must name the quarry: the sweep red at the
  Downed man standing again) and `bug73-ignore-chase` (the sweep red at a
  `strigoi_watch` between frames).
- **The register** gained `d2world.CheckClockWatches`, wire and live
  (`Game.checkLoad`).

**For the merge with B4b.** Both are this branch's. B4b's `checkLoad`
rebuilds every entity (step 3) before its list of checks, so the file's refs
resolve there; the merge keeps the `CheckClockWatches` entry after `combat` in
that list (a one-line hunk beside B4b's rewrite of the function) and the call
at the end of `validateSnapshots`. On the merged tree the scout's
`TestSaveResumeAFightHeIsNotIn` N3 control should be refused `BLOCK` at the
load rather than resumed; the script accepts either today ("refused, or not
S_T", its `refuse` field false), so the merge can make N3 a must-refuse.

# B4b × R1 integration (merge) (branch `merge-b4b-r1`, 29 Sep 2026)

The real merge of the two reviewed and fixed branches, from master `78a6ffe4`,
for the main session to fast-forward `master`. Every log is in
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-merge\`; the conflict log is `conflicts.md` there.

## What merged

- **`93aaf2d4`**, `git merge --no-ff save-b4b` (at `7f55e30a`): B4b
  (`5c705ce2`) and its review fixes (`c50e7c2a`, `7f55e30a`). It applied
  cleanly. It files BUG-65, BUG-75..84 and BUG-86 and brings harness 0.14.3.
- **`2025e358`**, `git merge --no-ff raid-r1` (at `60c4a17a`): R0.5
  (`f2fee245`), R1 (`7fcc70b0`), the R1 review fixes (`3b416f82`), BUG-85
  (`43db30d3`) and BUG-73 (`60c4a17a`). It files BUG-66..73 and BUG-85, and the
  world file is version 2.
- **`0660a9c5`**, the integration commit: the scout's playtests adjusted to
  the fixed code, BUG-87, and this section. A last docs commit records the
  runs (the ledger line and "The runs" below).

## The conflicts, and how they were resolved

Four files conflicted. **Both sides were kept everywhere.**

- **`load.go`, `checkLoad`'s step-4 list.** B4b's rewrite added the bodies
  check; raid-r1 added the one-line `d2world.CheckClockWatches(w.Combat,
  w.Notice, w.Pursuit)`. The raid's line sits right after `combat`'s
  `Validate`, and the bodies check follows it.
  - The rest of B4b's rewrite is untouched. The step-3 entity rebuild
    (`rebuildEntities`) still runs inside `checkLoad`, at the end of
    `CreateGame`, before the list.
  - The combat block, clock and all, is still checked at step 4 and restored
    at step 5 (`restoreWorld`), after notice and pursuit and after the rebuild.
    The scout's controls M3 and M4 show that this order is the one that works.
- **`docs/bugs.md`.** The rows are in number order: 65 (B4b), 66-73 (raid-r1),
  **74**, 75-84 (B4b), 85 (raid-r1), 86 (B4b), and now 87 (this merge).
  - **74 is unused.** It has a row that says so: the two fix rounds held 67-84
    between them, and no bug took 74.
  - Both launch-ledger tails are kept, B4b's lines first.
- **`docs/harness.md`.** B4b's `save_resume_test.go` text comes first, then
  raid-r1's `fight_he_is_not_in_test.go` bullet, which is now "the
  forty-eighth".
  - The count was 48, and is **49** with `save_clock_fight_test.go`.
  - The heading keeps B4b's **0.14.3**. raid-r1 never bumped the version and
    stayed at 0.14.1.
- **This file.** The sections are in order: B4b, then the B4b review fixes,
  then R0.5, R1, the R1 review fixes and the raid-r1 follow-up. This section
  comes last.

**Auto-merged, and checked by hand:**

- **`save.go` keeps both refusals.** B4b's `heldAction` refuses `FIGHTING`
  while any monster or villager holds an action. raid-r1's `CheckClockWatches`
  runs at the end of `validateSnapshots` and refuses `INTERNAL` with nothing
  written. **Q5 (a) holds as the code reads:** `Combat.Fighting` and
  `combatUnsettled` read his fight only, so a fight he is not in never refuses
  a save *as a fight*. It can still refuse one through an animation (BUG-87,
  below). **Since BUG-87's fix (`save-held`) it cannot: `heldAction` is
  gone.**
- `game.go`: `SetResolver` and `SetProtected` run in `CreateGame` before
  `checkLoad`.
- `save_resume_test.go` (raid-r1's `d2save.Version` check), `register.go` and
  `reachability.md`: both sides' rows are in.
- **Hand edit, `d2app/harness.go`:** raid-r1's harness items were added to the
  changelog comment of **0.14.3**.

## The golden

**`world-v2.json` matches the merged shape.** `d2core/d2save` is green on the
merge, and the shape hash is
**`fa82243b4569ce29eb4b2a5c7179f36489d4a90accf8800fa1fe61c586b71565`**
(`world_shape_test.go`, `b3ShapeHashes[2]`), raid-r1's, unchanged. Neither
fixed branch changed the shape, so nothing was regenerated.

## From the scout (rewritten against the fixed code)

The scout (`merge-scout`, `aa407372`..`5dceaf32`) merged the pre-fix branches
and ran the test neither builder could run: **a hunted night with a live clock
fight, saved and resumed.** Its findings now stand like this:

- **Its BUG-67 is BUG-85**, fixed on raid-r1 (`43db30d3`). A save on the frame
  a clock round resolved used to be refused `INTERNAL`.
- **Its music edge is BUG-86**, fixed on save-b4b. The village's `sound_env`
  and `music` are now in the digest's `process` part. The scripts still play
  one second first, which keeps their T where the scout measured it.
- **Its N3 hardening candidate is BUG-73**, fixed on raid-r1:
  `CheckClockWatches` runs at the load's step 4 and at the save. **N3 is now a
  must-refuse, and it is refused `BLOCK` on the merge.**
- **No integration fix to the load order was needed, and none is needed now.**
  The scout's M3 moved the clock fights' restore before the rebuild, and the
  load was refused `BLOCK` because the quarry was not in the world yet. Its M4
  moved only the stream, and the load was green. The step order is unchanged
  on the merge (checked by reading `load.go`), so those results carry over.
  They were not re-run here.
- Left as the scout left it: the R1 review's A1 (a monster in both fights) is
  kept out of these scripts, because it is tested on its own
  (`TestAFightHeIsNotInOneFightEach`). `omit` takes only top-level blocks, so
  `combat.clock` is dropped by editing the file.

## The integration test: `playtest/save_clock_fight_test.go`

The scout's two scripts are brought in as one new file, taken from
`merge-scout` (`git show merge-scout:playtest/save_clock_fight_test.go`) and
adjusted in two passes. Both passes are scripts, kept in `scaffold\`:
`adapt_scout_test.py` and `adapt_dials.py`.

1. **The save at T is retried** (`scSaveSettled`). The save is asked on T's
   frame. While it is refused `FIGHTING` on an action still playing, the script
   steps one frame and asks again. This is B4b's recommended (a) for B5.
   - **Any other refusal is RED.** Under Q5 (a), a fight he is not in never
     refuses a save as a fight.
   - T's own condition must hold at every retry.
   - The whole digest before and after the accepted save is one value, so S_T
     is read after the save.
   - **The retries are logged: 23 frames for the hunted night (all `A1`) and
     23 for the quick script (22 `A1`, 1 `GH`).**
2. **N1, N2 and N3 must be refused, each with its code and reason:**
   - N1, `combat.clock` omitted: `FILE`, from d2save's shape check (B3-8);
   - N2, a quarry no entity has: `BLOCK`, "does not resolve";
   - N3, the quarries swapped: `BLOCK`, "is aware of" (BUG-73).
   The hunted night's own control (V6, `combat.clock` dropped) must be refused
   `FILE` too.
3. **The round is slowed**, and this is the one dial that is steered: at night
   `round_minutes` is 3, at dawn 4. At the shipped round, a clock fight that
   has drawn blood holds an action on every frame until it ends (BUG-87,
   below), so T could never be saved. At 3 and 4 minutes, each round leaves 34
   frames at night and 22 at dawn on which a save can be made. The dial is set
   on every game on both sides of each comparison. In the quick script, the
   minutes before T (until both quarries are hurt) and the minutes after it
   (three rounds) follow from the dial.

**Green on the merge** (`pt-sc-1.txt`):

- **`TestSaveResumeWithAFightHeIsNotIn`** saved at 21:54 (night) after 74
  frames and 23 retries, with the zombie chasing him and `c:1` live at round 2
  (quarry 60 of 61, combat-clock draws 4).
  - V3: after a relaunch, S_R0 = S_T.
  - V4: S_R = S_U, with the same ending (`enemies_dead`, rounds 6, draws 18).
  - V5: in process, S_T, then S_U.
  - V6: N1 is refused `FILE`.
- **`TestSaveResumeAFightHeIsNotIn`** had both quarries hurt after 3 world
  minutes and saved at 02:56 after 23 retries, with 2 live clock fights.
  - V3, V4 and V5 are green.
  - N1 is refused `FILE`, N2 `BLOCK` and N3 `BLOCK` ("its living enemy ... is
    aware of ...").

**The negative controls:**

- **In the scripts:** N1, N2 and N3 as above, and the hunted night's V6.
- **Source mutation M1, the combat-clock stream not restored:**
  `restoreClock`'s `k.RNG.RestoreInto(b.rng)` was replaced by `_ = k.RNG`. It
  was applied, run and restored by `mut\run-m1.sh`.
  - The result is **RED in both scripts at V3**: `clock.rng` draws 8 -> 0 in
    the quick script and 4 -> 0 in the hunted night (`nc-m1.txt`).
  - The source was put back byte for byte: `cmp` shows it identical and
    `git diff` is empty (`mut\m1-restored.txt`).

## BUG-87: a clock fight stops every save from its first blow to its last death

**FIXED on branch `save-held` (29 Sep 2026) by option (a) below: see "BUG-87:
the held action is saved" at the end of this file.** What follows is the
merge's account, kept as it was.

This is the question the merge brief asked, for Josh: can B5's dawn autosave
and menu save simply retry, or does the held-action refusal need narrowing
for clock fights on raid nights?

**How it was measured.** A throwaway scaffold was used, never committed and
kept at `scaffold\zz_heldaction_measure_test.go`. Seed 1462, `zombie1` on
`fallen1` fifteen tiles off, with the shipped combat dials (`player_control`
human). The save was asked on **every frame** with `strigoi_save_game` and
`to`, which writes only a temp world file and refuses exactly as the real
save does. Each answer was classified as accepted, refused on a held action,
or refused otherwise. The three-fight scenes open their fights 8 frames apart.
The data is in `held-summary.txt`, `held-summary-dials.txt`, `held-*.csv`,
`pt-measure-1.txt` and `pt-measure-2.txt`.

| scene (round_minutes) | frames with a live clock fight | refused on a held action | longest refused run | mean wait for a save asked on a live frame |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| dawn, 1 fight (shipped 1) | 210 | 195 (**92.9%**) | 251 frames, **4.18 s** | 143 frames (2.4 s) |
| dawn, 3 fights (shipped 1) | 211 | 196 (**92.9%**) | 252 frames, **4.20 s** | 144 frames |
| night, 1 fight (shipped 1) | 288 | 264 (**91.7%**) | 320 frames, **5.33 s** | 173 frames |
| night, 3 fights (shipped 1) | 360 | 336 (**93.3%**) | 392 frames, **6.53 s** | 210 frames (3.5 s) |
| dawn, 1 fight (2) | 420 | 390 (92.9%) | 446 frames, 7.43 s | 234 frames |
| dawn, 1 fight (4) | 780 | 456 (58.5%) | 56 frames, 0.93 s (runs of 38) | 11 frames |
| night, 1 fight (2) | 624 | 456 (73.1%) | 56 frames, 0.93 s (runs of 38) | 14 frames |
| night, 1 fight (4) | 1344 | 494 (36.8%) | 56 frames, 0.93 s (runs of 38) | 7 frames |
| night, 3 pairs, at most 2 live at once (4) | 1352 | 592 (43.8%) | 56 frames, 0.93 s (runs of 38-46) | 10 frames |

**What it shows:**

- **Not one refusal was of another kind.** Every refusal was a held action
  (`A1`, `GH`, `DT`), so Q5 (a) holds as written.
- **The holds outlast the round.** A round's swing (`A1`) and flinch (`GH`)
  hold an entity **38 frames** (0.63 s). A death (`DT`) holds **56** (0.93
  s).
- **So at the shipped round the holds overlap.** A round comes every 24 frames
  at night and every 15 at dawn, one world minute at 2.5 and 4 minutes a
  second.
- **The only frames that took a save were the ones before each fight's first
  blow.** The refused run is the whole fight, plus its last death.
- **The run grows with the fight, and the measured fights were short.** These
  61-hit-point quarries died in about 6 rounds. A raid night's village fights,
  one after another, would refuse the save for as long as they run. **So
  "retry until accepted" waits for the village to stop fighting.**
- With a round of 4 minutes, a save waits at most 56 frames.

**The options, for Josh (recommended first):**

- **(a) Carry the held action's frame in the world file, and drop the refusal
  for held actions (a B5 prerequisite).** `d2mapentity.Motion` gains the
  action's elapsed frame, and `RestoreMotion` resumes it there. The file's
  shape changes: version 3, a new golden, and the B3-8 check. (**Taken** --
  as an amendment of version 2, not version 3: the milestone's rule allows it
  until saving is a player's, and B5 had not merged. See the next section.)
  This is the exact
  fix. BUG-76 refused only because the file carried the mode and not its
  frame. It removes the wait from B5's autosave and menu save whenever anyone
  fights, in the village or in a pack.
- **(b) Narrow the refusal to deaths (`DT`), and resume a swing or a flinch
  from its first frame.** Smaller, but no longer exact: the resumed swing ends
  up to 38 frames later than the saved one, and the digest compares `held` and
  `animation_mode` (BUG-82). The world could differ for about a second, and for
  longer if a held action gates a walk.
- **(c) Keep the refusal, and let B5's saves wait.** The autosave retries
  until the village's fights are over; the menu save says "not while they
  fight". This is simplest, but on a raid night the save may be unavailable
  for minutes.
- **(d) Retune the round.** At `round_minutes` 3 or more a save waits at most
  a second. This changes the pace of combat, not the save rule, so it is a
  combat ruling and not a save fix.

**Not decided here.** The merge scripts slow the round (option (d)'s effect,
in the tests only), so that they test resume exactness and not this refusal.
