# BUG-87: the held action is saved (branch `save-held`, 29 Sep 2026)

Josh's rule is that the save "stops at that point then resumes at that
point". The B4b review fixes kept that rule for a monster's or villager's
action still playing -- a swing, a blow taken, a death -- by REFUSING the save
while one was held (BUG-76: `Game.heldAction`, `FIGHTING`) and the load of a
file that held one (`heldInFile`), because the file carried the held mode and
not its frame. The B4b x R1 merge measured what that cost at the shipped
combat pace (BUG-87, above): in a fight he is not in, 92-93% of frames refused
a save, for the whole fight and its last death. This burst takes **option (a),
the recommended default (Josh can overturn it): the held action's state is
saved in the file, restored at its saved frame, and both refusals are
dropped.** It is exact. The options not taken: (b) refuse only during a death
(a swing or a flinch resumed from its first frame -- inexact); (c) keep the
refusal and let B5's saves wait (minutes on a raid night); (d) slow combat (a
combat ruling, not a save fix). The combat pace is unchanged: the re-measure
below plays the merge's fights to the same rounds, draws and endings.

Every log is in `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-held\`. Bug rows: BUG-87 (FIXED),
BUG-76 (superseded), and three found on the way: BUG-88 (fixed), BUG-89 and
BUG-90 (open). Harness 0.14.4.

## The held action, measured (`strigoi-measure-first`)

Two throwaway scaffolds, deleted before the commit and kept in `scaffold\`,
logged a held action's state on every frame at the harness tick (1/60 s):

- **A creature** (`zz_held_measure_test.go`, a unit test on the shipped
  feral-dog sheets; `measure-creature.csv`, `measure-creature.txt`): a bite,
  a blow taken, a death, and a bite turned 20 frames in.
- **An NPC** (`zz_held_state_test.go`, a playtest: `zombie1` on `fallen1`
  fifteen tiles off at the shipped round, the merge's fight; temporary `zz_`
  fields in the NPC's harness state for the composite's play count, frame
  count and every layer, removed before the commit; `measure-npc.csv`, the
  runs split by `scaffold\analyze_npc.py` into `measure-npc-runs.txt`).

What defines a held action exactly:

| part | a creature (PNG sheet) | an NPC (D2 composite) | carried |
|---|---|---|---|
| the mode | `heldMode` (`attack`, `hit`, `death`); `mode` is the sheet it plays on, idle's when the art has none | `heldMode` (`A1`, `GH`, `DT`); the composite's mode | `action` (and `mode`), as before |
| the frame | the sheet's `frameIndex` | the composite MODE's `frameIndex` -- what decides the end; the layers keep their own, drawn | **`action_at.frame`, new** |
| the sub-frame progress | the sheet's `lastFrameTime`, seconds into the frame | the mode's `lastFrameTime` | **`action_at.elapsed`, new** |
| the facing | `direction` (the sheet's row from it) | the composite's direction | `dir`, as before |
| the play count | 0 on every frame it is held (measured: every tick of every action) -- the action ends on the Advance that makes it 1 | the same, measured on every held frame of the fight | not carried: always 0, and the restore sets it so |
| whether it repeats | every sheet loops (`playLoop`); the hold is what ends it, at play count 1 | the mode wraps; the same | the art's, rebuilt with the entity |
| the speed | a PNG sheet plays in 1.0 s: attack 10 frames (61 ticks held), hit 6 (60), death 12 (60) | D2's animdata: A1 16 frames on `zombie1`, 10 on `fallen1`, at 0.04 s a frame (38 ticks for `zombie1`'s swing); GH 7 frames; DT 19 frames in 56 ticks | the art's |
| what the end does | `finishAction`: a bite or a blow back to idle (walk next frame if it still walks); a death to the Dead pose, a corpse, turned by `StopMoving` | `finishHeldAction`: A1 or GH back to NU; DT to DD, a corpse | the result is state the file already carries (`mode`, `corpse`) |
| the callback | nil: the game starts every action with nil (`npc_body.go`) | nil | nothing to carry |

**What the measurement changed or settled:**

- **The sub-frame time must be carried bit for bit.** A sheet's frame length is
  1/n s, and six ticks of 1/60 s land on its boundary only by float rounding:
  the dog's bite read 0.0999999999999999 s into frame 0, not 0.1, and stepped
  on the next tick. So `elapsed` is the float as the animation holds it (JSON
  round-trips a float64 exactly), and the controls below drop it and diverge.
- **The play count is 0 whenever a save can see a held action**, so it is not
  a field: the restore sets it to 0 (`Composite.SetProgress`), which also
  covers `SetMode`'s short-circuit on a mode the composite is already in.
- **An NPC's layers were at the mode's frame on every held frame** of the fight
  (no fighter turned while holding), so the restore puts each layer at the
  mode's frame (modulo its own count) with the mode's time -- exact for the
  measured fights and bit-exact in the unit test's 16- and 19-frame modes with
  8-, 9-, 16- and 19-frame layers. A turn DOES put the layers back to their
  first frame while the mode runs on (read, not seen): BUG-90.
- **At dawn a clock round (15 frames) restarts the swing and the flinch before
  they end** (StartAction cycles through NU): a dawn swing of `zombie1` never
  plays past frame 6 of 16. Nothing to carry: the restart is the next round's.
- **A creature turned mid-action restarts its sheet** (`rotate` ->
  `PNGAnimation.SetDirection` puts the frame back to 0, and `setTarget` calls
  it at every waypoint): the turned bite held 121 ticks, not 61. The save
  carries the frame as it is, so the resume is exact; the behaviour itself is
  BUG-89.

**Did not measure:** a creature's held action inside a real playtest fight
frame by frame (the creatures in the playtests are the packs; act 6i saves
their deaths and compares the whole world, but no scaffold logged a creature's
sheet per frame in a game); a villager's (a native's) held action in a game
(unit-tested only: the unit game's villager is a creature); frame lengths at
any tick but 1/60 s.

## The world file: `action_at`, version 2 amended

- `d2mapentity.Motion` gains `ActionAt *ActionProgress` --
  `"action_at": {"frame": 4, "elapsed": 0.0125}` -- written exactly when
  `action` is, and omitted otherwise, so a file that holds no action is the
  same bytes either side of the amendment. `Motion.check` refuses an action
  with no `action_at`, an `action_at` with no action, a negative frame and a
  NaN or infinite time; `World.Check` holds the time finite
  (`ReasonEntityFinite`).
- **An amendment of version 2, not a bump** (`d2save.Version` stays 2; its
  comment records it). The milestone's rule allows amending version 2's shape
  until saving is a player's, and B5 had not merged. A version-2 file written
  before the amendment cannot hold an action -- the save refused one -- and
  one that did would be refused at the load's step 3.
- **The golden**, `d2core/d2save/testdata/world-v2.json`, is regenerated
  (`-update-golden`): the fixture's wolf is now mid-bite (`mode` and `action`
  `attack`, `action_at` frame 4 + 0.0125 s), so the golden shows the field.
  **The shape hash, `b3ShapeHashes[2]` in `world_shape_test.go`, is
  `be2baeb5d220d42c0d27a1c4402cb3851ce09562e20cc9a49ee2df5af81c14d4`**
  (was `fa82243b4569ce29eb4b2a5c7179f36489d4a90accf8800fa1fe61c586b71565`);
  the two shape lines added are `entities[].motion.action_at,omitempty?.elapsed
  float64` and `entities[].motion.action_at,omitempty?.frame int`.

## Snapshot and restore

- **New engine accessors**, the smallest that define the state:
  `Animation.Progress() (frame, elapsed)` and `Animation.SetProgress(frame,
  elapsed)` (`d2asset`, and on `d2interface.Animation`; every animation kind
  embeds `Animation`), and `Composite.Progress() (frame, elapsed, played)` and
  `Composite.SetProgress(frame, elapsed)` -- the mode at that point on its
  first play, each layer with it. Both refuse a frame the art does not have,
  changing nothing.
- **`Creature.MotionSnapshot`** reads the sheet's progress while held;
  **`Creature.RestoreMotion`** checks the frame against the sheet the action
  will play on (idle's when it has none) BEFORE anything changes, then
  `setMode` (rewind, play count 0, facing -- which puts the frame to 0), then
  `SetProgress`: **the saved frame, not frame 0.**
- **`NPC.MotionSnapshot`** reads the composite mode's progress (an NPC with no
  composite, a unit test's, reports frame 0); **`NPC.RestoreMotion`** sets the
  mode, the facing, then `Composite.SetProgress`, after the facing because a
  turn resets the layers.
- **BUG-75 is kept**: a death still ends the walk where it begins
  (`mapEntity.halt`) and `Advance` does not step a dying body; a death restored
  mid-way is restored with no route, as it was saved, and lies where it fell.
- The load reads every restore back (`restoreMotionExactly`), so a frame or a
  time that did not take is a refusal, not a resume.
- **Harness 0.14.4:** an NPC or creature holding an action reports
  `held_frame` and `held_elapsed`, read from the animation itself (not from
  the snapshot, so a save that wrote another point is seen); the digest
  compares them. The one animation frame counter in an entity's state, and it
  is world state: it decides when the action ends.

## The refusals

- **Dropped:** `Game.heldAction` -- the save's `FIGHTING` "... is still
  playing its death: a save carries no animation's frame" -- and its clause in
  `fightUnsettled`; `heldInFile` -- the load's `ENTITY` (a monster) and
  `NATIVES` (a villager) for a file holding an action. Both functions and
  their register rows are gone. The `save.go` edit is those lines and two
  comments, self-contained (B5 edits `save.go` in parallel).
- **Kept:** HIS OWN SWING -- "his last swing is still playing"
  (`localPlayer.IsCasting`). It exists and covers his paced turn: his swing is
  played only in or just after his own fight, it is not in the file (rule 4
  stands him still), and "saving is refused mid-fight" is its own rule
  (`Combat.Fighting`, `combatUnsettled`, the fight edge, a strike waiting on a
  walk, a tactical walk out, the journal reading a fight), untouched.
- **New, at the restore** (each a refusal of the load's step 3, `ENTITY` or
  `NATIVES`, and the whole game torn down): a held action with no frame; a
  frame with no held action; a frame the art does not have; a NaN time. And
  the B4b review's "second hazard" returns as a refusal: a creature saved
  holding a death its art had no sheet for, loaded after an art pass adds one,
  reads back in another mode and is refused (above, in "The B4b review fixes").

## Act 6i, and the merge's scripts, at the shipped pace

- **`TestSaveResume` act 6i now saves MID-DEATH:** the quick resolve lays the
  slain; the save is made at once, on the first try, with at least one of them
  still falling (each in the file at its frame; his entity reports it); T'
  resumes exactly (S_R0 = S_T', the deaths at their frames), is the same world
  twenty frames on (the deaths still playing) and 120 frames on (every slain a
  corpse), and each resumed corpse lies where the saved game's lies.
- **`playtest/save_clock_fight_test.go` has no steer left.** `round_minutes`
  is gone from `scNightDials` and `villageFightDials`; `scShippedPace`
  requires the provider to report the shipped 1.0; `scSaveSettled` (the retry
  loop) is replaced by `scSaveAtT`, which asks once, fails RED on any refusal
  and returns 0 retries, which both scripts assert; and `scHeldInFile`
  requires T's file to hold at least one action still playing, at its frame
  -- the case the fix is for. **Measured on the first run: the hunted night
  saved at 21:51 with 2 actions held (A1 and GH at frame 0 + 0.0333 s), the
  quick script at 02:51 with 4 (GH, A1, A1, GH at frame 4 + 0.0233 s), both
  on the first try.**
- **New, `TestSaveResumeMidAction`** (in the same file, so the script count is
  unchanged): an NPC saved mid-A1, mid-GH and mid-DT at the shipped round,
  each on the first try, the file holding the frame and time the entity
  reports, "load last save" by the menu in process resuming it there, and the
  world the same 7 and 60 frames on (`pt-2.txt`: A1 at frame 1 + 0.01 s, GH at
  frame 1 + 0.01 s, DT at frame 3 + 0.0023 s, the death ending on the same
  frame in both games).

## Found on the way

- **BUG-88 (fixed): the clock strip's time-to-sunset was sampled on the
  refresh frame**, so a game resumed part-way through a minute held another
  sample of it; `TestSaveResumeMidAction`'s M-GH save (`pt-1.txt`) had 16.85 h
  saved ("Sunset in 16.9h") and 16.8356 h resumed ("16.8h"), and S_R0 = S_T
  went red on `ui.clock_strip_text`. `Clock.HoursToDusk` now counts from the
  start of the current world minute (`math.Floor`), the minute the HUD keys
  its refresh on; the readout moves by under a sixtieth of an hour, and
  `clock_strip_hours_to_dusk` is back in the digest's world part. Unit:
  `TestHoursToDuskCountsFromTheMinutesStart`; `TestClockHUD` green.
- **BUG-89 (open, Josh's):** a creature's held action restarts its sheet at
  every waypoint and turn (measured: 121 ticks against 61).
- **BUG-90 (open, render-only):** an NPC turned mid-action draws its layers
  from frame 0 while its mode runs on; a load draws them at the mode's frame.

| scene (shipped round) | frames with a live clock fight | refused on a held action: the merge (BUG-76) | after BUG-87 | refused otherwise, after | longest refused run, after |

| dawn, 1 fight | 210 | 195 (92.9%) | **0 (0.0%)** | 0 | 0 frames |

## Tests

**Unit** (every package green in the gate's `go test ./...`, `ok=60 fail=0`,
`gate-1.txt` and `gate-2.txt`; `unit-1.txt`, run while the scaffolds were
still in the tree, is green but for `tools/docscount`, which a scaffold fails
by design):

- `d2mapentity`: **`TestCreatureMotionResumesAHeldActionAtItsFrame`** -- a
  walking dog saved mid-bite, mid-blow and mid-death, each on its first tick,
  a tick in, seven in, half-way and on its last tick; rebuilt in another
  factory from the motion read back through JSON; read back equal, and the
  saved dog on every tick after (harness state, velocity, raw position) until
  60 ticks past the action's end -- the bite and the blow ending back to idle
  and walking on, the death in a corpse where it fell, on the same tick.
  **`TestAHeldActionIsAlwaysOnItsFirstPlay`** (the play count is 0 on every
  tick held). **`TestCreatureHeldMotionEveryFieldIsSeen`**: mid-bite, the frame
  put back to the first, moved on, the time into it lost or a hair off,
  another action, the held action dropped -- each diverges; the frame dropped
  or past the sheet -- each refused, changing nothing.
  `TestCreatureMotionEveryFieldIsSeen` gains five refusals (an action with no
  frame, a frame with no action, a frame the sheet lacks, a negative frame, a
  NaN time); `TestNPCMotionCarriesItsPose` and `TestMotionFieldsClassified`
  carry `action_at` (the creature's `animation` and the NPC's `composite` are
  now labelled `S:action_at`). B2b's `TestCreatureMotionResumesAnActionFromItsStart`
  is replaced by the first test above.
- `d2asset` (new, `composite_progress_test.go`, a hand-built mode with PNG
  layers -- a real composite needs the MPQs): **`TestACompositeResumesAtItsProgress`**
  (a 16-frame swing and a 19-frame death, layers of the mode's count and of
  fewer, saved at five points; a fresh composite turned as saved and set to
  the progress is the saved one on every tick -- mode frame, time, play count,
  every layer -- through the tick it plays through);
  `TestACompositeWithoutItsProgressEndsOnAnotherTick` (frame 0, or the time
  lost: another tick); `TestACompositesProgressIsSetOnItsFirstPlay`;
  `TestAnAnimationsProgressRoundTrips`.
- `d2gamescreen`: **`TestADeathSavedHalfPlayedResumesAtItsFrame`** (replaces
  `TestADeathIsNeitherWalkedNorSavedHalfPlayed`: the walking wolf slain 0, 3
  and 8 frames before the save; BUG-75's walk ends where the death begins; the
  save is MADE, the file holds the death at the frame his sheet shows; S_R0 =
  S_T; the two games frame by frame equal until both lie, on the same frame,
  where the death began; S_R = S_U a hundred frames on);
  **`TestASwingAndABlowSavedHalfPlayedResumeAtTheirFrame`** (a bite on the
  walker, a blow on the wounded, a blow on the villager -- a native, re-keyed
  in place -- saved 0, 4 and 11 frames in, frame by frame equal for 60
  frames, each ending on the same frame); **`TestAFileHoldingAnActionResumesItAtItsFrame`**
  (replaces `TestAFileHoldingAnActionIsRefused`: a death, a bite and a
  villager's blow written into the file part-way through their sheets resume
  there; with no frame, or at frame 99, each refused `ENTITY` or `NATIVES`,
  nothing waiting in the seam).
- `d2save`: the golden and the hash (above); `World.Check` refuses a NaN
  time into an action (`ReasonEntityFinite`).
- `d2world`: `TestHoursToDuskCountsFromTheMinutesStart` (BUG-88).

**Playtests:** `TestSaveResume` whole (act 6i mid-death), the two clock-fight

## The decisions (the recommended option each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **BUG-87-1, option (a): carry the held action, drop the refusals** -- the
  brief's decision, the merge's recommended default. Not (b) (a death-only
  refusal; a resumed swing restarts), (c) (wait; minutes on a raid night) or
  (d) (slow combat; a combat ruling).
- **BUG-87-2, amend version 2, do not bump.** The milestone's rule allows it
  until saving is a player's, and B5 has not merged; and no version-2 file
  that holds an action can exist (the save refused one until now), so every
  earlier version-2 file reads the same. The merge's option text said
  "version 3"; the brief overrode it.
- **BUG-87-3, the fields are `action_at {frame, elapsed}`, a pointer written
  only with `action`**, not flat `action_frame`/`action_elapsed`: a held
  action and its point are one thing, a file that holds no action is
  unchanged, and "an action with no point" is a refusal rather than a zero.
- **BUG-87-4, the play count is not carried** (measured 0 whenever held); the
  restore sets it to 0.
- **BUG-87-5, an NPC's layers are restored to the mode's point**, not carried:
  they are what is drawn, they were at the mode's point on every held frame
  measured, and carrying them would put render state in the file. The one
  case it misses -- a turn mid-action -- is BUG-90, render-only.
- **BUG-87-6, the harness reports `held_frame`/`held_elapsed` from the
  animation, and the digest compares them** (harness 0.14.4): the one
  animation counter in an entity's state, justified because it is world
  state; without it a resume that restarted an action is invisible until the
  action's end.
- **BUG-87-7, his own swing's refusal is kept** (it exists and covers his
  paced turn; "saving is refused mid-fight" is its own rule).
- **BUG-87-8, `TestSaveResumeMidAction` lives in `save_clock_fight_test.go`**,
  not a new file: the script count (49) and the docs count stay, and it is the
  same fight the merge measured.
- **BUG-88, fixed here, because the new script found it and it breaks the
  same rule** (a save resumed not at its point): the strip counts from the
  minute's start. The alternative, moving the strip's text to the digest's
  process part, would have hidden a difference the player can read.
- **BUG-89 and BUG-90 are filed, not fixed**: they change how long a
  creature's swing plays and how a turned monster is drawn -- combat feel and
  art, not the save -- and the save is exact about both as they are.

# BUG-87 review fixes (branch `save-held`, 29 Sep 2026)

The independent review of BUG-87 (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-rev-held\review.md`)
found the fix exact -- saves mid-swing, mid-flinch and mid-death resume
exactly -- and filed **0 A, 2 B and 6 C**, and the two bugs BUG-87 left open,
BUG-89 and BUG-90. **Every one is fixed here, each with a negative control
seen red.** On anything open the recommended option was taken (Josh can
overturn any; below). Every log is in `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-held-fix\`;
the controls in `nc\` (`nc\nc.py`, `nc\nc-summary.txt`). Bug rows: BUG-91
(B1), BUG-92 (B2), BUG-93 (C1), BUG-94 (C6), all fixed; BUG-89 and BUG-90
closed in place, BUG-90's row corrected (C5). Harness 0.14.4, amended (the
branch has not merged). **The world file's shape is unchanged** -- no field
added or removed; the golden and its hash (`be2baeb5...`) stand.

Every control is an exact-string mutation of the source, asserted to match
once, built, run against the tests that must see it, and put back; since the
tree was uncommitted while they ran, the proof it is back is each file's
sha256, byte-identical before and after (every line of `nc-summary.txt`).
The unit ones ran with no slot; the playtest ones under one hold of it
(`lock-waits.txt`, 20:21:32 to 20:30:35).

## Findings, fixes, tests and controls

### B1 -- a finite but impossible `action_at.elapsed` crashed the game (BUG-91)

The review saved `zombie1` mid-A1, edited the file to `elapsed -1.0`, and
every check took it (`Decode`, `World.Check`, the restore, the read-back); on
the first frame `Composite.Advance` took whole frames off the mode's frame
and `Composite.Render` indexed `Priority[dir][-8]` -- a panic. 1e17 s and
more did the same through the frame's int overflow, and a creature's sheet
looped once per frame the time spanned (1e300 s: a death that never lies).
The game never writes one.

- **The fix, where the frame length is known -- the animation:** both
  `SetProgress`s (`d2asset.Animation`, `d2asset.Composite`) refuse a time
  below `d2asset.ElapsedFloor` (-1e-9: Advance leaves a few ulps below zero at
  most), at or past one frame's length (`playLength/frameCount` for a sheet,
  exposed as `Animation.FrameLength`; the mode's `animationSpeed` for a
  composite, `Composite.FrameLength`), or not a number -- changing nothing. A
  composite's layers are then set unchecked (`setProgress`): the mode's time
  is the one that matters, and a layer's frame -- the mode's speed times its
  frames, over its frames -- can be an ulp shorter than the mode's (found:
  speed 10.24 s, 29 frames), so a time the mode takes must not be refused by
  a layer.
- **The file (`d2save`'s `checkEntities`) does NOT know the frame length** --
  it is the art's, which `d2save` never loads -- so it bounds what no art
  allows: a negative frame and a time below the floor
  (`d2mapentity.ActionProgress.Check`, shared with `Motion.check`; refused
  `entity-finite`). -1.0 s is now refused at the load's step 1 (`FILE`),
  before a game is built. The ceiling is the restore's: a time at or past the
  art's whole play is refused there (`ENTITY`, `NATIVES`), and one past a
  frame but short of the whole play is an art change (B2, below).
- **Unit:** `TestASheetRefusesATimeNoPlayCouldHave` and
  `TestACompositeRefusesATimeNoPlayCouldHave` (d2asset): -1.0, a hair below
  the floor, 1e17, 1e18, **exactly one frame's length**, a hair past it, NaN
  and both infinities refused, each changing nothing; a hair below zero, the
  floor itself, 0, half a frame and **the last value under one frame** taken,
  and each plays on with the mode's frame inside the mode.
  `TestACompositesLayersTakeEveryTimeItsModeTakes`. `TestCheckRefusesWhatNoLoadCouldRestore`
  (d2save) gains three cases (-1.0 s, -2e-9 s, frame -1).
  `TestACorruptHeldActionIsStillRefused` (d2mapentity) and the corrupt half
  of `TestAFileHoldingAnActionResumesItAtItsFrame` (d2gamescreen: -1.0 s,
  1e18 s and 1e300 s on a monster and the villager, refused `ENTITY` and
  `NATIVES` at the restore, nothing left in the seam).
- **Playtest -- the review's probe made an act:**
  `TestSaveResumeAHeldActionOutOfReach` (in `save_clock_fight_test.go`, so the
  script count stays 49). N1 saves a `zombie1` mid-A1 (frame 1 + 0.01 s);
  **N2** loads T's files with the swing at -1.0 s -- the probe's file --
  refused `FILE` ("its held "A1" at {Frame:1 Elapsed:-1} is not a point of a
  play"), he begins at dawn, and the game draws on (5 frames, 2 s of real
  drawing); **N3**, 1e18 s: refused `ENTITY` by the composite ("1e+18 s into
  a frame of 0.04 s is no point of a play"), fallen back to dawn, drawing on
  (`pt-targeted-1.txt`).
- **Controls:** **B1a** (the sheet's check off): red, "-Inf s into a frame"
  taken. **B1b** (the composite's check off): red in the unit test ("-1 s
  into a frame of 0.04 s" taken) and **in the game: N3's file was taken and
  the game panicked, `index out of range [-15]`** -- the review's crash, with
  the guard gone (`nc\B1b-composite-takes-any-time.pt.txt`). **B1c** (the
  file's floor off): red, `World.Check` takes "a second before an action's
  frame".

### B2 -- an art change between the save and the load refused the whole night (BUG-92)

BUG-87 made a held action routine in a save, and the load refused `ENTITY`
one whose saved frame the sheet no longer has, or that reads back in another
mode -- so an art pass that re-exported a sheet with fewer frames, or added
one for an action that had fallen back to idle, sent him back to dawn and set
the file aside.

- **The decision (Josh's; the recommended default taken): do not refuse --
  END the action.** The entity resumes where the file puts it, in the state
  its own end reaches: a swing or a blow back to idle (Neutral for an NPC;
  it walks on next frame if it still walks), a death to the Dead pose, a
  corpse, lying where the file says. The end is the game's own
  (`Creature.finishAction`, `NPC.finishHeldAction`, with no callback: the
  game starts every action with none). Not restarted: a restart would replay
  a death that had nearly finished and end it later.
- **The load's own verb:** `Creature.ResumeMotion` and `NPC.ResumeMotion`
  (from `Game.rebuildEntity` and `Game.rekeyNatives`, through
  `restoreMotionExactly`); `RestoreMotion` stays the strict restore the unit
  tests use (unregistered: no game caller, by design). `heldActionMisfit`
  draws the line (next section).
- **The load report says which entity and why:** `ended_actions` -- `id`,
  `who` (a villager's name key; another entity's kind and record), `action`,
  `why` -- and a note, **"an animation could not be resumed exactly"**, in
  `notes` and the log (`Game.noteEndedAction`, `load_ended.go`). The read-back
  is held to the ended motion. **The entity differs from the saved moment by
  design, and only it:** a script compares the rest (`sameWorldExcept` with
  that entity's fields exempt).
- **An NPC** is fitted to its composite mode's frames and frame length
  (animdata's, which an art pass does not re-export, so this is the rarer
  case); a composite that cannot load the saved mode at all is still refused.
- **Unit:** `TestAHeldActionTheArtNoLongerFitsIsEnded` (d2mapentity), one case
  per way an art pass leaves it -- **a death re-exported with fewer frames**
  (12 to 6, saved at frame 8), **a flinch re-exported with more frames** (6
  to 12, saved 0.15 s into a frame of 0.083 s), **a sheet added** for a bite
  that had fallen back to idle, **a sheet taken away**: each resumed and
  ended, never refused; `ended` names the action and the reason; the motion
  returned is the dog's; and it is **the state the saved dog reaches on its
  own when its action plays out** (20 to 51 ticks later), and the same dog on
  every tick for 60 more. The strict restore refuses the frame and time cases
  and reads another mode back for the mode cases (what BUG-87's load
  refused). `TestAFileHoldingAnActionResumesItAtItsFrame` (d2gamescreen),
  four through the load -- a monster's death at a frame its sheet lacks, one
  longer into a frame than one lasts, a bite drawn on idle with a sheet added
  since, the villager's blow at a frame its sheet lacks: each resumed, ended
  (`dead` a corpse, or `idle`), where the file puts it, and
  `LastLoad().EndedActions` and the note name it.
- **Playtest:** `TestSaveResumeAHeldActionOutOfReach` **N4**, the swing at
  frame 99 of A1's 16: resumed; `ended_actions [{A1 29f80080... npc zombie1
  "it was saved at frame 99, and its art has 16"}]`; the note; the fighter in
  NU, not holding, not a corpse; **S_R0 = S_T but for that fighter**
  (`sameWorldExcept`: every system and entity equal, its 4 pose fields
  exempt); 60 frames on.
- **Controls:** **B2a** (the refusal put back: the load's restore strict
  again) -- red in both unit tests ("resumed, not refused") and in the game
  (N4 refused `ENTITY`, "composite: frame 99 of a mode of 16"). **B2b** (a
  time past the whole play taken as an art change) -- red: 1e300 s and 1e18 s
  ended instead of refused, in both unit tests.

### The line between a corrupt file and an art change (B2)

A file cannot say which art it was saved with, so the line is drawn by what
ANY art could have written (`d2mapentity.heldActionMisfit`):

| the file's held action | what | the load |
|---|---|---|
| a negative frame; a time that is not a number, or below the floor (-1e-9 s) | CORRUPT: no art writes it | refused -- `FILE` at step 1 (`World.Check`, `entity-finite`), and at the restore for a World that skipped it |
| an action or mode no mode is named (`creatureModeNamed`, `monsterModeNamed`) | CORRUPT: no sheet in any version has it | refused `ENTITY`/`NATIVES` |
| a time at or past the art's WHOLE play (frames x frame length: one second for every creature sheet, which plays in its default second whatever its frame count) | CORRUPT: no re-export brings a saved time up to it (B1's 1e17, 1e18, 1e300 s) | refused `ENTITY`/`NATIVES` (`heldActionMisfit` leaves it to the restore; `SetProgress` refuses it) |
| drawn in another mode than saved (a sheet added for an action that fell back to idle, or taken away) | THE ART CHANGED | resumed, the action ENDED, noted |
| a frame at or past the art's frame count (a sheet with fewer frames) | THE ART CHANGED | resumed, ENDED, noted |
| a time at or past one frame, short of the whole play (a sheet with MORE frames has shorter ones) | THE ART CHANGED | resumed, ENDED, noted |
| anything else | the art fits | resumed at its saved point, exactly |

**A deviation from the brief's letter, taken on purpose (Josh's call):** the
brief put "an out-of-range elapsed per B1" -- at or past one frame's length --
on the corrupt side. But a sheet re-exported with more frames has shorter
frames (a creature's plays in one second whatever its count), so a saved time
can outlast a frame of the new art: an art change, which B2's decision says
not to refuse. So the load refuses only what no art could have written (below
the floor, or past the whole play) and ends the rest; the crash and the stall
B1 found are all past the whole play, and still refused. `SetProgress` itself
refuses anything at or past one frame, as the brief asked; the load never
hands it one.

### BUG-89 and BUG-90 -- a turn no longer restarts an action (closed)

- **BUG-89 (the recommended default: keep the frame through a turn).**
  `Creature.rotate` returns on the facing the creature already has -- the
  unchanged-facing guard `NPC.rotate` has always had, and `mapEntity.setTarget`
  calls the directioner at every waypoint -- and on a real turn puts the
  sheet back at the frame and time it held (`Progress` before `SetDirection`,
  `SetProgress` after), as an NPC's composite mode keeps its frame. A dog's
  bite across a waypoint where its route bends lasts 61 ticks, as standing
  (121 when turned 20 ticks in, before).
- **BUG-90, folded in.** `Composite.SetDirection` puts each layer back at the
  frame and time it held after the layer's own `SetDirection`
  (`keepProgress`), so the layers stay in step with the mode through a turn
  -- the frame a load sets them to -- and the restore is exact with nothing
  added to the file. **Its row is corrected (C5):** a turn reset the DCC and
  PNG layers; a DC6 layer's `SetDirection` already kept its frame
  (`dc6_animation.go:72-90`).
- **No combat figure moved.** Blows are the combat model's (`Game.Animate`
  passes no callback) and a death does not turn (`halt`), so only how long a
  swing or a flinch is DRAWN changes. `figures.py` compares each script's own
  log lines -- ids, paths and times normalized, every figure kept -- between
  BUG-87's full suite and this burst's targeted run: `TestCombatResolver` and
  its two siblings, `TestTacticalFight`, the four `TestAFightHeIsNotIn*`,
  `TestTheDeadWalk`, `TestSpawns`, `TestRising`, `TestDeath`,
  `TestASlainWalkerLiesWhereHeFell` and `TestSaveResumeMidAction`: **the
  same, line for line** (the two clock-fight save scripts differ only in the
  retry wording, C2) (`figures.txt`).
- **Unit:** `TestABiteAcrossAWaypointLastsItsSheet` (a bend and a straight
  route: 61 ticks, as standing), `TestATurnKeepsTheSheetsFrame`,
  **`TestAWaypointOnTheSameFacingDoesNotTurnTheSheet`** (the guard: a dog
  biting along a straight route passes 3 waypoints and its sheet is asked to
  turn no time) (d2mapentity); `TestATurnKeepsTheLayersFrames` (d2asset: a
  composite mid-swing turned keeps every layer's frame and time, and is the
  one a load rebuilds from its progress, tick for tick, for 60).
- **Controls:** **B89a** (the guard removed): red, the sheet asked to turn 3
  times. **B89b** (the frame not kept): red, the bite 67 ticks through the
  bend, and a turn put the dog's death back to frame 0. **B90** (a turn
  resets the layers): red, the layers at frame 0 where they were at 8.

### C1 -- kept evenings from before 0.14.4 no longer compare (BUG-93)

BUG-88 put `clock_strip_hours_to_dusk` back in the digest's world part and
0.14.4 added `held_frame`/`held_elapsed`, so every evening kept before them
compares red with a sound load -- and a control run on one
(`STRIGOI_SAVE_RESUME_FROM`) went red for that reason, not its own.

- **Documented** in `docs/harness.md` (0.14.4, and the knob's paragraph).
- **Stamped:** `keepEvening` writes `harness.json` -- the harness version,
  from `strigoi_ping` -- and a run given a kept evening (`TestSaveResume`
  acts 4-6, `TestSaveResumeNegatives`) refuses one another harness kept, or
  one kept before the stamp, **before it compares anything**
  (`eveningHarness`), worded so no one reads it as the control's red: "THE
  KEPT EVENING IS STALE -- NOT A RED OF THIS TEST: ... was kept by a harness
  before the stamp (no harness.json; 0.14.3 or earlier), and this game is
  harness 0.14.4 ... Keep a new one".
- **Re-kept:** this burst's green `TestSaveResume` kept an evening, copied to
  `wt-held-fix\evening` (0.14.4; the shared `pt\TestSaveResume\evening` is
  overwritten by any branch's run).
- **Checked, under the controls' hold:** `TestSaveResume` from the new
  evening -- stamp taken, acts 4-6g green, S_R0 = S_T at 694408d3cdf2
  (`nc\C1-fresh-evening-taken.pt.txt`); from `wt-merge\evening` -- refused
  as stale at once (`nc\C1-stale-evening-refused.pt.txt`).
- **Control C1** (the check off, the same stale evening): red at act 4, "the
  resumed world is not the saved one (resume digest 694408d3cdf2, want
  105f85c7838c)", on `ui.clock_strip_hours_to_dusk` and the rest of the old
  ui -- the review's numbers, and the wrong reason the stamp now prevents
  (`nc\C1-stamp-check-off.pt.txt`).

### C2 -- an assertion green by design (the retry count)

`scSaveAtT` fails fatally on any refusal and returned a retry count that was
always 0, which both scripts then required to be 0. **Removed:** it returns
the save's result alone; the scripts' `retries != 0` checks are gone and
their lines say "on the first try"; the teeth are the Fatalf.
**Control C2** (BUG-76's refusal put back: the save refused `FIGHTING` while
any monster or villager holds an action): `TestSaveResumeAFightHeIsNotIn`
red at V1, "the save at T was refused ... FIGHTING: NC C2: a monster or
villager still holds an action" (`nc\C2-held-action-refuses-the-save.pt.txt`).

### C3 -- no playtest control showed the in-play comparison biting after a real load

BUG-87's controls went red at the file-versus-report check (C1b, C2, C3b)
or at the load's read-back (C1a, C3a); none reached the comparison IN PLAY
of an NPC resumed by a real load, and `held_frame` is read from the same
`Composite.Progress` the snapshot uses.

- **Control C3** (`Composite.Progress` drops the time into the frame): the
  file, the entity's report and the read-back all agree on 0, so the save,
  the file check, the entity's own report after the load and **S_R0 = S_T
  all pass** -- and `TestSaveResumeMidAction` goes red **seven frames on**
  (M-A1, S_M: `zombie1`'s `held_frame` 4 saved, 3 resumed; `fallen1`'s GH
  the same), the resumed swing a frame behind
  (`nc\C3-progress-drops-the-time.pt.txt`). Its unit test,
  `TestACompositeResumesAtItsProgress`, is red too.
- **The table corrected** (BUG-87's "Negative controls", above): C2 was red in
  the unit tests at the load's refusal, but in the playtest at the
  file-versus-report check, before any load.

### C4 -- `action_at`'s own fields named in the shape sweep

`TestAFieldTheFileLacksIsRefused` lists `entities[0].motion.action_at.frame`
and `.elapsed` (frame 0 and time 0 are a real point, so a file without them
must not read as the action's first frame). **Control C4** (`frame` made
`omitempty`, so a file without it reads as frame 0): red on
`entities[0].motion.action_at.frame`.

### C5 -- BUG-90's row, and the guard

BUG-90's row said a turn resets every layer; a DC6 layer keeps its frame --
corrected in place. The unchanged-facing guard is BUG-89's above (control
B89a).

### C6 -- the dusk minute read "Sunset in 0.0h" (BUG-94)

BUG-88's floor makes the whole of 19:45 count 0 minutes to dusk; the HUD's
old sample, a frame into the minute, read a hair under 24 h -- "24.0h".
**Fixed, the recommended option:** `HoursToDusk` is in (0, 24] -- a count of 0
is a day away -- so 19:45 reads 24.0 h, as in play before BUG-88.
**The rounding is kept:** the minute's start is the more correct reading, and
the text moves one tenth on the minutes whose two readings round apart
(x.x5 h; 00:00 reads "19.8h", was "19.7h"). **Measured** by
`TestTheReadoutsTextOverADay` -- a whole day sampled as the HUD samples it, on
the first frame of each minute at the 1/60 s tick: the text moves by exactly
one tenth on **119 of 1,440 minutes** (the review's estimate: about 121) and
on no other, and 19:45 reads "24.0" in both. `TestHoursToDusk` pins 19:45 at
24 (it pinned 0). `TestClockHUD` green. **Control C6** (a count of 0 left 0):
red, "1185.0000: 0 in (0, 24]".

## Found on the way

- **A read-back refusal printed `action_at` as a pointer** (BUG-87's "What
  was not done": "ActionAt:0x344befc40cc0"). `restoreMotionExactly` now
  prints both motions as the file writes them (JSON), so a refusal shows the
  frame and the time that differed.
- **A composite's layer can be an ulp shorter per frame than its mode**
  (`TestACompositesLayersTakeEveryTimeItsModeTakes`: at a D2 speed of 10.24 s
  and 29 frames), so the layers are set unchecked after the mode's check --
  otherwise B1's own bound would have refused a time the game holds.

## Decisions (the recommended option each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B87R-1 (B2): an art-mismatched held action is ENDED, not refused**, and
  not restarted. Options not taken: restart it at its first frame (a death
  nearly done would fall again and lie later); keep the refusal and leave it
  to B5's menu message.
- **B87R-2: the corrupt line sits at what NO art could write** -- below the
  floor, a negative frame, past the art's whole play, an unnamed mode -- and
  a time past one frame but short of the whole play is an art change (the
  deviation above).
- **B87R-3: `d2save` bounds the floor and the frame's sign only**; the frame
  length is the art's, which the file check does not know.
- **B87R-4: BUG-89 and BUG-90 take the recommended default**: a turn keeps
  the frame (creature sheet, composite layers), and the creature's rotate
  gains the unchanged-facing guard.
- **B87R-5 (C1): the kept evening is stamped with the harness version**, and
  a mismatch is a refusal of the input, before any comparison.
- **B87R-6 (C6): 19:45 reads 24.0 h; the tenth-of-an-hour rounding is kept.**
- **B87R-7: harness 0.14.4 amended, not bumped** (the branch has not merged;
  B5 may bump at the merge). The digest's shape is unchanged; the load report
  gains `ended_actions`.

## Register

`Creature.RestoreMotion` and `NPC.RestoreMotion`'s rows become
`Creature.ResumeMotion` and `NPC.ResumeMotion` (the load's callers moved;
`RestoreMotion` has none in the game now, by design). Added, wire/live:
`ActionProgress.Check`, `heldActionMisfit`, `Game.noteEndedAction`. The
`Composite.SetProgress`, `Animation.SetProgress`, `Game.rebuildEntity` and
`Clock.HoursToDusk` rows updated.

## What the review fixes did NOT do

- **No NPC unit test with a real composite** (the MPQs): the NPC's ended
  action is carried by the playtest's N4; its refusals by N3.
- **An NPC whose composite cannot load the saved mode at all is still
  refused** (not ended): the composite's modes are the MPQs', not a sheet an
  art pass re-exports.
- **`TestSaveResumeNegatives` was not run** (opt-in, and not in the brief's
  list); its stale-evening refusal is the same `eveningHarness` that
  `TestSaveResume` ran.
- **A creature walking when its action is ended** resumes at the file's
  position and walks on from there; the saved creature would have walked on
  during the rest of its swing (a swing does not stop a walk). Only a death
  halts. Not a divergence the report hides: the entity is named.

# B5: the save reaches the player (branch `save-b5`, 29 Sep 2026)

Josh's ruling is that the save "stops at that point then resumes at that
point". B1-B4b built the file, the verb (`Game.SaveWorld`) and the load, all
merged and green; until this burst only the harness's `strigoi_save_game`
could make a save. B5 gives the save its callers in the game and tells him,
in plain words, what the save and the load did:

- **the escape menu** (the build plan's rules 1 and 2): SAVE GAME, SAVE AND
  EXIT GAME, and -- when a save is refused -- the reason under the entries and
  EXIT WITHOUT SAVING;
- **the close hook** (rule 3): the window's close button and Alt-F4 do what
  SAVE AND EXIT GAME does, and never hang the close;
- **the dawn autosave** (rule 10, ruled 27 Sep): every dawn he lives to see
  saves the game; refused, it is PENDING and taken on the first frame that
  saves, that day;
- **the load's notices** (rule 7): a save set aside, or resumed with a
  villager gone, is said on the HUD at the start of play.

The code is `d2game/d2gamescreen/save_player.go` (the game's half: the
menu's questions, the close hook's game half, the autosave's state machine,
the words, the load's notice, the `save` provider),
`d2game/d2player/escape_menu.go` (the menu) and `save_notice.go` (the HUD's
line), `d2app/close.go` (the App's close hook) and
`d2core/d2render/ebiten/ebiten_renderer.go` (the window's close). `save.go` --
`SaveWorld`, its refusals, its order -- is untouched: every caller goes
through `Game.SaveWorldAs`, a wrapper beside it.

## What the player sees

**The escape menu (Esc).** Its entries, in Strigoi's font set (Uncial
Antiqua for the entries, IM Fell for the line under them):

| Moment | Entries | The line under them |
|---|---|---|
| a save can be made | OPTIONS, **SAVE GAME**, **SAVE AND EXIT GAME**, RETURN TO GAME | (none) |
| a save is refused | OPTIONS, SAVE GAME, **EXIT WITHOUT SAVING**, RETURN TO GAME | the refusal in words, then "Leave now and you come back to your last save." |

- SAVE GAME: saved, the menu closes and he plays on, and the HUD says "Game
  saved." for three seconds; refused, the menu stays and says why.
- SAVE AND EXIT GAME: saved, the main menu. A save that fails (a write, not a
  refusal) keeps him in the menu, says "The game could not be saved: its
  files could not be written. / Your last save stands." and turns the entry to
  EXIT WITHOUT SAVING, so leaving unsaved is always his choice.
- EXIT WITHOUT SAVING: the main menu at once; the world file stays the last
  save, and the next load resumes it (rule 2: "you come back to your last
  save").
- The world is paused under the menu (the 12 Sep ruling), so the answer the
  menu got as it opened holds while it is up; a held action (a death still
  playing) stays held too, so the menu's advice is to return and try again in
  a moment.

Screenshots: `strigoi-harness-runs\pt\TestTheMenuSaves\<run>\b5-menu-savable-*.png`
and `b5-menu-refused-*.png` (rewritten by every run of the script).

**The refusals, in words** (`saveRefusalWords`; the code is the log's and the
harness's, never the player's):

| Code (`SaveWorld`) | He reads |
|---|---|
| `COMBAT` (since 30 Sep: the combat status -- his fight, a hostile chasing him, his swing or reaction, the grace after) | You can't save in combat. |
| `FIGHTING`, his fight running (until 30 Sep: it is `COMBAT` now, and these words went with it) | You can't save during a fight. |
| `FIGHTING`, no fight of his (its end not yet applied, a swing or a death still playing, a monster's held action: BUG-76, BUG-87 -- the held-action refusal remained until BUG-87's branch, `save-held`, merged and dropped it -- 29 Sep, "Merge: save-held + B5" below; the B5 review fixes) | You can't save while blows are still landing. / Try again in a moment. |
| `TALKING` | You can't save while you are talking. |
| `JOURNAL` | You can't save with your journal open. |
| `LOADOUT` | You can't save until you have chosen your gear. |
| `DEAD` | The dead can't save. |
| `NETWORK` | A network game can't be saved. |
| `NOT_READY` | You can't save yet: the game is still starting. |
| `NOT_READY`, "this game opened no hero save" (the B5 review fixes, C4) | This game has no save file to keep it in. |
| `NOT_READY`, "he has no kit file to save beside him" (the B5 review fixes, C4) | You can't save until your gear is in place. |
| any other (a code this build has never heard of) | You can't save right now. |

In practice the menu meets FIGHTING, LOADOUT and NETWORK: the journal and a
talk take Esc for themselves, and a dead man's Esc is the death screen's.

**The HUD's notices** (`GameControls.SaveNotice`: gold on a dark band at
y 438, above the journal's line, Font16 -- IM Fell):

| When | He reads | For |
|---|---|---|
| SAVE GAME saved | Game saved. | 3 s |
| the dawn's autosave taken on its own frame | Dawn. The game is saved. | 3 s |
| a pending autosave taken later that day | The dawn's save is made. | 3 s |
| night fell before any frame would save | The dawn's save could not be made today. Your last save stands. | 3 s |
| the autosave's write failed | The dawn's save could not be written. Your last save stands. | 3 s |
| a load refused and set aside (rule 7) | (why, below) / You wake at dawn. The save is kept, set aside -- not deleted. | 8 s |
| ...and the file could not be moved | (why) / You wake at dawn. The save is left where it was, unread. | 8 s |
| a load that took a villager the file lacks off the map | Your save is restored. A villager who was gone when you saved is gone again. (or "%d villagers...") | 8 s |

The load's "why" (`loadRefusalWords`): VERSION "Your save is from another
version of the game."; FILE "Your save could not be read."; NETWORK "A
network game does not resume your save."; HERO "The save beside this hero is
another hero's."; TORN "Your save was cut off while it was being written.";
SIDECAR "Your save could not be put in place."; MAP "The village has changed
since you saved."; NATIVES "The villagers are not the ones you saved with.";
SEED, ENTITY, BLOCK and anything else "Your last night could not be
restored." (since the B5 review fixes, C5: "Your last save could not be
restored.", right at any hour; a network game's and a torn save resumed
from its `.bak` have words of their own -- "B5 review fixes" below). Screenshot: `pt\TestTheLoadNotice\<run>\b5-load-notice-*.png`.

**The help screen (H)** gained an eleventh line: "Esc, then SAVE GAME: saved
where you stand. Every dawn you see saves too." (`strigoi_help.go`; the
overlay's `bullets` is 11, the last at y 259, still above Diablo II's
callouts at 355; `TestStrigoiHelpNamesTheKeys` requires "SAVE GAME" and
"Every dawn").

## The menu (rules 1 and 2)

- `d2player.MenuSaver` is the menu's one view of the save: `SaveRefusedNow()`
  (why not, in words, or "") and `SaveFromMenu(exit)` (saved, or not and why).
  The menu never sees a code, so a refusal the save gains or loses changes
  nothing in it. `CreateGame` hands the game screen to the menu
  (`EscapeMenu.SetSaver`).
- `EscapeMenu.open` asks `SaveRefusedNow` BEFORE the layout is set, so the
  entries are placed around the line they show; `showSave` sets the exit
  entry's words and the note's lines (three labels, each centred: a d2gui
  label of two lines draws them left-aligned).
- `Game.SaveRefusedNow` is the save's own refusal (`saveRefusal`), asked and
  not acted on: a read.
- **"Save and Quit"** is the plan's SAVE AND EXIT GAME (rule 1: "The menu has
  SAVE GAME (keep playing) and SAVE AND EXIT GAME"). It was there, and saved
  only his hero (BUG-96); it saves the moment now. SAVE GAME is new.

## The close hook (rule 3)

- `ebiten.SetWindowClosingHandled(true)` when the App has a hook
  (`Renderer.SetCloseHandler`, from `App.Run`). `Renderer.Update` asks
  `ebiten.IsWindowBeingClosed()` before the frame advances; if so it calls the
  hook and returns `ebiten.Termination` (a clean exit of `RunGame`). No frame
  of the game advances once the window is closing. Before B5 the close ended
  the process with no `OnUnload` at all (BUG-95).
- `App.closeTheGame` (`d2app/close.go`) runs once: the game in play, if any,
  goes through `Game.CloseGame(10 s)`, and `App.closed` then stops every
  frame and every draw (the screen it unloaded is still the screen manager's
  current one).
- `Game.CloseGame` runs the hook on a goroutine of its own while the caller
  waits for nothing else: `SaveWorldAs(close)` -- SaveWorld, the world file,
  his `.od2`, his sidecar, in B3's order, the generation in both -- and then
  the screen's own `OnUnload`, which writes his `.od2` and sidecar as it always
  has (of the SAME generation: SaveWorld set it) and closes the client. A
  refused save (a fight) still unloads: his `.od2` and sidecar are written of
  the LAST save's generation, so the world file still pairs with them and the
  next load resumes the last save, its embedded sidecar over the newer one --
  rule 3's "you come back to your last save", measured (`TestTheCloseHook`
  act 3). A panic inside the hook is a report, not a crash of the close.
- **It never hangs the close.** Past the limit the caller stops waiting and
  the process leaves. B3's order is what makes that safe: a save cut off
  between its files is a sidecar of another generation, refused TORN at the
  next load and set aside (rule 7), never half-resumed.
- **The harness's `strigoi_quit{graceful: true}`** runs the same
  `App.closeTheGame` on the game goroutine, then exits: the playtests' way to
  the close (a script cannot press a window's X). What they do not reach is
  the two ebiten lines; those are read, not tested.

## The dawn autosave (rule 10)

**Armed** in `earnExperience`, on the frame `nightSurvived` pays the night and
`dawnWatch` settles the promised watch (the plan's section 7: "after
`dawnWatch` has settled the night (the same frame `nightSurvived` pays)").
Not in a network game (rule 9: never saved), and not under the harness's
dial. **Tried at the END of that frame** (`advanceAutosave`, `Advance`'s last
line): after the night's experience, the watch, the journal's dawn lines, the
map's animations and the controls -- the state a harness save sees between
frames, so "the moment it was taken" is a moment a relaunch can be held to.

| State | Means | Leaves by |
|---|---|---|
| `idle` | no dawn has armed one in this game | a dawn: `pending` |
| `pending` | armed; tried at the end of every frame | a frame that saves: `taken` (by `dawn`); his own save while it waits: `taken` (by `menu`, `harness`...) -- `cover`; night falls first: `dropped`; a save that is not a refusal fails: `failed` |
| `taken` | the day's save is made | the next dawn: `pending` |
| `dropped` | night fell with every frame refused; a log line (Warning) and a quiet notice | the next dawn |
| `failed` | the write failed; logged (Error) and a notice; **never retried** -- a disk that refused a write would otherwise be written every frame | the next dawn |
| `off` | the harness's dial `save.autosave` false | the dial on, and the next dawn |

- **Any refusal is "not now".** The machine never reads the code: FIGHTING,
  TALKING, a monster's held action (B4b's refusal, BUG-87's shape) and a code
  this build has never heard of all leave it pending (the unit test runs
  four codes, one of them invented). So when `save-held` drops the held-action
  refusal, nothing here changes: the autosave is simply taken sooner.
  (Merged 29 Sep, "Merge: save-held + B5" below: nothing changed here. The playtest's
  autosave came 64 frames after the fight ended, the last refusal his own
  swing still playing, where B5 alone measured 60 frames, the last refusal
  the dog's death: with the death no longer refused, his swing is what the
  moment after a fight waits on.)
- **It outlasts BUG-87.** Measured on master: a clock fight refuses 92-93% of
  its frames in runs of up to 392 (6.53 s). The unit test holds it pending
  through 1000 refused frames in each of dawn, day and dusk, tried on every
  one and on none after it took; the playtest's fight across first light was
  refused 560 frames and the autosave was taken 60 frames after the fight
  ended (the dog's death still playing).
- **The window is "that day": until night falls** (`autosaveWindowOpen`:
  dawn, day, dusk; NightStart, 21:15, ends it). The plan's section 7: "it
  saves at the first moment that day when saving is allowed. It does not skip
  the day." (Decision B5-2 below, and a question for Josh.)
- **At most one per dawn.** `arm` replaces the last dawn's state; the file
  carries `dawn_paid_day` and `last_stage`, so a game resumed at dawn arms
  nothing (measured, `TestTheDawnAutosave` act 2).
- **A save of his covers it.** A menu save, a close or a harness save made
  while it waits is the day's save: the autosave is `taken` by it, instead of
  writing the same moment again a frame later.
- **Log lines** (`strigoi.log`): `AUTOSAVE armed: the dawn of day N`;
  `... pending (N refused frames; CODE: reason)` on the first refusal and
  every 60th; `... saved` / `... saved after N refused frames (the last
  CODE: reason)`; `... dropped -- night fell after N refused frames ...; the
  last save stands` (Warning); `... the save failed: ...` (Error); and
  `... is taken by the menu save`.
- It is not in the world file: at every save it is not pending (the save
  covers it), and a resumed game's own state is derived (`idle`, and the
  file's `dawn_paid_day`). `TestGameFieldsClassified` labels the four new
  `Game` fields D.

## The load's notices (rule 7)

`Game.noticeTheLoad` runs once the controls are bound (`bindGameControls`)
and reads `LastLoad()` -- the report of the load that opened this game,
including a refusal the dawn that replaced a torn-down game keeps (B4a's
`carried`). A game whose own first frame is about to tear its load down says
nothing; the dawn that replaces it says it. The report gained `dropped`
(`LoadReport.Dropped`, filled beside BUG-79's note in `rekeyNatives`): the
villagers the map built and the file lacked. Of the report's notes, only
that one is told: a label this build words otherwise (BUG-80) changes nothing
he could see. `preload` (`recovered`, `kept`) is not told (below).

## The harness (0.15.0)

- **`save` provider** (registered with the game screen): `autosave` (`state`,
  `day`, `tries`, `last_refusal`, `last_reason`, `by`, `taken_at`, `error`,
  `enabled`), `last_save` (`by`, `result`, `code`, `reason`, `words`, `at`,
  `day`, `world_path`, `saved_at`), `saves`, `load_notice`, and `refused_now`
  / `reason_now` / `words_now`. `autosave`, `load_notice` and the `*_now`
  fields are in the digest's `process` part; `last_save` and `saves` -- the
  record of the calls a script made -- are in neither part (the first full
  suite went red at `TestSaveResume` 7a, "saving moved the world: the digest
  parts [process] changed", when the record was in the process part: a save
  that moves nothing must not move the digest by being written down). One
  DIAL, re-applied by a load: `autosave` (bool).
- **`strigoi_quit{graceful}`**: the close hook, reported as `close`.
- **`strigoi_save_game`** goes through `Game.SaveWorldAs` (by `harness`).
- **`ui`**: `escape_menu` (`open`, `layout`, `entries`, `selected`, `note`,
  `exit_refused`) and `save_notice`, both in the process part.
- **`strigoi_get_game_info`**: the load report's `dropped`.
- The tool count is unchanged (37); `docs/harness.md` has each.

## TestSaveResume and the dawn

`TestSaveResume`'s subject is the save IT makes at T: act 1 crosses day 1's
dawn before T, and acts 3 and 6 cross day 2's after it. With the autosave on,
the dawn after T wrote its own moment over T's file (act 4 would load the
dawn, and 6b's "load last save" too). Its dial lists (`startDials`,
`huntedDials`, `quickResolvedDials`) now carry `autosaveOffDial`
(`save.autosave` false), so a relaunch and "load last save" re-apply it with
the rest, and `keptEvening` adds it to an evening kept before B5. Its act 3
comment is corrected: the script leaves through the harness's `navigate`,
which since B5 is what EXIT WITHOUT SAVING does -- SAVE AND EXIT GAME would
write the world file first. No other script in the suite needed the dial
(the full suite, below).

- `TestTheDawnAutosaveStateMachine`: idle asks nothing; taken on its own
  frame; pending through 1000 refused frames (four codes, one unknown) in
  dawn, day and dusk, then taken; dropped at night with nothing asked; failed
  and never retried; covered by his save.
- `TestTheDawnAutosaveOnAGame`: the glue on a savable unit game -- armed,
  refused by a real refusal (the fight's edge), pending with its code, no file
  written, the dial off and on, the provider's process part.
- `TestEveryRefusalIsSaidInWords`: every save and load code in words of its

  code "not now".
- `TestTheLoadsNotice`: every case of the load's notice, rule 7's "not
  deleted" among them.
- `TestTheMenuAsksTheSave`: the menu's two questions -- nothing refused, then
  a talk: the words, SAVE GAME and SAVE AND EXIT not made, nothing written.
- `TestTheCloseHookNeverBlocks`: a refused close writes no world file and
  still unloads; a panic is a report; an unload that never returns is left

  `TestEverySettableFieldIsADialOrNamedState` (d2app, harness tag: `save.

Every comparison is the resume digest and each system's state (`snapWorld`,
`sameWorld`), as `TestSaveResume` compares them.

## Decisions (the recommended option taken each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B5-1, "Save and Quit" is SAVE AND EXIT GAME**, the plan's rule 1 word for
  word; SAVE GAME is new beside it; refused, the exit entry is EXIT WITHOUT
  SAVING (rule 2).
- **B5-2, a pending autosave waits until night falls** (the plan's section 7:
  "that day ... It does not skip the day"), not until the next band edge --
  sunrise, 04:15, 22 real seconds after first light -- as the brief
  recommended. A raid's fights at first light can outlast 22 seconds (BUG-87:
  one clock fight refused for 6.5 s; a village's fights one after another
  for as long as they run), and a dropped autosave costs him the day: a crash
  that evening would cost a night and a day. For Josh (below).
- **B5-3, the autosave is taken at the end of the frame**, after everything
  the dawn's frame did, not inside `earnExperience`: the moment saved is the
  one a relaunch is held to.
- **B5-4, every refusal is "not now"; a failure is not.** The machine never
  reads a code (so the held-action refusal can go without a line here); a save
  that failed for any other reason is `failed` and not retried.
- **B5-5, a save of his covers a waiting autosave** -- the menu's, the
  close's and the harness's -- rather than a second write of the same day.
- **B5-6, the close hook is SAVE AND EXIT's save and then the screen's own
  unload**, under a 10 s limit on a goroutine of its own; a refused close
  still unloads (his `.od2` and sidecar, as ever, of the last save's
  generation).
- **B5-7, the harness's graceful quit is the close's test path**; the two
  ebiten lines are read, not tested.
- **B5-8, `save.autosave` is a harness dial**, off only in `TestSaveResume`
  (and its kept evenings); the suite otherwise runs with the autosave on, as
  the game ships.
- **B5-9, the load tells two things**: a refusal (why, and that the file is
  kept) and villagers gone. Not a renamed label (BUG-80) or a `.preload`
  recovered or kept.
- **B5-10, the words are keyed on the code**, and FIGHTING is split by
  whether HIS fight runs ("a moment" otherwise, B4b-R1's advice to B5).
- **B5-11, the look was measured on screenshots**: Diablo II's brown for the
  menu's line read too dark over the village at noon, so it is a pale gold,
  one label a line.
- **B5-12, no autosave is armed in a network game** (rule 9 refuses every
  network save; arming one would retry all day).
- **B5-13, the save provider's record is in no digest part**: `last_save`
  and `saves` log calls; `autosave`, `load_notice` and the `*_now` fields are
  process state.

The nineteen rows deferred to "M4.6 B5" -- every `Snapshot` the save takes,

`gameSpawner.Snapshot`, `Game.SaveWorld`, `d2save.Encode`, `d2save.WriteWorld`,
`Creature.CreatureID`, `Player.Facing` -- moved to WIRE, each with what it said
before recorded ("Deferred to M4.6 B5 and measured harness-only until M4.6
B5"). New wire rows: `Game.SaveWorldAs`, `Game.SaveFromMenu`,
`Game.SaveRefusedNow`, `EscapeMenu.SetSaver`, `EscapeMenu.saveGame`,
`EscapeMenu.saveAndExit`, `GameControls.SaveNotice`, `Game.CloseGame`,
`App.closeTheGame`, `App.onWindowClose`, `Renderer.SetCloseHandler` (a new

## Found on the way

- **The escape menu's layout ids are not what their declaration reads.**
  `noLayoutID layoutID = iota - 2` sits in a const block after five UI
  constants, so it is 3, not -2, and `mainLayoutID` is 5. Harmless (nothing
  depends on the sign); B5's `saveGameLayoutID` is -3, apart from all of them,
  and the harness reports the layout by name (`main`).
- **A save's record moved the digest (the first full suite).** The `save`
  provider first put `last_save` and `saves` in the digest's process part, so
  every save changed the digest by being recorded: `TestSaveResume` 7a and the
  two clock-fight scripts' V1 ("saving moved the world: the digest parts
  [process] changed") went red. The record of the calls a script made is in
  neither part now, like the harness's own call list (decision B5-13).
- **Measured for the fight across first light:** at the shipped graze factor
  a `fallen1` dies in four rounds, before 02:45 from 02:40; the script's dog
  takes no damage until it is made to (`graze_factor` 0, then crits).

- **The world is paused under the menu, and so are the held actions**: a
  death still playing when he opens the menu is still playing when he closes
  it, so the menu's advice for that refusal is "Try again in a moment", not
  "wait here".

## What B5 did NOT do (B6's, or later)

- **B6's closeout**: `TestSaveResumeNegatives` (the omit sweep) and the
  milestone's docs and bugs.
- **The console's `quit`** (the developer's `~` console) still exits at once,
  without the close hook. (Done by the B5 review fixes, below: BUG-102.)
- **The death screen's words** ("All since he last left the world is lost.")
  predate the save: since B5 all since his last SAVE is lost. A words pass.
  (Done by the B5 review fixes, below: "All since his last save is lost.")
- **Alt-F4 and the X button are not driven by a test** (the plan's section 8
  asked whether Alt-F4 goes through ebiten's close handling; it is the same
  close request to the window, read in the ebiten API, not measured).
- **No notice for a `.preload`** recovered or kept after a crash; the log and
  the load report carry it.
- **BUG-87 is not fixed here** (`save-held` carries the held action); the
  pending autosave is robust to it either way, and the menu's "Try again in a
  moment" covers its second or so. (Merged 29 Sep: "Merge: save-held + B5" below.)
- **No "saving..." sign** while an autosave waits: rule 10's notice is quiet
  by design, and says when it is made.
- The menu does not darken the world behind it (Diablo II's own menu did
  not).

## For the plan (the main session's to write into the build plan)

- **Section 5, B5's row: DONE** -- the escape menu's SAVE GAME, SAVE AND EXIT
  GAME and EXIT WITHOUT SAVING, the refusal in words; the close hook
