# B5 review fixes (branch `save-b5`, 29 Sep 2026)

The independent review of B5 (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-rev-b5\review.md`,
its probe `zz_rev_b5_probe_test.go.txt` and log `probe-1.txt`) found two A
defects that would cost Josh real progress, two B defects, a gap in the
tests' teeth and eight C items. Josh's rulings frame every fix: the save must
"stop at that point then resume at that point", and **a save or a refused
load must never cost him his progress**. Each finding below is fixed, tested,
and shown red by a negative control; the logs are in
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b5-fix\`. Bugs BUG-97 to BUG-104 (`docs/bugs.md`).

## Rule 3, as built now

> **Closing the window does what SAVE AND EXIT does. Only his live fight,
> his death or a network game makes it leave without saving -- and then he
> comes back to his last save.**
>
> **Reworded by the combat status (30 Sep 2026):** Closing the window does
> what SAVE AND EXIT does. Only **combat** -- his live fight, or a hostile
> chasing him -- his death or a network game makes it leave without saving,
> and then he comes back to his last save. The few seconds after combat (the
> grace, his last swing or reaction) are waited out, and then it saves.

- The close first **ends what only holds the screen**: a talk
  (`Game.EndTalk`) and his journal (`journalOpen = false`), exactly as a
  death ends them (`noticeDeath`). Neither is anything the world file keeps.
- It then **lets the moment after a fight settle**: while the save is
  refused `FIGHTING` with no fight of his (the fight's end not yet applied,
  his last swing, a blow or a death still playing), it runs frames -- up to
  eight seconds of game time and never more than four seconds of the
  close's wall time -- and saves as soon as the refusal clears.
- **What is left that leaves unsaved**: his live fight (`FIGHTING` with his
  fight running), his death (`DEAD`), a network game (`NETWORK`). A game
  still starting (`NOT_READY`) or his gear not yet chosen (`LOADOUT`) is
  refused too and loses nothing: the world has been held, or not yet built,
  since the game opened. The kit panel and the help screen are not refusals
  at all (they hold neither the world nor the save), so a close with either
  open simply saves.
- **Until save-held merges**, one more: a village's fight whose held actions
  (BUG-87: 6.53 s at most measured at the shipped round) outlast the
  settle's eight seconds. save-held drops that refusal; after the merge the
  settle only waits out his own swing and the fight's end, a frame or two.
  (Merged 29 Sep, "Merge: save-held + B5" below: measured 65 settle frames in
  `TestTheCloseHook` act 6 -- his own swing and the fight's end; the eight
  seconds are kept.)

## The TORN recovery, as built now

> **A save cut off between its files resumes the save before it.**

A save writes the world file, then his `.od2`, then his sidecar. A save that
stops after the world file landed -- a crash, a power cut, a close past its
limit, a write that failed and could not be put back -- leaves the new world
file beside the last save's sidecar, and the last save itself as the world
file's `.bak` (WriteWorld keeps it before writing). The load refuses the world
file `TORN` (its moment is not his sidecar's). **When the `.bak` is his
sidecar's moment** -- this hero's, and `SameMoment` with his sidecar, every
check step 1 runs on a world file -- the torn file is set aside as
`N.od2.world.json.torn.unread` (kept, never deleted), the `.bak` is put in
its place, and the load goes on with it. He is told: "Your save was cut off
while it was being written." / "The save before it is restored. The cut-off
save is kept, set aside -- not deleted." (`LoadReport.FromBak`; the load's
`notes` say what was done). A `.bak` that is not his sidecar's moment is not
resumed, and the load is refused `TORN` as before (rule 7: set aside, dawn).
`PeekLoad` (the seed a start is held to) sees the `.bak` too.

## Findings, fixes, tests and controls

Unit controls are source mutations applied as **go build overlays**
(`go test -overlay`, `nc_unit.py`, `nc-unit.sh`): each mutated copy is under
`wt-b5-fix\nc\<id>\` and the worktree is never changed, so the reach gate
and the playtests reading it at the same time saw the real source. Playtest
controls are the same, built into the game by the launcher
(`STRIGOI_HARNESS_OVERLAY`, new: `playtest/launcher.go`), run in three
batches whose mutations each go red in a different script at its own act
(`nc_pt.py`, `nc-pt.sh`). Every test named is green on the real source.

### A1 -- the close left unsaved on any refusal (BUG-97)

- **Fix** (`save_player.go`, `closeNow`, `settleForClose`; `escape_menu.go`
  `EscapeMenu.Dismiss`): the talk and the journal ended, then the settle
  (above), then the save. The escape menu is put away before the settle's
  first frame, because the world is paused under it. The settle's frames are
  the screen's own `Advance`, run on the hook's goroutine while the game
  goroutine waits (as the save and the unload already were); they take no
  autosave of their own (`closing`), and the close's save covers a waiting
  one. `CloseReport` gains `ended` (`talk`, `journal`, `menu`),
  `settle_frames` and `settle_ms`. The console's `quit` and the harness's
  graceful quit are the same hook.
- **Tests:** unit `TestTheCloseEndsATalkAndTheJournalAndSaves`,
  `TestTheCloseLetsTheMomentAfterAFightSettle` (settled on its fifth frame
  and saved; frames that never cure it given up at `closeSettleFrames`,
  unsaved, still unloaded; his death runs no frame and saves nothing);
  `TestTheCloseHookNeverBlocks` now refuses on `DEAD`. Playtest
  `TestTheCloseHook` **act 4** (a talk open: `close.ended [talk]`, saved, the
  resumed game at the close's minute with no talk open), **act 5** (an hour
  after his last save, his journal open: ended, saved, and the resumed game
  with the journal opened again is the moment he closed at, by the resume
  digest), **act 6** (the dog dead and the save still refused `FIGHTING` while
  it settles: `close.settle_frames` >= 1, saved, the resumed game a few
  settled frames on, his experience kept, no fight running).
- **Controls:** U-A1a (no `EndTalk`), U-A1b (the journal left open), U-A1c
  (no settle): each red, "not saved (TALKING / JOURNAL / FIGHTING: the fight's
  end has not been applied yet)" (`nc-U-A1*.txt`). Playtest P-A1-journal
  (batch P-A: act 5 red), P-A1-talk (batch P-B: act 4 red), P-A1-no-settle
  (batch P-C: act 6 red) (`nc-P-A.txt`, `nc-P-B.txt`, `nc-P-C.txt`).

### A2 -- a failed write tore the last save while the game said it stood (BUG-98)

- **Fix, all three parts:**
  1. **The rollback** (`save.go`: `putBackWorld`, the one change there
     besides B2's step 0; `d2save.RestoreWorld`): when step 2 (his `.od2`) or
     step 3 (his sidecar) fails after the world file landed, the world file
     is put back from the `.bak` WriteWorld just kept (or removed, when there
     was none: a first save). The three files are the last save's again;
     `SaveResult.PutBack` names the file, and it is not in `Written`. The
     game's generation is set only after step 3, so it never moved. If the
     put-back fails too it is logged (Error) and the `.bak` is left
     untouched -- the TORN recovery then resumes it. After a put-back the
     `.bak` holds the last save (the same bytes as the world file): the save
     before it is not kept, the one cost of a failed save.
  2. **The TORN recovery** (`load.go`: `resumeTheBak`, `peekTheBak`,
     `LoadReport.FromBak`), above.
  3. **The words are chosen from what the save left written**
     (`saveWords`, `worldFileLeftWritten`): "Your last save stands" only when
     the world file is not among `res.Written` -- never written, or put back.
     Otherwise the menu says "The game could not be saved, and your last save
     could not be put back as it was." / "Save again before you leave, if you
     can."; the dawn's notice "The dawn's save could not be written, and your
     last save could not be put back."; the close's report the same words.
     And a failed `strigoi_save_game`'s hint says `put_back`.
  Also: a write that fails leaves no temporary file behind (probe B found a
  stray `0.od2.tmp`).
- **Tests:** unit `TestAFailedSavePutsTheWorldFileBack` (his `.od2` refused,
  and his sidecar refused: the world file, the sidecar and the generation are
  the last save's, the words say it stands, the next load resumes it -- not
  TORN); `TestATornSaveResumesTheBak` (the put-back refused too: `Written`
  keeps the world file, the `.bak` is untouched, the words never say
  "stands"; `PeekLoad` sees the `.bak` and moves nothing; the load resumes
  the `.bak`, the torn file set aside as `.torn.unread` byte for byte, the
  notice said; THE CONTROL, a `.bak` that is not his sidecar's moment, is
  refused TORN); `d2save.TestRestoreWorldPutsTheLastSaveBack`. Playtest
  `TestTheMenuSaves` **act 6** (his `.od2` read-only -- the review's probe B:
  SAVE GAME fails, his three files hash as before, the world file is still
  his last save, the menu says "Your last save stands." and "Leave now and
  you come back to your last save.", EXIT WITHOUT SAVING, and the next load
  resumes his last save, not TORN, by the resume digest);
  `TestTheLoadNotice` **act 3** (a save cut off after the world file, made
  by hand: the last save's `.od2` and sidecar put back beside the new world
  file: the load resumes the `.bak`, `load.from_bak`, the torn file set aside
  as `.torn.unread` byte for byte, the notice, and the world is the last
  save's by the resume digest).
- **Controls:** U-A2a (the put-back removed: the pre-fix code), U-A2b (no
  `.bak` resume), U-A2c (the words not from `Written`) red
  (`nc-U-A2*.txt`); playtest P-A2-no-put-back (TestTheMenuSaves act 6 red)
  and P-A2-no-bak-resume (TestTheLoadNotice act 3 red), both in batch P-A.

### B1 -- the close's limit could cut a save inside a file (BUG-99)

- **Fix, the recommended option** (`d2items.WriteFileAtomic`,
  `RenameRetrying`, `CutWrites`; `waitForClose`, `closeGrace`):
  - **No file is ever written in place**, by any save, not only the close's.
    The fallback existed for a reason -- Windows refuses a rename while
    anything holds the target (the 23 Sep measurement: a polling reader, in
    the field a scanner, the indexer, Explorer's preview) -- so the RETRY
    stays and grows (twenty 25 ms waits, then 100, 200, 400, 800 ms: two
    seconds in all), and only the in-place write is dropped. A rename still
    refused is the write's failure; the world save puts its world file back
    (A2), and a kit save is made again by the next one.
  - **The close gives up only between files**: at its limit no write begins
    (`ErrWritesCut`) and the one in flight is waited for, up to `closeGrace`
    (3 s, longer than one write's retries). A disk that hangs past that is
    reported (`close.cut_mid_write`) -- and since nothing is written in place,
    the file it would have replaced is whole. A cut between files is the
    TORN recovery's case.
  - The world editor's map save shares the write (its comment updated).
- **Tests:** `d2items.TestAWriteIsNeverMadeInPlace` (a rename refused on
  every try fails the write, the file as it was, no `.tmp` left, tried
  once and after each wait; THE CONTROL: refused twice then let through,
  it lands), `TestTheCloseCutsWritesBetweenFiles` (the cut waits for a write
  in flight, which ends whole; a write asked for after the cut is refused and
  touches nothing; a write hanging past the grace is reported and its file is
  whole). Seams (`renameFile`, `renameBackoff`), so they run the same in CI.
- **Controls:** U-B1a (the in-place write restored), U-B1b (the cut does not
  wait), U-B1c (writes begin after the cut): each red (`nc-U-B1*.txt`).

### B2 -- a refused file that could not be set aside was overwritten (BUG-100)

- **Fix** (`save.go`, SaveWorld's step 0, `setAsideRefusedWorld`;
  `preload.go` `forgetIgnored`): before a save writes the world file, a file
  the load refused earlier in the run and could not set aside
  (`ignoredRefusal`) is set aside now, under the load's own name for its
  refusal, and is never kept as the `.bak`. Still held, the save is not made
  and says so ("The game could not be saved: the save that could not be
  read / is still held open, and is not written over.";
  `ErrRefusedFileHeld`). It is in SaveWorld rather than WriteWorld because
  the memory of the load's refusals is d2gamescreen's; every save of his
  files goes through SaveWorld.
- **Test:** `TestASaveSetsAsideAFileTheLoadRefused` (held: not saved, nothing
  written, the words; let go: set aside first, the `.bak` still the good save,
  the refused file kept; the save after keeps the new save as the `.bak` and
  the refused file where it was set aside).
- **Control:** U-B2 (no step 0): red, the held file written over with no
  error (`nc-U-B2-no-step-0.txt`).

### B3 -- the tests' teeth

- Closing with the journal or a talk open, and a failed write: A1's and A2's
  acts above.
- **A resume comparison across a dawn with the in-frame autosave on both
  sides** (`TestTheDawnAutosave` acts 0, 3 and 4): a harness save at 02:38
  (act 0, its three files kept by the script); the original game crosses the
  dawn (its autosave taken on its own frame) and a second on is `sDay`. Act 3:
  his files put back as act 0 left them, T0 resumed, the dawn comes on the
  same frame (165 from the save), the resumed game takes its own autosave, and
  a second on it is `sDay` by the resume digest. **Act 4**, the claim the
  review said rested only on act 7a's between-frames save: the same resume
  and frames with the autosave OFF is `sDay` too -- the dawn's save, taken
  inside the dawn's frame, moved nothing. Control P-B3 (the autosave nudges
  the clock inside its frame): acts 1-3 stay green (the nudge is in both
  games) and act 4 goes red (batch P-C).
- **BUG-96's negative control:** P-BUG96 (SAVE AND EXIT GAME back to
  `navigator.ToMainMenu()` alone, the pre-B5 code): `TestTheMenuSaves` act 5
  red, "SAVE AND EXIT GAME wrote no new save" (batch P-B).

### C -- words, docs, polish

1. **The network game's words** (C1): the menu's refusal line ends "Leave
   now: your hero and his gear go with you. The night does not." (not "you
   come back to your last save": a network game has none to come back to);
   the load notice for a LAN client says "Your hero and his gear come with
   you. The saved night is kept, set aside -- not deleted." (not "You wake at
   dawn": he joins the host's hour). The menu's note is the game's now
   (`MenuSaver.SaveRefusedNow` returns why and what leaving does). Unit
   `TestTheWordsAreTrue`, `TestTheLoadsNotice`; control U-C1.
2. **One hero write** (C2, BUG-101): a SAVE AND EXIT or a close that saved
   sets `exitSavedHero`, and the unload does not write his `.od2` and sidecar
   again (`Game.unloadSavesHero`); any frame clears it. So `.od2.bak` is the
   save before, not this moment (rule 5). Unit
   `TestTheExitsSaveWritesHisOd2Once`; control U-C2.
3. **The console's `quit`** (C3, BUG-102) runs the close hook
   (`App.closeTheGame`) and then ends the process. d2app
   `TestTheConsolesQuitClosesTheGameFirst` (harness tag); control U-C3.
4. **Stale text** (C4): `death.go`'s header (the close runs `OnUnload` since
   B5, and saves nothing of a dead man); the death screen says "All since his
   last save is lost." (saves are made at the menu, at dawn and at the close,
   and "load last save" resumes the last); the held-action mentions say the
   refusal remains until BUG-87's branch (save-held) merges; `NOT_READY`'s
   "no hero save" and "no kit" reasons have words of their own ("This game
   has no save file to keep it in.", "You can't save until your gear is in
   place."), and "You can't save yet: the game is still starting." is the
   rest. Control U-C4.
5. **Names** (C5, BUG-104): a torn file is set aside as `.torn.unread`
   (`d2save.TornPath`, `SetAsideTorn`, and the load's `setAsideFor`, which
   every set-aside path goes through). The hero's files are recognised by
   name -- every `N.od2.*` is his (`d2hero.IsHeroFile`, `HeroFiles`) -- so
   a `.torn.unread` is his to the delete and to `to`'s fence, as a
   `.v2.unread` is; nothing reads a set-aside file back. "Your last night could not be restored." is now "Your
   last save could not be restored.", right at any hour. Control U-C5.
6. **Decision B5-2 for Josh** (below).
7. **For the merge with save-held** (below).
8. **Attached mode** (C8, BUG-103): documented in `docs/harness.md`, and the
   playtests refuse the verbs that write a hero's files -- a hero made, a
   save to his files, a close -- when the session is attached to a game whose
   `saves_dir` (new in `game_info`) is this user's own
   (`session.refuseRealSaves`).

## The decisions (the recommended option each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B5R-1, the settle's bound:** eight seconds of game time (the longest
  BUG-87 refusal measured at the shipped round is 6.53 s) and four of wall
  time, well inside the close's ten. The review's recommendation, with the
  bound chosen to outlast the measured worst case until save-held merges.
  (It merged the same day; the bound is kept, its comment corrected:
  "Merge: save-held + B5" below.)
- **B5R-2, LOADOUT and NOT_READY stay refusals of the close**: nothing he
  played can be lost (the world is held or not yet built), and a close cannot
  choose his gear for him.
- **B5R-3, never write in place, anywhere** (B1's recommended option), not
  only while closing: a torn file is worse than a failed save now that a
  failed world save puts itself back.
- **B5R-4, B2's guard is SaveWorld's step 0**, not WriteWorld's: d2save does
  not know what the load refused.
- **B5R-5, after a put-back the `.bak` is the last save** (WriteWorld's
  copy), not the save before it: the spec's "restore from the `.bak`
  WriteWorld just kept", and the files agree.
- **B5R-6, the controls are overlays**: the mutation is real source, built,
  and the worktree is never changed while other runs read it.
- **B5R-7, the attached-mode guard is the playtests'**: a game started by
  hand with the harness keeps every verb (a person driving it is choosing
  to); a script is refused before it writes the real user's saves.
- **B5-2 kept: a pending autosave waits until night falls** (C6). The
  trade-off, for Josh: a dawn refused for hours -- a fight at first light,
  then a noon fight that goes badly -- is taken at the first savable moment
  that day, which may be after the bad fight, replacing his last good save
  (the dawn's, or an earlier one) with a worse moment. The alternative (the
  window ends at sunrise, 22 seconds after first light) drops the day's save
  whenever first light's fights outlast it, and a crash that evening costs a
  night and a day. **Question for Josh**, below.

## For the merge with save-held (C7)

Every B5 path that saves, then crosses a dawn, then loads -- the paths whose
load would read the dawn's autosave instead of the save the script made,
unless the dawn saves nothing (`autosaveOffDial`) or the script restores its
files:

- `TestSaveResume` -- saves at T, crosses day 2's dawn in acts 3 and 6, loads
  T in acts 4 and 6b: carries `autosaveOffDial` in `startDials`,
  `huntedDials` and `quickResolvedDials`, and `keptEvening` adds it.
- `TestTheDawnAutosave` acts 0/3/4 -- saves at 02:38, the original crosses the
  dawn (its autosave overwrites T0), then act 3 and act 4 load T0: the script
  writes act 0's three files back before each load; act 4 sets the dial.
- `TestSaveResumeNegatives` loads files the negative controls wrote with `to`
  (not his files): no dawn path.
- Nothing else on save-b5 saves before a dawn and loads after it:
  `TestTheMenuSaves`, `TestTheCloseHook` and `TestTheLoadNotice` stay within
  day 0; `TestTheDawnAutosaveWaitsOutAFight` saves only by the dawn.
- **On save-held, to re-check at the merge** (done: "Merge: save-held + B5" below, "The
  `autosaveOffDial` audit" -- no script needed the dial): `TestSaveResumeMidAction` and the
  reworked `save_clock_fight_test.go` scripts (the dawn script's V2 steps
  three rounds after its save): any of them that saves before a dawn edge and
  loads after it needs `autosaveOffDial` in its dials (or its files kept and
  put back).
- Textual conflicts expected (the review's list): `game_controls_harness.go`,
  the harness version (0.15.1 here), `save_resume_test.go`, `register.go`, the
  notes; and `save.go` (A2's rollback and B2's step 0 are here; save-held
  changes the held-action clause of `fightUnsettled`).

## Register

Eight new wire rows (`tools/reachcheck/register.go`, "THE M4.6 B5 REVIEW
FIXES"): `Game.settleForClose`, `EscapeMenu.Dismiss`, `Game.putBackWorld`,
`d2save.RestoreWorld`, `resumeTheBak`, `d2save.SetAsideTorn`,
`setAsideRefusedWorld`, `d2items.CutWrites`. Each is a fix the playtests'
own saves (`strigoi_save_game`) would never show gone dark. The
`RenameRetrying` row's words follow B1 (two seconds of retries, never in
place). `d2items.uncutWrites`, the unit tests' reset, is unexported: an
exported symbol only tests call is the hollow class the register exists to
find.

# Merge: save-held + B5 (29 Sep)

Branch `merge-save`, worktree `strigoi-wt-merge2`, from master `b98bdfec`, for
the main session to fast-forward `master`. **After this merge saving is
player-reachable:** Josh saves with the escape menu's SAVE GAME and SAVE AND
EXIT GAME, the window's close saves, and the dawn saves on its own; a
monster's or villager's action still playing is saved at its frame (BUG-87)
instead of refusing the save. Every log is in
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-merge2\`.

## What merged

- **`0adf1295`**, `git merge --no-ff save-held` (at `c113f4a7`): BUG-87
  (`b07875c8`, `e8531622`) and its review fixes (`ff60df20`, `c113f4a7`). It
  files BUG-88..94 (BUG-89 and BUG-90 closed), brings harness 0.14.4 and the
  world file's `Motion.action_at` (version 2, amended; shape hash
  `be2baeb5...`), and stamps kept evenings with `harness.json` (BUG-93).
- **`43abf4cf`**, `git merge --no-ff save-b5` (at `02990ba0`): B5
  (`3928b075`) and its review fixes (`02990ba0`). It files BUG-95..104 and
  brings harness 0.15.1: the menu's SAVE GAME and SAVE AND EXIT GAME, the
  close hook, the dawn autosave, the load's notices, the rollback from `.bak`
  and the TORN resume.
- A docs commit after them records the runs: this section, `docs/bugs.md`
  (BUG-105 and the ledger line) and `docs/harness.md` (0.15.2, the measured
  frames).

## Master had moved: the Janissary integration

Master was `1f60a66f` when both branches were cut. Another session then
integrated GPT's refined Janissary art (`0ba2b4d5`, `6a43e559`, `b98bdfec`).
Read in full before merging (`janissary-log-p.txt`):

- `player.go`: a visual held-action path for the hero -- `Player.StartAction`
  plays his hit, shield block and death (`actionHeld`, `actionMode`,
  `corpse`), and `castMode` keeps his swing's A1 pose. Reactions do NOT set
  `IsCasting`, so they are not save or input locks; `StandAt` (the load's rule
  4) clears them all.
- `combat_resolver.go`: `ActBlock`, emitted for a blocked, non-lethal blow.
  `npc_body.go`: `Game.Animate` sends the hero's hit, block and death to
  `StartAction`; a monster's block plays its GH.
- `save_review_test.go`: the four new Player fields classified D.
- `docs/bugs.md`: BUG-18's row edited and three ledger rows. **It filed no BUG
  number.** `docs/harness.md`: the script count 49 -> 50 and a bullet for
  `janissary_art_test.go`; **no harness version change** (it stayed 0.14.3).
  `register.go`: one row, `Player.StartAction`.

None of it touches the save's files. It meets this merge in one place, below
("Found on the way", BUG-105).

## The conflicts, and how they were resolved

**Both sides were kept everywhere.**

The first merge (save-held onto master) conflicted in one file:

- **`docs/bugs.md`.** BUG-18's row is the Janissary session's (its anchor
  offsets) and BUG-19's is save-held's (the flakes under load): each side
  changed a different row, and each row was taken from the side that changed
  it (checked against the base line by line). The ledger keeps the Janissary
  session's three rows, then save-held's two.
- `docs/harness.md` and `register.go` merged without conflict (the Janissary
  bullet and `Player.StartAction` beside save-held's text and rows).

The second merge (B5 onto that) conflicted in eight:

- **`d2game/d2gamescreen/load.go`.** `LoadReport` keeps save-held's
  `EndedActions` (`ended_actions`, BUG-92) and B5's `Dropped`; `LastLoad`
  copies both. Everything else merged cleanly and was checked by hand:
  save-held's `rebuiltEntity.ResumeMotion` signature, its two call sites
  (`rekeyNatives`, `rebuildEntity`) and `restoreMotionExactly` returning the
  ended action; B5's load notices and the TORN resume from the `.bak`
  (`resumeTheBak`, `from_bak`).
- **`d2game/d2player/game_controls_harness.go`, `HarnessDigest`'s delete
  list.** save-held took `clock_strip_hours_to_dusk` OUT of it (BUG-88: the
  strip samples the minute's start, so it is compared again); B5 put
  `escape_menu` and `save_notice` IN (process part). The list now has B5's
  two and not the strip.
- **`d2app/harness.go`.** Harness **0.15.2** (below).
- **`playtest/save_resume_test.go`.** save-held's `eveningHarness` (BUG-93)
  and B5's `hasDial`, side by side.
- **`tools/reachcheck/register.go`.** save-held's BUG-87 rows, and its removal
  of `Game.heldAction`'s row -- the symbol is gone -- are kept; B5 had edited
  that row (upgraded to live), and the edit went with it. B5's
  `gameSpawner.Snapshot` row (live since B5) replaces save-held's (deferred to
  B5). The reach gate passed on the result (below).
- **`docs/bugs.md`.** BUG-87's row is save-held's (B5 did not edit it);
  BUG-88..94 (save-held) then BUG-95..104 (B5). The ledger keeps both
  branches' rows, save-held's first.
- **`docs/harness.md`.** The clock-fight bullet is save-held's, followed by
  B5's `save_player_test.go` bullet, now **the fifty-first** (the Janissary
  file became the fiftieth on master); the count is **51**. The tools
  heading says 0.15.2. The `game_info`, `save_game` and `quit` rows carry
  both sides' additions, in version order (0.14.4, then 0.15.0 and 0.15.1).
- **This file.** save-held's two sections, then B5's two; this one last.

**Auto-merged, and checked by hand:** `save.go` (save-held's removal of
`heldAction` and its note on the swing clause; B5's rollback --
`putBackWorld` -- and step 0, `setAsideRefusedWorld`); the one
line B5 added to `save_clock_fight_test.go` (`s.refuseRealSaves` in
`scCall`, BUG-103) inside save-held's rework of that file; `hud.go` (B5's
notice widget beside save-held's BUG-88 comment).

## The Janissary collisions

- **Bug numbers: no collision.** The Janissary session filed no number (it

commit:

- **`save_player.go`**: the autosave's "every refusal is not now" header (the

  sooner); `saveRefusalWords`; the close's settle constants; `closeNow`'s
  "until save-held merges, one more" (now in the past tense).
- **The FIGHTING words** ("You can't save while blows are still landing. /
  Try again in a moment."): **the no-fight-of-his case is still reachable,
  so the words stand as they are.** It is his last swing still playing, the
  fight's end not yet applied, or its experience or pace not yet taken.
  Measured on this merge: `TestTheCloseHook` act 6 ran **65 settle frames**
  before the close could save (`pt-1-c-list.txt`), and
  `TestTheDawnAutosaveWaitsOutAFight`'s autosave was refused for **64 frames

  On B5 alone it was 60 frames, the dog's death still playing.
- **The close's 8 s settle is kept**, its comment corrected: it was sized for
  the held-action refusal (6.53 s measured by BUG-87); his own swing still
  refuses, and a settle that ends early costs nothing.
- `save_player_test.go`, `save_b5_review_test.go`: the same, in comments.
- `d2save/world.go`: saving is a player's from this merge, so any further
  amendment of version 2's shape is refused `FILE` in every save Josh has

- The notes: this section, and a pointer at each of B5's "until save-held
  merges" paragraphs above.

## The `autosaveOffDial` audit

A path that saves, then crosses a dawn, then loads would load the dawn's
autosave instead of the save it made. B5's notes (above, "For the merge with
save-held") listed its own; the re-check of save-held's scripts:

| Script | Save | After the save | Dial needed? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TestSaveResumeMidAction` | the first dawn (the game opens at 02:45): a clock fight's first held A1, then GH, then DT | 60 frames per action, then "load last save" in process | no: no dawn edge is crossed |
| `TestSaveResumeAHeldActionOutOfReach` | first dawn, as M1 | edited files loaded in process; 60 frames | no |
| `TestSaveResumeAFightHeIsNotIn` | first dawn, 02:51 (measured) | three one-minute rounds | no |
| `TestSaveResumeWithAFightHeIsNotIn` | the first night, 21:51 (measured) | 12-24 ten-minute steps: at most four hours, to 01:51 | no: dawn is 02:45 |
| `TestSaveResume` | T at 01:37 | acts 3 and 6 cross day 2's dawn | yes: carries it (B5) |

The game opens at 02:45, dawn, and the autosave is armed only on a night's
turning into dawn (`nightSurvived`), so a script saved in the first dawn
reaches the next arming a day later. **No script needed the dial added.** All
four ran green on the merge.

  session chose "no save lock for a reaction" on purpose (its brief and
  register row), and Josh's 29 Sep ruling is that no new mid-combat work goes

# The combat status (30 Sep ruling) (branch `combat-status`, 30 Sep 2026)

Worktree `strigoi-wt-combat`, from master `d5106126`. Every log is in
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-combat\`.

## Josh's words

- 30 Sep: *"I think we should have a combat status for the player, and you
  can't save while in combat."*
- On the questions that followed, the recommended option both times. What
  puts him in combat: *"Fight, action, or chased"* -- his fight is live, or
  his own swing or hit/block reaction is playing, or a hostile is chasing
  him; the status clears a few seconds after the last of these ends. On
  screen: *"Marker by the health globe"* -- a small "In combat" marker by the
  health globe, on while he can't save; a letter until art exists.
- 29 Sep, behind it: *"most games don't let you save mid combat. I think
  you've found out why."* So the save **refuses** in combat rather than
  carrying what a fight leaves half-done -- and BUG-105 (his reaction, not in
  the file) closes by the same rule.

## The definition, as built (`d2game/d2gamescreen/combat_status.go`)

He is **in combat** while any of these holds (`Game.combatTrigger`, first
match wins; the harness's `combat_reason`):

| Code | Holds while | Read from |
|---|---|---|
| `fight` | his fight is live -- his own encounter only; an open turn (`Awaiting`) is his fight's by construction (both read `Combat.his`), and a fight he is not in (the raid's clock fights) never counts by itself | `Combat.Fighting` |
| `chased` | a hostile is chasing him | `Pursuit.ChasersOf(his id)` (new) |
| `swing` | his own swing is playing | `Player.IsCasting` |
| `reaction` | his hit or block reaction is playing (not his death: a dead man is `DEAD`) | `Player.Reacting` (new: `actionHeld` and not the death mode) |

**"A hostile"**: only a monster ever chases him in the game. Every chase the
game starts comes from the notice model's watches (`startChasesForTheAware`),
and only the spawn tables watch him (`Spawns`, each member of each group they
place); villagers and his squads watch no one. So the trigger is any chase
whose quarry is he (the harness's `strigoi_pursue` can start others: a
script's stand-in, and hostile by construction).

**And for a grace period after the last of these ends:**
`combatGraceSeconds` of **play** (not game time: every live frame counts, a
paced fight's held-world frames too -- reworded 1 Oct, the review's C1),
`DefaultCombatGraceSeconds` = **3 s**
[DIAL] (the harness's `save.combat_grace`, a dial a load re-applies). At the
end of every frame (`Game.advanceCombatStatus`, in `Advance` just before the
dawn autosave): a trigger seen holds the grace full; none counts it down by
the frame's own seconds, on every frame the screen is live -- **the escape
menu pauses it**, as it pauses the world (a network game's menu pauses
nothing, and neither does it here); at 0 (floored at 1e-9: sixty slices of
1/60 never sum to exactly 1) he is out. `Game.CombatReason` is the trigger
now, or `grace` while only the grace runs; `Game.InCombat` is either. The log
gets one line each way: `COMBAT in (chased): <ids> chasing him` and `COMBAT
out: 3.0 s after <the last trigger>` -- a friend's log says how often and how
long he could not save.

**None of it is in the world file**, and none needs to be: a save is never
made in combat, so at every save the grace is 0 and no trigger holds
(`TestAnAcceptedSaveIsOutOfCombat`: 600 frames of triggers on and off and a
save tried at every one -- 132 made, each at grace 0 with no trigger; 468
refused). `Game`'s three new fields are classified: `combatGrace` T
(refused: `TestEveryTransientIsRefusedOrReset` sets it and sees COMBAT),
`combatLast` and `combatGraceSeconds` D. The player's `actionHeld` moved from
D to T (refused COMBAT), and `isCasting`'s T is COMBAT's now.

## Measured first (`strigoi-measure-first`)

The design depended on what a chase does over time, so a throwaway scaffold
(`playtest/zz_combat_status_measure_test.go`, deleted before the commit) read
only the engine's own providers every 3 harness frames (0.05 s of game time)
from dusk on day 0 to 04:15 on day 1, seeds 1462, 99 and 7, the amazon
standing where the game put him, the shipped world, food and water refilled
(`s0-chase-1.txt`: as is; `s0-alive-1.txt`: his health put back below 200 so
the whole night is seen). The dated doc is `Claude doc outputs\Combat Status -
S0 Measurements.md`. What it found:

1. **A chase does not flicker as its hunter re-paths**: a chase lives in
   `Pursuit` from `Chase` to `Release`; `solve` replaces the route, never the
   chase. Outside a fight a chase of him was one unbroken run: 32.15 s,
   44.0 s, 88.0 s. The grace need not cover re-pathing.
2. **Inside a paced fight "chased" flickers** (off 1.20 s every 1.2-2.4 s:
   the fight releases each member's chase while it holds it and the notice
   loop takes it again) **but his fight is live throughout**: the union of
   the triggers was one unbroken interval per fight in every seed.
   **"Chased" latches** (Josh's ruling of 30 Sep; the R2 review's B1: on
   `raid-r2`, with a second quarry near, a chase on him can drop for
   0.4-0.8 s and come back): the 3 s grace is the latch. Unit
   `TestAChaseThatFlickersStaysCombat`: a chase released for 0.8 s and taken
   again keeps him in combat on every frame between, with no `COMBAT out`
   edge, the save refused `COMBAT` throughout; its in-test control (a 0.3 s
   grace) lets him out, and control U2 (no grace) turns it red.
3. **His swing and reaction outlast the fight's end** by up to about 1.3 s
   (seed 99: the fight ended with his get-hit and swing playing; the swing
   ended 78 frames later). The 3 s grace covers it, and FIGHTING's
   bookkeeping (a frame or two).
4. **How often and how long he cannot save, standing in the open**: the
   first chase at dusk (19:41 or 21:13); from then **in combat until the
   pack or he is dead** -- unhealed, he died in his first fight in all three
   seeds (51.5, 53.9, 90.2 s after 19:41); healed, one or two episodes a
   night (seed 99: 33.1 s, then 89.0 s from 23:04 to dawn; seeds 1462 and 7:
   133 s from 21:13 to 02:47), **68-75% of the night's 178 s of game time
   (72-76% with the grace)**. By day nothing chases him (the tables' day
   weight is 0). The grace adds 3 s per episode.
5. **A chase outlives the notice that started it** (BUG-108, filed; a unit
   scaffold, `s0-chase-memory.txt`): a dog that forgot him (noticed false
   after 41 frames) was still chasing him 3000 frames later. Nothing
   releases a chase when its hunter loses him; so a monster that saw him
   once keeps him in combat until it dies, a fight takes its chase, or
   daybreak despawns its group. **A question for Josh** (the report).

## Every save path

- **`Game.SaveWorld`** (`save.go`): the order is arguments, readiness and
  network, `DEAD`, **`COMBAT`**, `FIGHTING`, `TALKING`, `JOURNAL`, `LOADOUT`.
  **The fold (the simplest clear option):** COMBAT took FIGHTING's two
  clauses that were the same thing -- "a fight is running" (`Combat.Fighting`)
  and "his last swing is still playing" (`Player.IsCasting`) -- and they are
  gone from `fightUnsettled`. What is left of FIGHTING is the fight's own
  bookkeeping (its end not yet applied -- `wasFighting` --, its experience or
  pace not taken, a strike waiting on a walk, a tactical walk out, the
  journal reading a fight): a frame or two after his fight, well inside the
  grace, so in the game FIGHTING is a backstop (and the whole of it with
  `save.combat_grace` at 0). The refusal's reason names the trigger: `he is
  in combat (chased): <ids> chasing him`, `(grace): 1.23 s left of the grace
  after ...`.
- **The words** (`saveRefusalWords`, `d2player.SaveRefusedCombatWords`):
  COMBAT is **"You can't save in combat."**; FIGHTING is only ever the moment
  after a fight now, "You can't save while blows are still landing. / Try
  again in a moment." -- the `hisFight` split and "You can't save during a
  fight." went with the fold.
- **The menu** (SAVE GAME, SAVE AND EXIT GAME): refused like any refusal --
  the line under the entries is the words, then "Leave now and you come back
  to your last save.", and the exit entry is EXIT WITHOUT SAVING (B5's
  machinery, unchanged). The world, and so the grace, is paused under the
  menu: the answer it got as it opened holds while it is up, and he closes
  it and tries again when the marker is gone.
- **The dawn autosave**: nothing in its machine changed -- every refusal is
  "not now" to it -- so a dawn that finds him in combat is PENDING, tried
  every frame, and taken on the first frame he is out of combat, that day
  (dropped only if night falls first). Measured in `TestTheCombatStatus` act
  5: chased at first light, pending (refused COMBAT), and taken by the dawn
  180 frames after the chase ended.
- **The close hook** (rule 3): a close in combat -- his live fight, or a
  hostile chasing him -- leaves without saving, promptly (21 ms measured),
  and he comes back to his last save. **The settle** (the B5 review's A1)
  now also runs frames while the save is refused COMBAT for the grace, his
  swing or his reaction (`Game.settlesForClose`) -- **the recommended
  option: it waits out only the grace's remainder** (and a swing or reaction
  still playing, each of which ends on its own), up to the same bound (8 s
  of game time, 4 s of wall time); **never a chase**, which does not end on
  its own, **nor his live fight**. Measured: `TestTheCloseHook` act 6, closed
  with his swing still playing after the dog died: 244 settle frames (the
  swing's rest, then 180), 6 ms, saved.
- **The harness's `strigoi_save_game`** is `SaveWorld`: refused `COMBAT`.

## Rule 3, reworded

> Closing the window does what SAVE AND EXIT does. Only **combat** -- his
> live fight, or a hostile chasing him -- his death or a network game makes
> it leave without saving, and then he comes back to his last save. The few
> seconds after combat (the grace, his last swing or reaction) are waited
> out, and then it saves.

("his live fight" is "in combat" wherever rule 3 is quoted since this
burst: `save_player.go`'s header and `closeNow`, the B5 review fixes' "Rule 3,
as built now", above.)

## What the player sees: the marker (`d2game/d2player/hud_combat.go`)

- A small square **over the health globe** -- top-left (54, 468), 32 x 32:
  centred on the globe (x 54-86), its foot at y 500, above the globe's stone
  frame (top about y 505) -- drawn **only while `InCombat`**. Clear of the
  globe and of the left hand's icon (x 117-165, y 552-600); the life
  tooltip's line (at 15, 487) crosses it only while the globe is hovered.
- **Until art exists it is a red "!"** (Font24 -- Strigoi's IM Fell; Diablo
  II's under -classic) on a dark square with a red edge (0x8c1c14). A glyph,
  not a letter, because every letter the HUD draws beside the globes is a
  KEY -- F strikes, L lights, and C opens his character panel -- and a
  letter here would read as one more key (decision CS-5).
- **The art drops in with no code**, as the hands' does (`iconArt`, which
  `handArt` now shares): `data/strigoi/ui/combat.png`, one 32 x 32 frame;
  art that does not fit is reported (`combat marker art refused`) and the
  glyph drawn. The art need is in `docs/art-spec.md` section 7 and in
  `Claude doc outputs\Art Needs - 30 Sep 2026.md`.
- Screenshots (rewritten by every run): `strigoi-harness-runs\pt\
  TestTheCombatStatus\<run>\combat-0-out-*.png` (none), `combat-1-chased-*`
  (the marker), `combat-1-menu-*` (the menu's refusal over it),
  `combat-2-grace-*`, `combat-2-out-*`, `combat-3-village-*`.
- **The help screen (H)** gained a twelfth line: "The red mark over your
  life globe: in combat. You can't save until it goes." (`strigoi_help.go`;
  `bullets` 12, the last at y 279, above the callouts at 355;
  `TestStrigoiHelpNamesTheKeys` requires "in combat" and "can't save").

## BUG-105: closed

His hit or block reaction playing puts him in combat, and for the grace
after, so a save cannot land during one. Unit: `TestEachTriggerAlonePutsHimInCombat`
(the hit and the block, each alone: refused COMBAT, then 180 frames of grace,
then saved), `TestPlayerAndControlsFieldsClassified` (`actionHeld` refused).
Playtest: `TestTheCombatStatus` act 4 -- he holds and every blow is a hit;
the frame his get-hit plays, his fight is disengaged and the dog let go; the
save during the reaction is refused COMBAT for `reaction`, and made 210
frames later. Controls U5 and P6 red.

## The world file: no shape change

No field was added to any block: the grace is 0 at every save
(`TestAnAcceptedSaveIsOutOfCombat`). `d2save.Version` stays 2, the golden
file `world-v2.json` and the shape hash `be2baeb5...` are unchanged
(`TestTheFileShapeIsPinnedToItsVersion`, green). A file from before this
burst that holds a chase of him -- B4b's hunted nights did -- still resumes,
and he is in combat the moment it does (`TestAFileHoldingAChaseOfHimResumesInCombat`).

## Existing tests whose moment moved (the assertion kept; each documented)

The rule is not weakened anywhere: where a test saved at a moment that is now
combat, **the moment moved**, and where the refusal it expected is COMBAT's
now, **the code and words changed** -- nothing else.

**The moment moved (the premise "save while chased" is impossible now):**

- **`TestSaveResume`, act 1-2 (T).** B4b's T was "the first frames of the
  chase, the pack walking in" -- a hostile chasing him: refused COMBAT. Now
  the pack is placed with the notice radius still T's half a tile (`startDials`):
  on the map, WATCHING him (its watches in the file), NOT YET AWARE; the
  chase and the mid-walk motion the file must hold are a fallen's, placed
  seven to ten tiles off and sent after the villager farthest from it
  (`farthestNative`; act 7c's arrangement); the notice radius opens to 24 at
  T (between the precondition and the snapshot, no frame between:
  `Notice.SetRadius` evaluates nothing), so the pack sees him and gives
  chase in the frames after T -- in both games alike (`huntedDials` carries
  24 for the resumed one). `huntedPrecondition` now requires the pack
  watching him and none aware, **no chase of him**, the walker's chase, the
  walker mid-walk (waypoints, or -- a route of one straight leg, measured --
  a target ahead), and **out of combat**. Measured on the moved T: act 3's
  two hours still hold the pack's fight, **13 rounds and 45 blows**, none
  quick-resolved, ending `enemies_routed` -- B4b's evening's own numbers.
  **Also in act 1:** the wounded survivor "let go" is now unwatched AND its
  chase released: `strigoi_watch{release}` is `Notice.Unwatch` alone, and the
  dog -- `d545358d...` -- was still chasing him at T (the first run's red:
  `pt-targeted-1.txt`), a hostile chasing him all evening (BUG-108's harness
  face).
- **`TestSaveResume`, act 7c.** "The save taken the moment [a pack] arrives":
  the pack now placed with the notice radius at half a tile, after the grace
  after the dog's fight has run out (at most 400 frames stepped); asserted
  out of combat before the save.
- **`TestSaveResume`, act 8 (rule 4, a save mid-walk).** With the hunted
  night's radius the pack sees him within his three walking frames and the
  save is refused; the act's dials are now `huntedDials` with T's half-tile
  radius (`walkDials`), on both sides of its comparison.
- **`TestSaveResume`, act 6i (BUG-75/87's death window).** BUG-87 made the
  save "at once, on the first try, while the slain still play their
  deaths" -- the second after HIS fight: the grace, and (measured, the first
  run) three more of the night's pack members still chasing him 900 frames
  later. Now: the save at once is **refused COMBAT** (asserted); every watch
  on him is dropped and every chase of him released (the script clears the
  board, as `TestTheCombatStatus` lets its hostiles go); T' is the first
  frame out of combat (180 frames later); the slain lie where they fell at
  the first look, at T', 120 frames on and resumed; S_R0 = S_T', S_M', S_U'.
  **What moved out of this act:** a death saved at its frame -- at T' none
  of the slain still falls (the deaths, 56 frames, end inside the grace's
  180). That case is `TestSaveResumeMidAction`'s M-DT (a village fight he is
  not in), unchanged and green.
- **`TestSaveResumeWithAFightHeIsNotIn`, V1.** T was "the first frames of
  the chase with the clock fight live": now the notice radius is two tiles
  until T -- the clock fight's monster, a tile from its quarry, sees it; the
  zombie seven or more tiles off does not -- and the shipped radius again at
  T, before the save, so the zombie sees him and gives chase after T in both
  games. `scReadyAtT` requires the zombie watching him and nothing chasing
  him; the file is hunted by its watch (`hisWatchers` > 0) and holds **no**
  chase of him (`hisChases` == 0).
- **Unit tests (no MPQs):** `b4bHunt` (every B4b load test) and
  `TestARisenManWalkingAtTResumes` pursued HIM; the chase is now of the
  villager (the watch on him stays), which the re-key must also give back.

**What P6 says about BUG-105 in the game:** with the reaction trigger gone the
save was STILL refused -- for the grace after his fight, which a get-hit that
begins in his fight's last round (30 frames) always ends inside. So in the
shipped game the grace alone would cover BUG-105's case; the reaction trigger
is what covers a reaction outside his fight (and a grace dialled to 0), and
U5 is its proof alone.

## The harness (0.16.0)

`strigoi_save_game` refused `COMBAT`; the `save` provider's `in_combat`,
`combat_reason`, `combat_detail`, `combat_grace_left`, `combat_grace`, and
its second dial `combat_grace` (`harnessDialFields`; `TestEverySettableFieldIsADialOrNamedState`
green); the `ui` provider's `combat_marker`; `strigoi_quit{graceful}`'s
settle for the grace. The tool count is unchanged (37). An evening kept by
0.15.2 is refused as another harness's; this burst kept a new one,
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-combat\evening-1` (`harness.json` 0.16.0), and
`TestSaveResumeNegatives` ran against it, green (`pt-negatives-1.txt`: every
omit refused FILE, every emptied block resumed and diverged -- "empty
pursuit.chases" on the walker's chase).

## Decisions (the recommended option each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **CS-1, the fold:** COMBAT took FIGHTING's "a fight is running" and "his
  last swing is still playing" (they are the same thing as being in combat);
  FIGHTING keeps the fight's bookkeeping as a backstop, and its words are
  only "blows are still landing". (The alternative, keeping both and COMBAT
  covering the rest, would have said two things for one state.)
- **CS-2, COMBAT right after DEAD** in the save's order: a dead man is DEAD
  (his death is not a reaction), a network game NETWORK; everything else a
  fight leaves is behind COMBAT.
- **CS-3, the grace is seconds of play** ("game time" until the review's C1,
  1 Oct: a frame a paced fight holds the world on counts too) -- the frame's own seconds on a live
  screen; the escape menu pauses it with the world (so a man cannot open the
  menu to wait it out), and it is held full while a trigger holds.
- **CS-4, the close waits out only the grace's remainder** (and a swing or
  reaction still playing) -- the brief's recommendation -- up to the same
  8 s / 4 s bound; never a chase or his fight.
- **CS-5, the marker's placeholder is a glyph, "!"**, red on a dark square,
  not a letter: every letter by the globes is a key (F, L; C opens his
  character panel). Over the health globe, not beside it: to its right is
  the left hand's icon, and the stone frame fills its left.
- **CS-6, "a hostile" is any chase of him**: only monsters chase him in the
  game (reasoned from the call graph above); no hostility table is read.
- **CS-7, no shape change, no version bump**: the grace is 0 at every save,
  asserted.
- **CS-8, moved moments, not weakened rules** (the list above): where a test
  saved while chased, its T moved to a moment no hostile chases him; where a
  script "let a monster go", it now releases its chase too.
- **CS-9, the marker is drawn in -classic too**: the save and its refusal are
  both games'.

## Found on the way

- **BUG-108: a chase outlived the notice that started it** (above, S0 item
  5, and `docs/bugs.md`). With the combat status it decided how long he
  could not save after a monster lost him. FIXED by the review's fixes
  (below): the chase ends when its hunter forgets him.
- **The harness's paused game loop runs zero-delta frames.** While the
  harness holds the clock, ebiten's own loop still calls `Advance` with no
  elapsed time between harness calls (`harnessStepDeltas`: "frames advance
  with zero deltas"). The grace moves only on elapsed time, so it is right;
  but the dawn autosave's `tries` count those frames too (act 5's `tries`
  was 1028, more than twice the frames the script stepped), so under the
  harness `tries` is not a count of stepped frames. Recorded, not changed.
- **`strigoi_watch{release}` leaves the chase** (BUG-108's harness face):
  a script's let-go now releases both (`csLetGo`; `TestSaveResume` act 1).
- **The survivor's id** in `TestSaveResume` was the chaser at T in the first
  failing run (`d545358d...`, the wounded survivor: its let-go left its
  chase), confirmed by the passing run's precondition log.

## The review's fixes (1 Oct 2026)

An independent review of `combat-status` at `1a4784a8` (probes in the
reviewer's worktree `strigoi-wt-rev-combat`, logs in
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-rev-combat\`). Josh was away; **each fix below was
decided on the coordinator's default, and Josh can overturn any.** Every new
unit assertion was seen red under its control (`nc-unit-2.txt`); the two
playtest controls for act 2b (P-B2, P-B3-X1) ran after the laptop's drop
(`nc-pt-rest.txt`, `nc3-P-B2-no-moment-words.txt`,
`nc3-P-B3-X1-grace-runs-under-menu.txt`), red, with P5 and P6 red again.

- **A1, a leave in combat wrote half of him.** A close refused COMBAT, or
  EXIT WITHOUT SAVING in combat, left the world file at the last save -- but
  the unload still wrote his `.od2` and sidecar at the combat moment (the
  experience and gear of the fight he left), and the next load paired them
  with the old world (the sidecar carries the last save's generation). Now a
  single-player game left in combat writes no part of him
  (`Game.leavesInCombat`, asked by `unloadSavesHero` at the top of
  `OnUnload`, before `releaseWorld` closes what it reads): he comes back to
  his last save whole. A network game keeps his hero and gear as before.
  Unit `TestALeaveInCombatWritesNoPartOfHim` (the reviewer's
  `TestReviewCloseInCombatKeepsTheHeroHalf`, asserting the opposite; control:
  the same close out of combat saves and writes the 500 experience); control
  R-A1, red. **Reported, not changed:** EXIT WITHOUT SAVING *outside* combat
  (after a refusal for TALKING, JOURNAL, a failed save) still writes his
  `.od2` and sidecar at that moment beside the last save's world file, as B5
  shipped it -- the same hole, outside the combat status's ruling.
- **A2 / BUG-108, a chase outlived its hunter's notice.** Option (a): the
  chase ends when its hunter forgets him (`Pursuit.GiveUpOnTheForgotten`);
  N is the notice's `MemoryMinutes` [DIAL], 2.0 world minutes -- why, in
  `docs/bugs.md`. **And the window's close in combat asks once:** "You are
  in combat. / Close again to leave without saving." on the save notice; a
  second close within `closeAskWindow` (5 s of wall time [DIAL]) leaves
  without saving (`Game.AskBeforeClose`; the renderer's close handler now
  answers whether the window closes, `App.onWindowClose`). Only the window
  asks: the console's quit and the harness's graceful quit are a decision
  already, so the playtests' closes are unchanged. A close in the grace (or
  his swing or reaction) is not asked: the close settles it and saves. Tests:
  d2world `TestAChaseEndsWhenItsHunterForgets`, unit
  `TestAForgottenChaseEndsAndHeCanSave`, `TestAWindowsCloseInCombatAsksOnce`,
  d2app `TestAWindowCloseInCombatAsksFirst`; controls R-A2-no-give-up,
  R-A2-close-never-asks, R-A2-window-never-asks, red.
- **B1, the close's settle ran a world he did not see.** A close begun in
  the grace ran up to 180 frames of the world -- a monster could notice him
  in them, open his fight, and the close left unsaved. The close now holds
  the world (`WorldHeldBy` "closing", `d2player.WorldHeldByClose`); the
  grace, his swing and reaction (the map's animations) and the fight's own
  bookkeeping (`advanceWorldOrHold` applies the fight's end) still run. Unit
  `TestTheClosesSettleHoldsTheWorld` (the reviewer's
  `TestReviewCloseInGraceRunsTheUnseenWorld`, asserting the fix: 180 settle
  frames, no world minute, no chase, saved; in-test control: unheld, the dog
  comes for him); control R-B1, red.
- **B2, the moment after combat under the menu.** The menu pauses the
  world, so a grace (or a swing or reaction) seen from the menu never ends;
  "You can't save in combat." with nothing in sight read as a fault. Its own
  words now: **"Return to the game; you can save / a moment after the
  fighting stops."** (`SaveRefusedCombatMomentWords`). The other option --
  SAVE AND EXIT settling like the close -- was not taken: SAVE GAME could not
  do the same without running frames he does not see, and the two entries
  would answer differently for one moment. Unit
  `TestTheMomentAfterCombatHasItsOwnWords`; `TestTheCombatStatus` act 2b;
  controls R-B2 and P-B2, red (P-B2 at act 2b: the menu said "You can't
  save in combat.").
- **B3, the grace under the escape menu was untested** (the reviewer's X1
  survived). `TestTheCombatStatus` act 2b: in the grace, 300 frames under
  the menu move it not at all, the menu says the moment's words, and back in
  the game it runs out from where it stood (green twice, `pt-fix-1.txt` and
  `pt-fix-2.txt`). Control P-B3-X1, red (act 2b: the grace ran out under
  the menu, 2.000 s before, out of combat after).
- **C5, `renderCombatMarker` was untested** (X4 survived). d2player
  `TestTheCombatMarkerDrawsOnlyInCombat` (a recording surface: nothing out of
  combat, the red-edged square at 54,468 in combat; the label is drawn only

- **The grace's floor was untested** (X13 survived): unit
  `TestARemainderUnderTheFloorEndsTheGrace`; control R-X13, red.

# The raid's R2: notice sides, Seek, and his hiding (branch `raid-r2`, 29 Sep 2026)

**Written by one burst, built and tested by a second** (29 Sep 2026): every
`go build` and `go test` the writing burst asked for was refused by its
session's permission classifier; Josh approved the build at 19:17, and the R2
build compiled it (no fix needed), regenerated the golden file, recorded the
hash, ran every test, benchmark and control, and committed it on `raid-r2`
in two commits, the second this section's (`d2save`, `save.go`, `load.go`,
the golden file and the hash, the save-path test lists, these notes). The
results are in the R2 build note (`The Village at Night - R2 Build Note - 29
Sep 2026.md`), and the logs in `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-r2\build\`.

**Version 2's shape, amended with no bump** (the milestone's rule: R1 took the
one bump; a later burst amends version 2, and a version-2 file from before
the amendment is refused `FILE` and set aside -- R1 review's B3):

| Where | Change |
|---|---|
| `notice.watches[].side` (new; `d2world.WatchSnapshot.Side`) | `"hostile"` or `"living"` (`d2world.WatchSide`). Required: a watch with no side or an unknown one is refused (read as either, it would make a wolf a villager or a villager a wolf) |
| `seek` (new top-level block, after `pursuit`; `d2world.SeekSnapshot`) | `rows` (`d2world.SeekRowSnapshot`: `watcher`, `target` -- `"player"` for him, `reason` -- `pending` / `living` / `none` / `fighting`, `until_look_minutes`, `candidates`), `stand_ins`, `slots`, `looks`, `retargets`, `rays` |
| `d2save.Blocks` | `"seek"` between `"pursuit"` and `"corpses"` (World's field order) |
| `version` | still **2**. `testdata/world-v2.json` must be regenerated (`go test ./d2core/d2save -run TestTheFileShapeIsPinnedToItsVersion -update-golden`) and the printed hash recorded as `b3ShapeHashes[2]`, replacing R1's `fa82243b...` -- **done by the R2 build: `f0748aba9c91c0eebcdb17a000f0e07b1e3c22b9dd6aee8bef2e0f12a51ce5b6`**, the golden file 19 lines longer (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-r2\build\golden.txt`). `save-held` amends version 2's shape too, so its merge regenerates the golden file and the hash once more. The fixture (`b3Fixture`) gained the wolf's `side` and a `seek` block (one row, one stand-in): construction data the new fields require |

**What is NOT a shape change, and why.** The brief's P2 (b) makes `hidden` "a
list of quarry refs (T; refused while any is set)". Built: it stays one flag,
and his sleep hides the quarry bound by `Notice.SetPlayer` (the game binds
him every frame, and `applyTalk` before a sleep); with no player bound -- every
unit fixture -- it hides every quarry, as T6 built it. The list has one member,
him, and being T (refused while set) it is never in the file: no shape change,
and nothing for the "`hidden` list" the brief's section 4 names. `sheltered`
is untouched in code: under Q9 (c) every arrival is anchored on him, so "no
arrival anchored on him" is still "no arrival" (the comment says so).

**Nothing in the seek block resolves, on purpose.** A row is Seek's
bookkeeping about a watch, not the watch; the watch is the notice block's and
resolves there. A row whose watcher no longer watches is dropped at the next
step (`Seek.sync`) in the resumed game exactly as in the saved one, and a
stand-in that no longer resolves is simply not among the living at the next
look. Requiring either to resolve would refuse saves the game makes -- the R1
review's B1 (BUG-68) was that mistake in the combat block. So `Seek.Validate`
and `Restore` take no Resolver, and `World.checkRefs` checks no seek id.
What the restore does refuse: a row out of watcher order or saved twice, a
watcher that is empty or the word player, a row that is its own target, a
reason Seek does not give, a next look not above zero (between two steps every
row's next look is still to come), a pending row that counted candidates,
more rows than phases given out, more retargets than looks, a stand-in empty,
the word player or named twice, any count negative or not finite -- and a
Seek already holding a row or a stand-in (the B2 convention).

**Where it sits.** Save: `worldFile` takes `v.seek.Snapshot()` (it refuses
nothing: Seek holds nothing a save may not carry), `validateSnapshots` runs
`Seek.CheckSnapshot`, and `saveRefusal`'s NOT_READY clause requires a Seek
(the unit fixture `b3SavableGame` builds one, as CreateGame does). Load: step
4's list has `{"seek", v.seek.Validate(w.Seek)}` after pursuit; step 5
restores it after pursuit and before combat, and the load's steps gain
`"seek"` (the two d2gamescreen tests and `TestSaveResume`'s block list that
name the steps and blocks were extended by that one name).

**The classification.** `Notice.player` is W (his id, bound every frame);
`watch.side` is `S:notice.watches[0].side`. Seek: `dials` D; `notice`,
`spawns`, `combat`, `quarries`, `resolver` W; `standIns`, `rows`, `slots`,
`looks`, `retargets`, `rays` S; a row's `target`, `reason`, `untilLook`,
`candidates` S. `Game.seek` is `S:seek`. Seek has no T field.

**The five ways** (`d2core/d2world/seek_snapshot_test.go`), over a fake night
of eight wolves, him, two villagers and a stand-in, the quarries walking: the
classification; the round trip through JSON into a fresh night (snapshot for
snapshot, and sixty frames on); the sweep over every leaf with no exemption;
six probes (a row under another watcher's id, a row's target another quarry,
a row's reason another, a row on another phase, one more phase given out,
another stand-in), each accepted and seen; and the refusals above, each
refused with nothing changed. The notice block's `side` is exercised in
`TestNoticeSnapshotEveryFieldIsSeen` (three mutations added to its table:
lost and unknown refused, living diverged) -- the classification's teeth
require every S label to be seen.

**What the R2 build measured of the save (29 Sep 2026).**
- **The five ways, green:** `TestSeekSnapshotFieldsClassified`,
  `TestSeekSnapshotRoundTrip`, `TestSeekSnapshotEveryFieldIsSeen` and
  `TestSeekSnapshotRefusals`, and `TestNoticeSnapshotEveryFieldIsSeen` with
  its three `side` rows. Their control, `seek-rows-not-restored` (`Restore`
  drops the rows): red in the round trip and the sweep
  (`wt-r2\build\ctl-seek-rows-not-restored.txt`).
- **The shape:** `TestTheFileShapeIsPinnedToItsVersion` green on the new
  hash; `d2save`'s and `d2gamescreen`'s tests green (the load order with
  `"seek"`, the Game field `seek` classified `S:seek`).
- **In the game:** `TestSaveResume` green, every act. Its file grew with the
  block: 7a's 21 blocks and 10,352 bytes are 22 and 10,472, act 2's hunted
  night 37,002 bytes is 39,066, and act 4's and 6b's load steps name `seek`.
  Its act 3 and act 6i figures moved with target choice (below and the build
  note). `TestSaveResumeAFightHeIsNotIn`, `TestSaveResumeWithAFightHeIsNotIn`
  and `TestSaveResumeInTheFirstSecond` green: a save made with the village
  fighting takes the seek block and resumes to the digest.
- **Josh's ruling of 29 Sep ("most games don't let you save mid combat"):**
  R2 needs **no new mid-fight exactness**. A row of a fighter is plain data
  (`reason` `fighting`, its next look), restored exactly; `Combat.engaged`
  is read from the combat block the file already carries. Seek refuses no
  save and adds no refusal; his fight is refused as before
  (`fightUnsettled`), and a fight he is not in saves as R1 left it (Q5 (a);
  BUG-87's held-action refusal unchanged).
- **The moved figure the save scripts show:** `TestSaveResume` act 3, the two
  hours after T -- 4 encounters, 13 rounds and 45 blows, he at 177 at dawn
  (master `1f60a66f`) -- are 3, 6 and 16, he at 193. The mover is target
  choice. After the save, R2's game log of that launch loads a fallen's
  swing and flinch (`FARHssdA1hth.dcc`, `FARHssdGHhth.dcc`) and master's
  does not; the only `fallen1` that is a quarry is the deployed squad's
  model, now among the living, so a hunter took it and a clock fight opened
  on it (INFERENCE from the log: the script reads no seek or clock state).
  Act 6i: the quick resolve after 1,800 frames
  slew 4, and the save was refused 80 times (65 on a death still playing),
  where master's was 1,200 frames, 2, and 93 (69). Every assertion held.

# The raid's R2 review fixes, and version 3 (branch `raid-r2`, 1 Oct 2026)

The independent review of R2 (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-rev-r2\review.md`: 0 A,
3 B, 7 C) fixed on `raid-r2` before its merge, by one Claude Code build agent
(Opus 5.5). Logs: `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-r2\fix\logs\`.

| Item | What was wrong | Fix | Test | Control (red, then green restored) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | Strict nearest with no hysteresis: between two near-equal quarries a hunter's watch, and its chase, flipped on every look (0.4-0.8 s real) | A seen target is sticky. The watch keeps its own target while it is in reach and in sight unless the nearest is nearer by `SwitchMarginTiles` (1.5, Pursuit's `RepathTiles`), and for `DwellMinutes` (2.0, Pursuit's `MinRepathMinutes`) after a switch it keeps the new one while it is seen. A target out of sight, out of reach, hidden, protected or dead is no reason to wait. New reason `held`, new total `holds`, row field `dwell_minutes`; dials settable (`switch_margin_tiles`, `dwell_minutes`; both 0 is R2's strict nearest) | `TestSeekHoldsASeenTargetAgainstANearEqualOne` (the review's flicker probe: 0 retargets in 10 looks; control: dials at 0, 10 of 10), `TestSeekSwitchesOnlyForAQuarryNearerByTheMargin`, `TestSeekDwellsOnANewTarget`, `TestSeekDoesNotHoldATargetItCannotSee` | `sticky-off` (4 tests red), `dwell-never-set`, `dwell-never-ends`, `held-unseen` |
| **B2** | Seek's `route_solves` was a literal 0 while each retarget costs the chase one A* in the same frame; the benchmark's quarries never moved | Worded: "Seek solves no route; each retarget costs one pursuit solve" (seek.go, game.go, harness.md). Counted: Pursuit's `rechases` (`rechase_solves` in its provider and the world file's pursuit block). Measured: `BenchmarkSeekRechaseMovingQuarries` (below). The first solve is NOT deferred (see the decisions) | `TestPursuitCountsTheRechaseSolves`; the pursuit sweep's two new rows (`rechase_solves` moved: diverges; past `solves`: refused) | `rechase-uncounted`, `rechase-unrestored` |
| **B3** | The dead-body gate had no test | -- | `TestSeekPassesOverTheDead` (a villager at body 0, 3 tiles nearer: the wolf keeps him; control: alive, she takes it) | `deadgate-off` |
| **C1** | Phases were given round-robin by creation count, so two groups made 23 frames apart bunched two looks in one frame | Each new row takes the least-loaded phase of the rows already looking, counted on the absolute frames they fall due (`phaseLoad`, `leastLoaded`) | `TestSeekStaggersGroupsMadeOnDifferentFrames` (the review's probe: max 1 a frame, was 2) | `roundrobin-on` |
| **C2** | "Current first among equals" untested | -- | `TestSeekBreaksATieForItsOwnTarget` (dials at 0, so the tie-break alone decides) | `tie-off` |
| **C3** | `combat_clock_snapshot.go` said a chase is never moved | Comment corrected: since R2 a chase follows its watch on the next `startChasesForTheAware` | -- | -- |
| **C4** | R2 amended version 2 with no bump | **Version 3** (below) | `TestTheFileShapeIsPinnedToItsVersion`, `TestAnOlderVersionsFileIsRefusedAndSetAside` | the shape test itself |
| **C5** | Register and harness rows | Done at the master merge (Seek.Snapshot and CheckSnapshot are the player's since B5; harness one above master's) | the reach gate | -- |
| **C6** | `TestHisSleepHidesOnlyHim` accepted no notice row; either of two `SetPlayer` bindings kept it green | The script requires the row. The binding in `applyTalk` is removed: advanceWorld binds him every frame and a sleep is answered in a talk, so frames have always run first. One binding, one control | `TestHisSleepHidesOnlyHim` | `no-frame-binding` (playtest, game.go's binding removed: red) |
| **C7** | Two design questions | Decided on default, below | `TestSpawnsDaybreakKeepsAPackThatSeesAVillager` | `aware-him-only` |

`TestSeekAFighterKeepsItsTarget` sets the margin to 0: its control (m:3 out of
the fight) has the villager only 0.6 nearer, which the margin would now hold,
and the test is about the fighter's rule.

**Decided on default 1 Oct 2026 (the review's C7). Josh can overturn either.**
- **(a) His own squad's deployed model IS a quarry.** Ruling 3 of 28 Sep: the
  dead hunt the nearest living, one rule for all; the models are real entities
  since 11 Sep. `Game.seekQuarries` already names them; nothing changed.
- **(b) Daybreak does NOT send home a pack that sees any living quarry** -- a
  squad model or a villager as much as him -- the same rule that already kept
  a pack that noticed him (`Spawns.aware` reads any noticed watch). Pinned by
  `TestSpawnsDaybreakKeepsAPackThatSeesAVillager`.
- **B2: the re-chase's first solve stays in the frame Seek retargets.**
  Deferring it to `Pursuit.Advance` moves the same A* one frame later; the
  real fix for a solve's cost is a per-frame solve budget in Pursuit, which
  every chase start and re-path would share (they cost the same and predate
  R2). Left for its own burst.

**The re-chase, measured** (`BenchmarkSeekRechaseMovingQuarries`, the real
village, stationary hunters, quarries walking 3 tiles back and forth; median
of 3 at 2400 frames; `fix\logs\bench1.txt`):

| N x M | Variant | Retargets / world min | Rechase solves / min | One rechase solve, mean | Worst | Most in one frame |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 24 x 30 | sticky (shipped) | 7.4 | 7.4 | 2.5 ms | 13.4 ms | 1 |
| 24 x 30 | strict (R2 as built) | 9.3 | 9.3 | 3.0 ms | 20.4 ms | 1 |
| 48 x 60 | sticky | 17.2 | 17.2 | 2.2 ms | 25.7 ms | 2 |
| 48 x 60 | strict | 22.8 | 22.8 | 1.5 ms | 14.1 ms | 2 |

Stickiness cuts retargets by a fifth to a quarter when the quarries truly
move (and to zero in the near-equal flicker). **A rechase solve is over the
2 ms frame budget on its own** (mean 1.5-3.0 ms, worst 13-26 ms when the
bounded search runs out), the same cost as any chase start or re-path; the
stagger keeps it to ceil(N/24) a NIGHT frame only -- a long step looks every
row in one frame (the second review's B2, below: 8 in one frame, worst solve
42 ms, worst frame 48 ms). The notice-and-Seek frame, unchanged
in kind: staggered worst 30 / 82 / 290 us at 24x30 / 48x60 / 96x120 (all-due
0.17 / 0.73 / 3.2 ms).

**Version 3** (Josh's ruling of 30 Sep: every shape change bumps). On this
branch before the merge: `b3ShapeHashes[2]` back to R1's `fa82243b...` and
`world-v2.json` back to the base's, `[3]` the new shape. The master merge
keeps master's `[2]` (`be2baeb5...`, save-held's `action_at`) and
`world-v2.json` untouched and regenerates `[3]` and `world-v3.json` once on
the merged tree. Version 3 over version 2: `notice.watches[].side`, the `seek`
block (rows with `dwell_minutes`, `holds`), and `pursuit.rechase_solves`. **A
version-2 file is refused on its version and set aside as `.v2.unread`**; he
begins at dawn with his hero, kit and progress. There is no migration.

| After T | Retargets | Holds | His fights | Clock fights (on the deployed model) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R2's strict nearest (dials at 0) | 13 | 0 | a third, 6 rounds, 16 blows; he at 193 | 1, routed by the model |
| Sticky (shipped) | 11 | 31 | none | 2 (3 joined), 13 rounds, routed by the model |

The pack that turns to his squad's model, two tiles from him, now stays on it
and is routed by it; under the strict nearest some hunters flickered back to
him, which is what gave his fight its blows. That is the review's B1 working
as built, with the model a quarry (C7 (a)). **Act 3 now asks for blows and
rounds in his fight and the clock fights together** (no quick resolve in
either): a clock fight after T is fought blow by blow on the combat-clock
stream and is in the digest act 6 compares, which is what BUG-77's assertion

The second independent review (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-rev-r2b\`, at
`8e747573`), fixed on `raid-r2` by the same build agent. Logs:
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-r2\fix\logs\`. Each new assertion has a source
control, red with the mutation and green restored byte for byte
(`ctl-<name>.txt`).

| Item | What was wrong | Fix | Test | Control |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1** | The first fixes' act 3 summed his fight's blows with the clock fights', so a resume that put HIS combat stream one draw off passed (the review's control p2) | His squad's model is deployed across the village (`squadModelAt`), so under the shipped sticky dials the pack comes for HIM; act 3 again requires his own fight to land rounds and blows after T, none quick-resolved, as its own check; the clock fights after T are measured on their own line and, when any started, must land blows and rounds. The clock `quick_resolved` clause is dropped: a clock fight does not quick-resolve at `quickResolveOff` or at 0 (measured), so it could not fire | `TestSaveResume` act 3 | the review's p2 overlay (`pt-ctl-p2.txt`) |
| **B1** | A watch whose own target died stayed aware: daybreak kept its pack, and a chase could start on the corpse | `Notice.gone` (wired by `NewCombat` to its `deadByBody`): a watch whose target is dead by its body is no aware pair (`AwarePairs`, `Aware`) and no reason for daybreak to keep its pack (`Spawns.aware` reads `awareOfTheLiving`). The watch itself, its memory and counters are untouched (no shape change); a dead clock quarry's watches are still released where the death is handled | `TestSpawnsDaybreakSendsHomeAPackWhoseVillagerDied` (control: alive, kept), `TestAWatchOnTheDeadIsNoAwarePair` | `aware-reads-noticed`, `pairs-ignore-gone`, `gone-unwired` |

A first chase and a script's `strigoi_pursue` (`Chase`) are never capped | `TestRechaseIsCappedAFrame` (the review's long-step probe: 1 in the long frame, the other 7 one a frame; control: the dial at 0, all 8) | `rechase-uncapped`, `budget-never-reset` |
| **C1** | A watch's own target dead or protected: no test held the gate on the watch's own target | -- | `TestSeekLeavesAnOwnTargetThatIsGone` (the reviewer's probe and its protected twin) | `current-skips-dead`, `current-skips-protected` |

| **C3** | Stale comments | seek.go (the stagger caps night-length steps only; Seek solves no route, the re-chase is capped in Pursuit), game.go (`startChasesForTheAware`), these notes | -- | -- |
