**A1, measured** (`pt-a1-1.txt`, and green again in `pt-targeted-2.txt`): with
the model at (14.5, 33.5) the pack comes for him -- 5 retargets, 0 holds after
T; his fight 7 rounds and 27 blows after T (he at 185 at dawn); one clock
fight still opened on the model (6 rounds, 18 blows). **The review's p2
control is now RED** (`pt-ctl-p2.txt`): his combat stream restored one draw
off resumes S_T exactly at act 4 and diverges at act 6 on his fight's first
roll after T (`combat.actions`, roll 74 saved, 86 resumed).

**Decided on default, 1 Oct 2026 (Josh can overturn):** A1 moves the model
rather than putting strict dials in `huntedDials` (the shipped dials stay
under test); B1 makes a corpse's watch not AWARE rather than releasing it in
Seek (the release belongs where deaths are handled, and R1 already does it
for a clock fight's quarry); B2 caps re-chases at 1 a frame rather than
deferring them to Pursuit's re-path cadence (a deferred re-chase is asked
again on the very next frame, so a pack follows its watches within N frames
and no saved state is added).

**The rechase in the frame, honestly.** On the moving-quarry benchmark at
48x60 the busiest frame held 2 re-chases before the cap and the worst single
solve was 42 ms (worst frame 48 ms, p99 13-14 ms, mean frame 2.6-2.9 ms;
the reviewer's `bench.txt`). With the cap (`post2.txt`, median of 3): never
more than 1 re-chase in a frame at either size; one re-chase solve mean
1.5-2.6 ms, worst 12.7-16.7 ms; the frame mean 1.9-2.5 ms, p99 10.5-12.9 ms,
worst 14-37 ms -- the worst frames are now re-path and chase-start solves,
which the cap does not touch. At 48x60 strict the cap moved 1.6 rechases a
minute onto later frames (22.8 -> 21.2 a minute in the window). A* on this map is
over the 2 ms frame budget by itself whatever the cap: the cap bounds how
many land in one frame, not what one costs.

# Fog of war F3, "kept": the fog block, and version 4 (branch `fog-f3`, 1 Oct 2026)

Fog of war's third burst (`claude/fog-of-war-build-plan.md` §4 F3, §3.7):
the explored grid is saved, so the ground he has seen is still remembered
after a load. Built on master `0ea27076` (fog F1 and F2 merged, the world file
at version 3 from the raid's R2).

## Measured first

- **His world files** (1 Oct 2026, read only): `%APPDATA%\OpenDiablo2\Saves`
  holds 8,059 files -- 4,623 `.od2` heroes (harness and test heroes among
  them), 3,435 `.od2.strigoi.json` sidecars, one `.strigoi.json.tmp` -- and
  **no `.world.json` of any version, and no `.unread`**. So **none of his
  files is set aside by this bump** (0 of 0). The harness's games save in
  their own `%APPDATA%`, never his.
- **What R2 merged:** all of it -- version 3 is R2's, on master.

## The block

`World.Fog` (`d2world.FogSnapshot`, `d2core/d2world/fog_snapshot.go`), the last
top-level block (`Blocks`: ... `"scene", "fog"`):

```json
"fog": { "map": "<the map's SHA-256>", "w": 48, "h": 48, "explored": "<base64>" }
```

- **`explored`** is the grid as bits, row-major (tile (x, y) at bit y*w+x),
  eight tiles a byte, lowest bit first, the bits past w*h zero; standard padded
  base64 on one line. The village is 288 bytes, 384 characters.
- **`map`** is the map it was explored on: the authored map's SHA-256, the
  same value as the file's `map.sha` (`""` for Diablo II's generated Act 1,
  whose identity is the seed). **The F1 review's C6:** the grid is keyed on
  the map, not its size. The file's own check (`World.checkFog`, reason
  `fog`) refuses a grid whose map is not the file's; the load's step 4
  (`Fog.Validate`, through `Game.fogValidate`) refuses one that is not the
  game's map or size, BLOCK -- D5's rule: a grid of another map is refused
  and the file set aside, never laid over this map. (In practice the file's
  map is refused first, MAP, when the map changed; the fog's own check is the
  backstop that makes the grid self-describing.)
- **Empty** (`"map": "", "w": 0, "h": 0, "explored": ""`) is a fog that has
  never looked.
- **Not saved:** what he sees now (`visible`, derived: the first frame's
  update recomputes it from his eyes, and `Restore` marks the fog to); the
  dials (`day_sight`, the dark radii, the look -- this build's tuning, D2);
  the counters and the probe (this process's); and whether fog is drawn
  (`-fog`, the view, as `ui.zoom` is).

`FogSnapshot.Check` refuses anything `Snapshot` could not have written: a
negative size, one side zero, a side past 4,096 tiles, an empty grid naming a
map or holding bits, a grid not exactly `ceil(w*h/8)` bytes, bits past the
last tile, and any spelling but the encoder's (the decoder would skip a line
break; the check re-encodes and compares).

## Decided (the recommended option; Josh can overturn)

- **F3-1. Version 4, no migration.** Every shape change bumps (Josh, 30
  Sep). A version-3 file is refused on its version and set aside as
  `.v3.unread`; he begins at dawn with his hero, kit and progress (the
  sidecar), as at the last bump. Measured: no file of his is affected.
  Versions 1-3 keep their golden files and hashes byte for byte; version 4's
  is `af2b191baa0f5816e1eaa3f7604d55e3ff9668bc6c8c4a54642689fefc0aa11f`
  (`testdata/world-v4.json`).
- **F3-2. The grid is saved whenever a Fog exists -- always, in the game --
  and is empty when fog never looked.** Not "only when fog is enabled":
  `enabled` is the view (`-fog`), as `ui.zoom` is, and the explored grid is
  the world's. A game with fog off never updates the fog, so it saves the
  empty block; a grid loaded into a game with fog off is kept, undrawn, and
  saved again unchanged -- so launching once without `-fog` never throws away
  the ground he had explored. (`TestAGameWithFogOffSavesAnEmptyGrid`.)
- **F3-3. The map key is the SHA**, not the path: the same bytes under another
  name are the same map; an edited map is another (D5).
- **F3-4. The load refuses a fog block that does not fit as BLOCK** (with
  "fog:"), as every other system's Validate does -- no new load code. The
  file's own refusal has its new reason, `fog`, after the scene's.
- **F3-5. The explored grid is in the fog provider's digest WORLD part** (`map`,
  `w`, `h`, `explored`, `grid` -- the base64), so the resume digest compares
  it; everything else of fog stays in the process part. Consequence:
  `TestFogNeverTouchesTheSim` exempts fog's own hash and the `systems` part
  (which hashes every system's world state together) -- with fog off nothing
  is explored, so they differ by design -- and still compares every other
  system hash for hash; it gained a control that fog's own world state DOES
  differ.

## The load order

Step 4 checks the fog block (after the scene's stage); step 5 restores it
after the scene, before he is stood at `hero.pos` (`loadSteps`: ... `"bodies",
"scene", "fog", "hero", "world_rng"`).

## Tests and controls

Logs in `strigoi-harness-runs\wt-fog3\`; negative controls in `nc\`.

- d2world (`fog_snapshot_test.go`): `TestFogSnapshotFieldsClassified` (every
  Fog field S/D/W), `TestFogSnapshotRoundTrips`, `TestFogSnapshotOfANewGame`,
  `TestFogRefusesAGridOfTheWrongSize` (size, map, malformed; a refused
  Restore changes nothing), `TestFogSnapshotEveryFieldIsLoadBearing` (the
  B-series sweep and probes), `TestFogDialsAreNotSaved`.
- d2save: the shape pin at version 4; `TestCheckRefusesWhatNoLoadCouldRestore`
  gains fourteen fog cases (reason `fog`); `TestAFieldTheFileLacksIsRefused`
  gains `fog.map`, `fog.explored`, `fog.w`; `TestAnEmptyFogBlockIsAFile`;
  `TestAGeneratedWorldIsAMap` keys its grid on no SHA.
- d2gamescreen: `TestAFoggedGameIsKept` (save, load into a new game, the same
  grid, nothing visible until the first update), `TestAGameWithFogOffSavesAnEmptyGrid`,
  two fog cases in `TestARefusedLoadRestoresNothing`'s table (another
  size; this size on another map: BLOCK), the load-order lists, the field
  classification (`fog: S:fog`), and the fog digest test (the grid in the
  world part and nowhere else).
- Playtest: `TestFogIsKept` (`fog_test.go`; below), `TestSaveResumeNegatives`
  gains "omit fog", `worldBlocks` gains "fog".

### The F3 review's fixes (1 Oct 2026)

- **A1, a night save resumed to other ground.** Fog keyed his carried torch
  by its tile but lit from his exact point, so the game that ran on kept the
  lit set computed where he ENTERED the tile, and a resume recomputed it from
  where he STANDS (101 vs 110 tiles in the reviewer's unit probe; 331 vs 332
  in his night playtest). Fixed in fog's own rule, independent of where the
  light model or the renderer put the torch: fog sees by a carried source
  from the CENTRE OF HIS TILE, the disc it iterates and the "lit" it asks
  (`FogLight.LitCarriedAt`) alike (docs/fog.md). `TestAMidTileTorchSaveResumesToTheSameGrid`
  (a resume at every frame of a torch-lit walk) and `TestFogIsKept`'s acts
  7-8 (23:00, his kit's torch, saved at the far edge of a tile).
- **B1, the encoding pinned:** `TestTheGridsLayoutIsPinned` (a revealed 5 x 3
  grid is `"/38="`; `"UUA="` is exactly tiles (0,0), (4,0), (1,1), (4,2)).
- **C1, the save's fog check is covered, not dropped:** the live game's grid
  is always of its own map, so only a probe reaches it -- as for every other
  block (B2/R8): `TestTheSaveRunsTheLoadsOwnChecks` gains a `fog` probe (a
  well-formed grid of 39 x 40 keyed on the file's map).
- **C3:** `-fog` / `fog.enabled` changes the world part of the digest (a
  fog-off game never explores), so resuming a save with a different `-fog`
  gives S_R != S_U by design (docs/fog.md).


# B6: the omit sweep, and the milestone's closeout (branch `save-b6`, 2 Oct 2026)

Master `d65e3a1c` (world file version 4). Josh's brief for B6 (the
coordinator's): "the omit sweep (`TestSaveResumeNegatives` over every block,
into the suite), plus whatever the plan's section 5 gives B6". Section 5's
row: "`TestSaveResumeNegatives`, docs, bugs -- every block diverges when
dropped. Closes J1 review B6."

## What the sweep is

`omitSweep` (`playtest/save_resume_negatives_test.go`) is **`TestSaveResume`'s
act 9**, run last, on the evening acts 1-3 built and in the session acts 4-6i
used. The control of the controls first (T's untouched file resumes S_T), then
every row: T's file edited, put beside his act-3 files (`negLoad`, which also
clears a `.bak` and a `.preload` an earlier save left in the home), started
with no seed, the evening's dials re-applied, the digest taken, and the row
judged (`judgeRow`). Every row is run and every failure reported (`t.Errorf`),
so one run shows the whole table.

- **Rows from `d2save.Blocks`.** `omitRows` makes one OMITTED row per block
  from the list itself (the key deleted, as `strigoi_save_game{omit}` writes
  it): each must be refused `FILE` with the rule `block-missing` (`version`'s
  is `VERSION`, rule `version`: Decode reads the version first).
  `emptyRows` is the hand-written EMPTIED half: each block's content taken
  out. `TestTheOmitSweepCoversEveryBlock` (no game) fails while any block of
  `d2save.Blocks` lacks an emptied row, has other than one omitted row, or a
  row names a block the file does not have -- so a block added to the file
  cannot be forgotten.
- **Each row says what it wants**, and the judge holds it to that:
  `wantDiverge` -- resumed, S_R0 != S_T, and the divergence includes one of
  the row's `moves` (the part or system the block restores; "diverged, but
  not where the block lives" is red); `wantRefused` -- the load report's code
  AND d2save's rule (a FILE refusal is any of thirty rules, so the code alone
  would take a refusal for the wrong reason: the B3 review's A2, one level
  up), and a fall back that is not the saved moment; `wantSame` -- the one
  block tolerated by design (below), which must resume S_T exactly, so a load
  that starts to read it is red and the row is re-decided.
- **Why the emptied half has teeth.** The emptied file differs from T's in
  that block alone, and a load is deterministic: a load that never read the
  block would resume the emptied file exactly as the untouched one -- S_T,
  which the control has just shown. So a divergence proves the load READS the
  block, and the `moves` that it reads it into the right system. (It does not
  prove the block is restored CORRECTLY: that is acts 4-6.) The omitted half
  pins Decode's rule and the refusal path (code, rule, set aside, dawn): green
  by design for the load (BUG-83), as before.
- **Fast and parallel-safe.** The evening is built once per suite run (acts
  1-3, which `TestSaveResume` builds anyway); act 9 loads one game at a time
  in its own session and home, so the suite's `-parallel` changes nothing.
  Each load is about 1.0-1.3 s (a game start with the harness's 90 s wait,
  `negLoad`); the 52 rows and the control take **59-65 s**, and
  `TestSaveResume` went from about 167 s (`wt-hfix\pt-set-3.txt`) to 229 s.
- **`TestSaveResumeNegatives`** is now the same sweep against a KEPT evening
  (`STRIGOI_SAVE_RESUME_FROM`), skipped without one, for controls: about 65 s
  with no acts 1-8.

## The rows, as measured (`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-b6\pt-saveresume-1.txt`)

Omitted, all 23: refused `FILE` `block-missing` naming the block (version:
`VERSION` `version`), fell back to dawn. Emptied:

| Block | Row | Seen (and wanted) |
|---|---|---|
| version | 0 | refused VERSION, rule version |
| build | "" | **tolerated by design**: resumed S_T (World.Build is informational: a load does not refuse another build's file of the same version) |
| saved_at | "" | refused FILE, rule generation (the embedded sidecar's generation is the file's saved_at) |
| map | path, sha "" | refused FILE, rule map ("an authored world names its .tmj") |
| seed | "0" | refused FILE, rule rng-stream (the world stream saved on seed 99) |
| rng | world draws 0 | diverged: part rng, system scene |
| rng | uuid bytes 0 | diverged: system uuid |
| hero | facing 0, stamina 0, run off | diverged: part entities |
| sidecar | kit.pack [] | **diverged: system kit -- since BUG-113's fix; before it, A HOLE** |
| sidecar | progress xp 0, talents [] | diverged: system progress |
| sidecar | village rep 0, flags [] | diverged: system village (and meters, ui) |
| sidecar | land gathered 0 | diverged: system village (land_gathered) |
| sidecar | journal written {} | diverged: system journal |
| clock | elapsed 0 | diverged: system clock (and eleven more) |
| light | sources [] | diverged: system light (and kit: the torch's minutes are no longer zeroed, D1) |
| squads | the deployed squad removed | diverged: system meters |
| spawns | groups [] | diverged: system spawns, seek |
| spawner | arrival 0 | diverged: system scene |
| notice | watches [] | diverged: system spawns |
| pursuit | chases [] | diverged: system pursuit |
| seek | rows [] | diverged: system seek |
| corpses | bodies [], risen_as, walker, last {} | diverged: system corpses |
| rising | pressure_accrued and every count 0 | diverged: system rising |
| combat | every count 0, next_id 1 | diverged: system combat (measured first: counts zeroed under next_id 3 are refused BLOCK -- "next_id is always one past") |
| bodies | [] | diverged: system combat, scene |
| entities | the non-native removed | refused FILE, rule body-orphan |
| entities | two entities' ids swapped | refused FILE, rule member-place |
| scene | watch_stood 0, field_dead [] | diverged: system scene, village |
| fog | **filled** (every tile of the file's map explored) | diverged: system fog -- the hunted evening is fog-off, its grid empty, so an emptied block would be T's file; emptying a grid he had is `TestFogIsKept` act 5 |

The sidecar is one block of five documents, each bound by its own code
(`bindKit`, `bindProgress`, `bindStanding`, `bindLand`, `bindJournal`), so each
has its row.

## BUG-113: the digest held no kit (found by the sweep; fixed)

The first run (`wt-b6\pt-dev-1.txt`, against the evening master's suite kept,
restamped for the run) found one hole: **the emptied pack resumed the saved
moment**. No provider reported the kit: the `ui` provider's `kit_rows` are the
panel's rows, built only while the panel is open (`[]` at T), and
`kit_torch_left` is the torch alone. So every resume comparison since B4a would
have passed a load that bound the wrong kit. **Fixed:** a `kit` provider
(`kitProvider`, `d2gamescreen/kit.go`), registered when the kit binds and
unregistered with the screen -- `loadout`, `worn`, `pack` as the sidecar writes
them, `bound`, `choosing_loadout` -- all in the digest's WORLD part. With it,
`TestSaveResume` acts 4-6i are green unchanged: the resumed kit is the saved
one. The digest's shape moved, so 0.16.4's evenings are refused as stale (the
stamp, BUG-93).

## The load report's `rule` (harness 0.16.5)

`LoadRefusal.Rule` and `LoadReport.Rule` (`json:"rule"`): `d2save.ReasonOf`
of the error behind a refusal made from d2save's own -- Decode and Check
(`VERSION`, `FILE`) and the pairing (`HERO`, `TORN`) -- through
`refuseLoadErr`; "" for every other (`BLOCK`, `MAP`, `NETWORK`, a file that
cannot be read). Set on the report by `SetLoadAside`, the ignored-file path and
the torn-with-no-bak path. It is the load report's (this process's), never the
digest's.

## J1 review B6 (the clock not saved) -- closed for a resume; BUG-114 for the rest

The plan's section 5 has B6 close the J1 review's B6: "the clock is not saved,
so a reload reopens on 17 June while the journal may already hold later
pages". **For a resumed save it is closed**: the world file carries the clock
(B2a/B4a), `TestSaveResume` act 4 compares the clock and the journal (S_R0 =
S_T), and act 9's emptied clock diverges in both. **It is not closed where a
game begins without the world file** -- rule 7's fall back, rule 9's network
game, a hero with no world file: a new clock starts at elapsed 0. Measured in
act 9 (logged once per sweep, not asserted): T's file refused, he fell back to
"1462-06-17 02:45" with journal pages `[1462-06-17 1462-06-18 1462-06-19]`.
Filed as **BUG-114**, for Josh to rule which day a fall back wakes on.

## Decisions (the recommended option each time; Josh can overturn any)

- **B6-1 -- the sweep is act 9 of `TestSaveResume`**, not a script of its own:
  the evening is built once per suite run (its own acts 1-3 would add about two
  minutes and a second hunted night to every suite), and one session loads one
  game at a time, so `-parallel` is moot. `TestSaveResumeNegatives` stays, as
  the kept-evening entry for controls.
- **B6-2 -- `build` is tolerated, by design**, not made load-bearing: it is
  informational (B3), and a load that refused another build's file of the same
  version would set aside every save across a rebuild. Its omission is still
  refused. Its row holds it: should a load start to read it, act 9 is red.
- **B6-3 -- `rule` on the load report (harness 0.16.5)**, so a refusal is
  judged by its rule, not by matching words in its reason.
- **B6-4 -- the sidecar's five documents each get a row**, and the fog block
  is FILLED on the fog-off evening rather than emptied (TestFogIsKept act 5
  empties a fogged day's).
- **B6-5 -- BUG-114 is not fixed here**: which day a fall back wakes on is a
  ruling and a change to the sidecar's shape.
- **B6-6 -- `negLoad` clears a `.bak` and a `.preload`** before each row:
  in `TestSaveResume`'s home act 5 has saved again, and a row refused TORN
  would resume the `.bak` (T's own file, B5 review A2) and read as a hole. No
  row today is refused TORN (saved_at's is refused by Check first, rule
  generation), so this is defensive and has no control.

- No independent reviewer: this agent cannot spawn one.

# B6 review fixes (branch `save-b6`, 2 Oct 2026)

An independent review of `02d6ba24` (`strigoi-wt-rev-b6`, logs
`strigoi-harness-runs\wt-rev-b6\`): the mechanics hold, but **act 9 proves each
BLOCK is read, not that each FIELD is restored**. Its probe changed every leaf
of T's file in turn -- 169 diverged, 41 refused, **65 SAME** -- and five of its
eight source mutants survived act 9: M2 (the fog grid laid out reversed), M3
(fog keyed to any map), M4 (a squad's morale restored as 100), M7 (scene
`last_stage` and the watch clock not restored), M8 (the blow log dropped).

## A1 -- the fields the digest could not see (BUG-117)

Each SAME classed, by reading the code first (unobserved, or ignored on bind):

- **Unobserved: restored, never reported.** The combat block's `blow_log`
  (`Combat.Restore` rebuilds it; no provider reported it). The journal's
  written order (each entry's sequence number), page numbers, rungs, each
  task's number and edge memory, and `seen` (`Journal.Restore` reads every map;
  the provider reported which ids were written and the tasks' states only).
  **Neither is ignored on bind** (the review's M6, the journal not bound on a
  load, was red at the control). **Fixed:** the combat provider reports
  `blow_log`; the journal provider `written_seq`, `page_seq`, `rungs`,
  `task_rows` (state, seq, mem) and `seen` -- in the digest's world part.
- **Spent by the first frame: restored and reported, sampled again at once.**
  Scene `last_stage` (progress sets it to the stage every frame), `watch_clock`
  and `watch_clock_set` (keepWatch sets them every frame). At T the clock
  agrees with all three, so a load that dropped them (M7) resumed T. Not a
  restore bug: they decide something only at a moment where they disagree
  with the clock, so act 9 now builds those moments (below). The journal's
  rungs and task memory are spent the same way by its first Evaluate.
- **By design** (the field sweep's allow-list, each with its reason): the
  carried torch's x,y (the light model sets it from where he stands, BUG-110);
  a lit torch's `burn_left` (D1); a velocity at a standstill; a squad's
  `meters.activity` (the stance follows the post).

**The perturbed rows** (`perturbRows`): values T's evening does not have,
where a load that defaulted the field would resume T -- s:2's morale 37
(kills M4), s:2's man wounded to 23, rising stood again 3 / wandered 2, the
watch clock 4 minutes behind (credited at the post), a logged blow's damage
(kills M8 with the report), two journal entries' order swapped, a pack
member's motion (target, dir, speed; the review's B2). **Twin rows** (the
judge compares the row's resume with its TWIN's, a file that differs in the
one field, instead of S_T): `watch_clock_set` true vs false with the watch
clock behind, and `last_stage` night vs dawn with the clock at the unpaid
dawn after T (03:15, day index 2) -- the night is paid in one and not the
other. Together they kill M7, and each half of it (M7a: `last_stage` alone).

## B1 -- the fog row

It fills an UNEVEN grid (a 6 x 6 corner block and every 37th tile) and the
row's `check` asserts the resumed game's grid is the file's bit for bit (the
fog provider's `grid`). An all-explored grid is its own mirror, so M2 passed.
**M3 (fog keyed to any map) is not reachable from a file**: Check refuses a
fog grid of another map than the file's (`fog`), and a file of another map
than the game's is refused MAP (D5) before the fog block is read -- an
equivalent mutant at the load level, left.

## B2, B3, B4, C1

- B2: the pack member's motion row (above), `wantDiverge`, part entities.
- B3: `TestTheKitProviderReportsWhatHeCarries` holds the provider to the
  sidecar's own JSON of the kit (`d2items.HeroBytes`) field for field, and
  changes make, condition, points, a pack count and the torch's minutes one
  at a time (UK1, UK2: the report stripped of `worn`, of `make`: red).
- B4: act 9's PASS line names its slowest row and its time (a twin row,
  2.5 s: two loads).
- C1: "the system the block restores" is now "where the block shows: the
  part or system of the digest that reports it".

## The field sweep, adopted: `TestSaveResumeFieldSweep`

`playtest/save_resume_fieldsweep_test.go`, the reviewer's probe as an opt-in
companion to `TestSaveResumeNegatives` (`STRIGOI_SAVE_RESUME_FROM`;
`FIELD_SWEEP=<prefix>` narrows it): every leaf perturbed in turn, classed
REFUSED, DIVERGED or SAME, and **every SAME red unless it is on
`fieldSameByDesign`** -- the four by-design fields above, the scene's two
spent fields (their teeth are act 9's twins), and the journal's rungs and
task memory (spent by the first Evaluate). `scene.watch_clock` is perturbed
BEHIND (ahead, the first frame credits nothing either way).

## BUG-114 -- fixed on Josh's default (a)

A game that resumes no world save wakes at **dawn (02:45) of the day after
the last day his sidecar knows** -- his journal's `last_date`, already in
the sidecar (no change to its shape) -- never earlier than the clock stands;
a hero with no dated page wakes on the epoch as before
(`d2gamescreen/wake.go`, called in `bindKit` before `bindProgress` samples
the clock, so the dawn is not paid as a night survived). Act 9 asserts it on
its first refusal ("fallen back to dawn of 1462-06-20 02:45, the day after
the last his journal knows"), and `refusedToDawn` (acts 6c-6g) the day after
his sidecar's last page. **For Josh to overturn:** (b) leave 17 June; (c) a
day count in the sidecar (a shape change).
